## Summary

The Attention System is the simulation of the web's regard for the ship: one global, integer-only level that rises with the light the crew burns, the noise they make, the power they draw and the depth they work at, and falls back toward a depth-set baseline when they show restraint. Crossing one threshold starts a seeded, escalating 15 to 30 second **tell** (sound in a structure-borne bed, filaments resolving at the lamp cone's edge, never a meter) that restraint can abort; if it is not aborted, a **breach** puts STRESS on the section where the crew was loudest and on everything welded to it, sweeps the home-ship's single beacon, and ends in a short silence. It owns the tell-window contract: no web-originated harm lands outside a told breach, which is how Pillars 2 and 3 become testable.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed), Life-Support Graph (designed), Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed), Player Controller & Input (designed), Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed), Suit & Equipment (designed; soft, none at MVP)`; neighbours designed after this GDD (rows refreshed in the 2026-10-27 consistency pass where asked): `Ship Body & Interior Physics, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Adaptive Audio & Tells (Designed, adaptive-audio-and-tells.md)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Modular Ship Assembly, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table, Debug Overlay and Derelict Authoring are Designed too; a matching status does not mean matching contracts, see OQ-AT-28 for the contract-diff outcome)*

## Overview

The Attention System is the web's regard of the ship, as simulation. In one sentence: it is a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `attention.state`, advanced by one divisor-6 step hook, `attention.step`, that holds **one global attention level** which rises with the light the crew keeps burning, the noise they make, the power they draw and the depth they work at, falls back toward a depth-set baseline when they show restraint, and drives a four-phase breach machine, **CALM → TELL → BREACH → SUBSIDING**, whose second step, the tell, is a promise to the player: a window of 10 to 30 seconds, seeded and fixed at the moment the level crosses the threshold, in which the world escalates for as long as the crew stays loud and the player can still choose to be quiet *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

**The data layer.** Stimuli reach the level by two paths and no others. *Pushed noise* arrives through one Class B mutator, `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)`, from the Player Controller (gait classes, and one per-tick carry-burden class *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), Interaction (tool and one-shot classes); Horror Entities never calls it (HE-36); Attention owns the unit table `U[class]`, and one walking tick of noise defines the unit (1 attention unit, AU). *Ordered reads* take light from the Emitter State's per-emitter list and power from the Life-Support Graph's `power_use(section_id)`; the Suit pushes nothing, and Attention never reads Audio. The level is held in milli-AU, integrates the three channels with one memory constant `TAU_ATT_TICKS`, and decays by a ceiling step toward the baseline `B`, so it always reaches `B` exactly. One threshold `ATT_THRESHOLD` (theta) with a hold band (theta_HOLD) starts and ends events. The machine's only writes to the world are two: M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, ...)` at `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`, and the `set_beacon` command. Everything else it does is **publish** `attention.view`, a SIM read view of integers that Lighting, Audio, Horror, Sanity, Save/Load and its own presenter read.

**The player-facing effect.** The player never sees the level. They meet this system as the hull creaking, from everywhere and nowhere, and creaking again, a little sooner each time; as filaments at the very edge of their lamp's cone that were texture a moment ago and are now strands; as dust in the beam turning as if the air had been displaced. That is the **tell**, and its length is not shown. Inside it the player can do the one thing the web responds to, which is less: stand still and set the cutter and any cargo down (the real levers), and, helping a little, kill the chest lamp or open the breaker *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the breaker saves about 2 seconds and its inrush on re-closing gives them back, so it is the least of these, F-25 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). If the level falls under the hold band and stays there, the tell ends in nothing and the creaking stops (an abort). If it does not, the tell ends in a **breach**: the section where the crew was loudest, and every section welded to it, takes STRESS (worn hardware fails faster than a person can walk), the home-ship's single beacon sweeps red, and a short silence follows when it is over. The player can survive a breach, or leave it by pod; they cannot win it. Camera, controls, lamp and ambient light do not change in any phase.

**The contract this GDD owns.** Every web-originated harm to the ship, and provisionally to a crew body, must land inside a told breach: a tell of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (derived as `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)`) that the player could perceive and could have aborted by restraint *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Audio is the guaranteed channel**: the structure-borne bed reaches every living player whatever is lit. The visual channel exists while an eligible emitter is lit, so a player who is fully dark has the audio channel only (ART:954 accepts this). The levers that abort a tell do not weigh the same: **stopping movement and stopping the tool are the lever; darkness and power-down help a little** (F-25 lists the seconds each saves). The Graph's fairness gate covers *endogenous* losses only (LS-13); for every non-player source it states "must therefore be preceded by that system's own Pillar 2 tell". This GDD is that tell. It is written as a testable predicate, `tell_window_contract`, audited by a `TellAudit` ledger that exists as a test fixture before Horror Entities and Adaptive Audio are designed, and it discharges the Graph's review criterion at LSG:1063.

**What the game loses without it.** The core loop's hinge ("Light, noise, power spikes and depth raise it; it is shown in the world; crossing a line flips the rhythm. Dread is feeling it come." — game concept, Core Loop) would be a flat spawn timer, which is the exact outcome Pillar 3's design test rejects, or a hidden threshold, which Pillar 2's design test rejects. Horror Entities would have no phase to act inside and no permission predicate to honour; Save/Load would have no tell to defer an autosave around; Lighting would have no tier to gutter on; Adaptive Audio would have no beat to sound; Sanity would have no true, unhideable event to measure a lie against. The core hypothesis (the two-rhythm boarding-and-breach loop is tense and absorbing for a 30-minute solo session) cannot be tested without it.

**MVP scope.** One threshold, one tier of lamp tell, one locus rule, one breach content (STRESS plus beacon plus published phase; RUPTURE and GOUGE are Horror's), one noise-only push API, ring-1 baseline plus placeholder rows for rings 2 and 3, and a contract-only suppressant (no effect verb, and the item itself is out of the MVP start kit, user ruling X6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Multiple tiers, per-ring thresholds, the suppressant verb, per-player attribution and rate-coupled tell length are Vertical Slice addenda and are named, not absorbed (AT-30); the per-ring light and power stimulus gain is a pillar-flagged Vertical Slice requirement on Depth Rings & Scarcity (AT-6); phantom tells and false calm are rejected by pillar, permanently (AT-17).

**Index wording reconciled.** The index rationale (IDX Systems Enumeration bullet 17) is honoured with five corrections, each recorded as a refresh owed and never as an edit:

| The index says | This GDD does | Why | Refresh owed |
|---|---|---|---|
| noise and power stimuli "pushed in by Graph, Suit, Interaction, Assembly and Horrors" | **two intake paths**: noise is pushed (Controller, Interaction; Horror pushes none and is not a declared caller, HE-36); light and power are *ordered reads* (`emitters()`, `power_use`); Suit and Assembly push nothing at MVP | the Graph publishes `power_use` as a read (LS-19); Suit declares "No stimuli pushed" (SE-31); Interaction already pushes LOUD at cut completion (IA-33), so an Assembly push would double count | index bullet 17 wording (C6); kernel Interactions row 4 (C7) |
| "flicker as a Lighting modifier" | the lamp effect is a **gutter**, published as a tier | Lighting bans the word for the tell (LO-19, LO-20) | index wording (C10) |
| "Lighting never reads Attention" (IDX soft-cycle note) | Lighting reads only the published tier and timing; Attention reads nothing Lighting produces | LO-20 supersedes it | index soft-cycle note (C12) |
| "baseline set by depth" | `B = B_table[ring]` of the deepest section holding a living player; per-ring values belong to Depth Rings (VS) | one deterministic rule; "loudest player" is undefined | none |
| Suit listed as a Hard dependency | Suit is **soft, none at MVP**: contract text for the suppressant only | Suit pushes nothing and Attention reads nothing of it | index edge Attention → Suit (C14) |

**Reading guide.** Detailed Design holds the thirty-odd named rules `AT-1` to `AT-32` (Core Rules), the state machine, and one Interactions table per neighbour. Numbers in this document are `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless a registry name says otherwise; every locked constant (`TICK_RATE`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S`, `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, `NOISE_PER_TICK`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, `MAX_EMITTERS`, `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`, `AUTOSAVE_*`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `MOD_FLOOR_PM`) is cited by name and never restated with a number of this document's own. Lighting's **`gutter_recipe` bounds** (its variables `G_LO` and `G_HI`, tier 1 at 480 and 960 frames, REG:2187-2188, registry `gutter_recipe` variables) are not registry entries: they are cited as "Lighting's `gutter_recipe` bounds" and take no `referenced_by` from this document (C24).

**Implementation digest** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(one page for story authors; the rule ids carry the detail and where this digest and a rule differ, the rule wins).* (1) *Shape:* one `SIM` block `attention.state` (WORLD, SHARED, schema 1) and one hook `attention.step` at divisor 6, offset 0, `after [graph.propagate.power, emitter.place]`; integer-only; the only RNG draw is the tell draw, `draw_epoch(attention.tell, rng_counter)` through the `RngFacet` (AT-1, AT-28) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*. (2) *Intake:* noise by `push_stimulus(NOISE, source, class)` from the Controller and Interaction only (the Controller also pushes one per-tick carry-burden class, F-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, attributed to a section at push time into `pending_noise_mAU` and `pending_ledger_mAU[s]`; light from `emitters()`; power from `power_use` as `W_P × nl_w` plus `W_S ×` a lamp-excluded five-run delta from Attention's own `nl_hist_w` (AT-3, AT-4, F-1 to F-4). (3) *Level:* `L' = clamp(L + I_run − dec, B, L_CEIL)` with `dec = ceil(6 (L − B) / tau)`, and `dec = 0` on a run that drained a `BURDEN` push (a salvage carry held aboard a non-home body, not `IN_POD`, AT-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*; `B` from the deepest living seat's ring (AT-5, AT-6, F-5, F-8). (4) *Machine:* CALM to TELL (at `L ≥ theta`, past both timers), TELL to BREACH (due run, `L ≥ theta_HOLD`, not `locus_vacated`), BREACH to SUBSIDING to CALM; TELL to CALM on an abort (twenty runs under the hold line, or at the due run under it, or the departure void) (AT-7 to AT-13, AT-22). (5) *Tell:* length drawn once (900 to 1800 ticks, floor 600 on every path) under a key that changes per restore epoch; beats on a hash schedule whose interval and tier follow a behaviour-keyed escalation clock (frozen while `L < theta_HOLD`) with one counter for both senses; nothing published reveals the end (AT-8, AT-9, AT-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (6) *Breach:* STRESS on the locus body in bursts, `set_beacon` at T+1; light and power feed the locus ledger through their own sections and noise dominates it (AT-11, AT-12, AT-14, AT-18, F-6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. (7) *Contract:* `exogenous_permitted(section)` and the `TellAudit` replay are the testable form of Pillars 2 and 3 (AT-15, F-23). (8) *Persisted:* the field list in AT-1; every restore failure is a hard `ATTENTION_RESTORE_*` and nothing is clamped. (9) *Lints that gate data:* F-15 and F-15b abortability (rows `x_dark`, `x_dark_home_lit`, `autonomous_transient`), F-16 dark floor, F-20 and F-21 autosave deferral, IR-1 to IR-3, IR-4 (`carry_decision`, F-26), the DOCK_STATUS budget (F-16), F-24 cue floor and rung width, the theta upper bound (F-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (10) *Not built here:* Horror's behaviours, Audio's sounds, Lighting's gutter, the suppressant verb, the per-ring gain (AT-30). **Provenance codes (key).** `Rn` and `CQ-n` are rulings and checkpoint questions of the 2026-10-26 authoring run, `CP-Cn` and `GD-n` are consistency-pass and game-designer findings, `Cn` rows are refreshes owed (Cross-References), `AF-n` are art flags and `Xn` are additions. The ruling files are not in the repository, so the rule text is authoritative and a code is only a pointer; moving rationale into a separate decision log is recorded in OQ-AT-28 and not done in this pass.

## Player Fantasy

**It noticed.** The Synaptic Sea's web does not hunt you; it *looks*, and what it looks by is what you do. Everything you do that is loud, bright or hungry is something it can see, and the acts do not weigh the same: a running step or a cut costs far more than a lamp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Depth sets how closely it is already watching. Your choices move it from there.

Most of the time you are in the boarding rhythm: still, dusty, deliberate, every door a decision about how loud to be. Then the hull creaks, and creaks again, from everywhere and nowhere. At the edge of your light, filaments that were only texture resolve into strands, and the dust in your beam turns as if something displaced the air. The creaks come closer together for as long as you stay loud, and nothing tells you when they will stop; go still and, once the web's regard has fallen back under its hold line, they stop quickening and hold where they are, because the signal is earned *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. That takes a while: 8 seconds after a full stop in the dark, about 12 with the lamp on and the home ship lit, and until then the creaks keep tightening, so a player who stops with the lamp on usually hears the tell reach its second tier before it holds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the lag stated honestly, F-13, AC-AT-135)*. Your lamp, your hands and your view do not change. The ship does, and the web is leaning in. This is the tell: ten to thirty seconds that are yours.

In that window, restraint is a real move. Stand still, set down the cutter and whatever you are hauling, and the web can lose interest; kill the lamp and open the breaker and it loses interest a little sooner *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The creaking stops and the strands sink back into texture. Or you decide the scrubber is worth it, finish the cut, and run for the pod while the section you stressed fails faster than you can walk. Leave, and the web lets go: the red beacon that swept the home-ship while the breach was on goes dark, and the wreck behind you goes on failing in silence. You did not win. You chose, and you can say afterwards what you heard and what you did about it.

The tell never lies. Your sanity can show you a monster in the dark. It cannot fake the creak that comes before the web turns on the ship or on you, and it cannot hide it. When harm arrives without that warning, the first thing to doubt is your eyes. *(Crew-body scope provisional: Horror Entities must repeat AT-15 or surface a CONFLICT.)*

**The emotion is being noticed, not being attacked.** The art bible names it: "It noticed. The small-animal feeling of being looked at. Not yet fear — attention." (ART 2.7). This system owns the gap between *noticed* and *fear*, and makes that gap the player's to spend. The power fantasy is the night-shift engineer's or the cave diver's: *I know what makes me loud, and I can choose to be quiet.* The player has no power over the web; their power is over their own emission. They never beat it. They go unnoticed, or they get through being noticed, or they leave.

**Framing choice (orchestrator ruling, logged).** Three framings were weighed. F1, *the web watches the ship* (direct, the pillar sentence itself), is adopted. F2, predator and prey, was rejected because it drifts toward hunter vocabulary (the combat anti-pillar) and toward Horror Entities' turf, and because it implies a hunted *body* when this system watches the *ship*. F3, ship as nervous system, was rejected because it collides with the art bible's stage-3 body imagery, which belongs to the sanity lie ("pipes read as veins"); the sane world must not speak the lie's language. Only F2's *affect* (ART 2.7's small-animal line) is kept.

**Pillar text served (verbatim).**

> **Pillar 3, The Web Watches.** "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention. Attention is shown in the world (creaking, flicker, filament movement), never as a hidden meter. Depth sets the baseline; player action moves it from there. Restraint and stealth are real strategies. *Design test*: a flat spawn timer vs. attention-driven escalation → attention."
>
> **Pillar 2, Dread Before Chaos.** "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress). This pillar owns tempo and presentation; Pillar 3 owns causality. *Design test*: breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10–30 second escalating tell → the tell."

*Lexical note.* The pillar's word "flicker" stays inside the quotation; the pillar text is not ours to edit. In every sentence this GDD writes, the lamp effect is a **gutter** (Lighting bans "flicker" for the tell, LO-19, LO-20). *Scope note.* P2's examples "gauge drift" and "hull stress" are not Attention tell channels at MVP: the real-gauge needle is dropped (C1, AT-17) and hull-stress hairlines are cut and routed to the art-director (AT-20). Attention's tell is sound (a creak beat in a structure-borne bed) and light (filaments resolving at the cone's edge, dust turning, an optional lamp gutter). Hull stress is the breach's *consequence* (STRESS), which is where P2's example lands.

**The design test sentence (this system's, paired with the pillars').** *A breach — or any web-originated harm — that lands without a full escalating tell the player could have perceived and acted on, vs. one that lands only after a tell the player could have aborted by restraint → the abortable tell.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* In pillar form: **"a breach the player could not have prevented vs. a breach they chose not to prevent → the one they chose."** It has two machine-checkable halves. *Could have perceived:* `TellAudit` holds over every recorded run, and every legal `(tell_len, seed)` publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats in the floor window, the audio channel alone always sufficient and the visual channel alone sufficient while an eligible emitter is lit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Could have aborted:* the abortability invariant (AT-10) holds at every ring's survivable-dark floor. A tell that always ends in breach would be a timer in costume, which fails Pillar 3's design test; a tell the player physically cannot beat is the same thing.

**Anchor player moments (worked; numbers are the rulings' placeholders).**

*Moment A, "I can leave now" (the abort, the Pillar 3 payoff).* Derelict A, ring 1. The player is partway through a salvage cut with two fixtures lit from a breaker they closed earlier. The level crosses `ATT_THRESHOLD`; a seeded tell begins. Beat 0 is a creak in the bed, audio first. Over the next beats two or three filaments at the cone's edge resolve into strands, and the dust turns. The player releases the channel (the partial is kept for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`), stands still, kills the chest lamp and opens the breaker *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the stop is what aborts the tell; the lamp and the breaker each shave about 2 seconds, and the breaker's inrush returns its 2 seconds when it is closed again, F-25 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). The level falls under theta_HOLD and stays there for `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, or is under it at `breach_due_T`. Phase returns to CALM: no beacon, no STRESS, the beat stops. With the lamp off and the breaker open no lit region remains, so no filament is drawn at all (ART 3.3 / Principle 1). *An abort always forfeits the partial* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The earliest abort completes 594 ticks after the stop on a clean ship, 6 ticks (100 ms) inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, a window no player can use, and 606 ticks with the two wake glows the MVP ships with, past it (F-15b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and with the home ship lit it completes later still; the cut restarts from zero. That is the price of restraint, stated plainly and chosen on purpose, and the breach that has teeth (AT-11) is what balances it. *What the player learns:* the web is causal and restraint is a move. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* On a derelict, Moment A competes with walking out: a hop away from the locus voids the due-run breach and keeps the finished cut (AT-22), while the abort forfeits the partial, so when the pod is within about 37 tiles (the shortest played tell at the 2 px/tick walk; about 19 tiles at the 1 px/tick carry) leaving tends to beat restraint; the consequence is stated under Moment D, measured by AC-AT-134 and decided under OQ-AT-36 (leave it, measured; R21) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*.

*Moment B, "We chose to be loud" (commit, breach, escape).* Same setup, but the scrubber is worth it. The player keeps cutting through the tell and finishes; cut completion is a one-shot LOUD push (IA-33). At `breach_due_T` the level is still at or above theta_HOLD, and the breach commits. Derelict A's locus section and every section of its body take STRESS. A worn pipe that was ticking toward failure now fails faster; its trail outruns the player's walk. There is no beacon on A (the world's single beacon is on the home-ship): the held rim, the trails, the noise of failing hardware and Audio's alarm pulse on its fallback clock are the alarm (the tell bed has ended at BREACH entry, Audio AA-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. They carry the scrubber to the pod. **Pod-hop departure from derelict A ends the breach** *(Web Map WM-10 and WM-24; the departed derelict goes dormant in the same apply)*: they escaped, they did not win. The machine moves to SUBSIDING and the home-ship beacon switches off. In co-op the crew at home saw the beacon sweep red from breach entry until that departure (the true global fact "a breach is on"); the escaping player arrives to a dark beacon and silence. Derelict A's STRESS lapses on the Graph's own hold, after the alarm has stopped. *Had they hopped while the tell was still running,* the due-run breach would have been voided (AT-22): leaving during the warning is the cheapest escape, and it costs whatever they had not yet carried out *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Variant, home-ship locus:* there is no escape by pod; the breach runs until `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` or until the crew goes quiet for `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` after `BREACH_MIN_TICKS`; welded derelict sections share it, so a welded derelict is not a safe harbour. *What the player learns:* the breach was a price they chose with full warning, not an ambush. They can tell the story afterwards ("we heard it creaking and kept cutting"). Death in a breach is lethal (user decision: "death is a bitch. It happens.") and fair, because it was told.

*Moment C, "The lights we left on" (restated to what the MVP can make true).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The player leaves the home ship's fixtures lit and works a derelict. A derelict the player has left goes dormant at once and contributes nothing (WM-24, LS-17), and the lit home ship alone settles the level at 1,679 AU against a theta of 6,000 (F-25), so the lights **cannot raise a tell on their own**. They do two things *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. First, they **shorten the next loud act**: with the home ship lit, a player running with the beam on from the idle level crosses theta in 1,476 ticks (24.6 s); with it dark the same run takes 2,412 ticks (40.2 s). Leaving the lights on means the next loud act tips you about 16 seconds sooner at the placeholders (F-25). Second, they **hold a standing claim on the locus** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: light and power feed the section ledger through their own sections (F-2, F-6), and the lit idle home ship settles at a steady ledger of about 1,379,000 mAU (766 mAU per tick × 1,800). Noise dominates the ledger, so a player walking with the beam in the derelict (a steady 2,318,400: 1,288 × 1,800) keeps the locus there, and so does anyone working or hauling; but a player standing still in the derelict with only the beam on (a steady 518,400: 288 × 1,800) is quieter than the ship they left lit, so a tell that opens then (for example as a rearm or refractory window ends, E-14) has its locus on the home ship, and a home-ship locus cannot be escaped by pod (OQ-AT-12). The locus is also the home ship whenever the crew welds or runs there (AT-14). **One meaning, used everywhere in this document:** 'the lights you left on' is the home ship's standing light and power while the crew is elsewhere; it raises the level the crew carries and holds a ledger that wins the locus whenever the crew's own section is quieter than it at TELL entry. *What the player learns:* the web sees the ship, not the body, and a lit ship is a small standing debt that comes due the next time they are loud, or the next time they are quiet somewhere else.

*Moment D, "It's heavy" (the haul under a rising tell; user ruling X3).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The cut is done and the module comes off the wall; the player lifts it and starts for the pod. Carrying is a stimulus they chose and can hear: the Controller pushes a burden on every tick the cargo is in their hands (`U_BURDEN`, F-26), walking or standing, so a haul is never quiet and standing still with the cargo in hand is not a way to wait the web out: while they hold salvage the web forgets nothing (the decay is suspended, F-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. Only salvage counts: a module carried at home, or the player's own kit, adds no burden. Partway along the carry the hull creaks: the cut and the load together took the level across the line (at the placeholders, 318 ticks into a 28-tile carry). The player decides: keep walking and trust that the pod is nearer than the tell is long, or set the module down, go still and let the web lose interest, and come back for it. Setting it down is the lever; the module stays where it fell, and re-taking it needs light, and taking the same module up again straight after an abort grants no fresh grace (the rearm keys on the item, AT-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. *What the player learns:* the web hears what you carry as well as what you do, and 'drop the cargo' is the price of hauling, chosen with warning. **The consequence the rulings leave, stated so the user sees it before the first playable** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: keeping walking to the pod is usually the better answer, because a hop away from a derelict locus voids the due-run breach (AT-22) and keeps everything carried, while setting the module down to abort forfeits the haul for now; when the pod is nearer than the shortest tell is long (about 19 tiles at the 1 px/tick carry for a hauler, about 37 tiles at the 2 px/tick walk for anyone else, PC-15), walking out is close to dominant for any derelict tell, hauling or not, and both restraint aborts and breaches trend toward zero. This follows from two user rulings (the void, OQ-AT-12; the carry burden, X3) and is not reopened here: AC-AT-134 measures it and OQ-AT-36 is decided (leave it, measured; R21) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*.

*Moment ordering guard (the playtest tripwire for commit-dominance).* Moments A and B must both occur within the MVP 30-minute session for a typical player. If playtests show only A (never a breach) or only B (nobody aborts, or can never abort), the tuning is wrong, not the fantasy. Only B is the failure AT-11 names: a breach that costs too little against an abort that costs the partial makes commit dominant, empties P3's restraint and teaches players to ignore tells. Only A is the opposite failure: restraint is free and the breach is never met. The Feel Acceptance Criteria and AC-AT-80 carry the measurable form. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The guard is promoted to a **first-playable gate** together with the breaches-per-session range (3 to 8 told breaches in a 25-to-35-minute session, registered as `BREACHES_PER_SESSION`; Horror's HE-F16 assumes it): both degenerate poles fail it, throttle play (a level held just under theta) gives near zero breaches and riding every tell gives about 13 (F-25 occupancy), so neither a never-breached nor an always-breached build passes first-playable.

**Emotional arc across one cycle (never a meter).**

| Phase (`attention.view.phase`) | Player feels | What the world does | What the player can do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CALM** (boarding) | forensic curiosity under a held breath (ART 2.6); every door a decision, part of it how loud | dust drifts in the beam; "nothing else moves unless the player moves it"; filaments are texture at the cone's edge; the player's own run footsteps are audibly distinct from walking (PCI:135), the only noise readout. Above `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` the world stirs in `CUE_STEPS` quantised steps: ordinary hull-settling creaks a little more frequent, a few more filaments at the rim. Never a continuous mapping of `level_pm` | spend light, noise and power deliberately; weigh a module "against the noise of taking it" |
| **TELL** (`tell_len` from `TELL_MIN_TICKS` to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, seeded at entry) | *it noticed*: prickling, then leaning in | beat 0 in the entry run, audio first; on every beat one creak in the structure-borne bed, audible to every living player, and one filament-resolve in every lit region; spacing and density rise with the escalation clock (time spent at or above the hold line, frozen once the player's restraint has brought the level under it, 480 to 714 ticks after a full stop depending on what is left lit, so the beats keep tightening for that long first *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), never with progress, so nothing says when it ends *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; fixtures and lanterns may gutter (never the cryo emergency lamp); beam, ambient and camera do not change | **decide**: stand still and put the tool and any cargo down (the lever), kill the lamp or open a breaker (a little help), leave by pod (from a non-home locus this voids the breach, AT-22); sustained restraint aborts; or commit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **BREACH** | controlled panic (ART 2.8): "more things moving, not the screen fighting the player" | STRESS on the locus body; the home-ship beacon sweeps red; horrors may act inside the window (Horror's GDD, provisional); the level keeps integrating, so staying loud keeps it going up to `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | patch, re-route, go quiet, or leave (pod-hop departure from a non-home locus body ends it) |
| **SUBSIDING**, then refractory | *then silence* (LSG:986: "A long tell, a spreading wrongness, a short collapse, then silence") | beacon off; no new STRESS; the last deposit lapses on the Graph's hold, so the wrongness may outlast the alarm; filaments sink back; creaks thin | repair; the level is cut to a residual, not wiped, so a player still loud at the end of the refractory tips straight into a new tell |

*Flow note.* CALM is long and flow-friendly; TELL is a deliberate flow break that hands the player a decision; BREACH is high-arousal recovery; SUBSIDING plus the refractory is the pacing valley. The valley is gated by machine state and published in the read view; it is not a hidden scheduler.

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination (the tell is a truth channel, AT-17).** Nothing Attention publishes or presents is added, removed, delayed or altered by the Perception Filter or by Sanity, and Attention reads no sanity and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` value. This makes the tell one of Pillar 4's surviving channels of truth and gives low-sanity play a sharp verification tool.

1. *"No tell, no harm from the web."* One predicate (AT-15) is audited over every web-originated M17 RUPTURE/GOUGE/STRESS, every environmental M18 EXOGENOUS and, provisionally, every lethal act by a horror on a crew body. So when a phantom entity lunges (hallucination class a) or a false "hull failing" state appears (class b) with no creak beat, no resolving filaments and no beacon before it, the player has a cheap first check that costs attention: *the web didn't look up, so this isn't the web.* It is only as good as the listening behind it (you must have been listening to the bed), and listening is a spend, an ear kept on the structure instead of on the work. The check rules out web-originated **harm**, never an entity's **presence**: a thing standing in the dark in CALM may still be real, so "an entity seen in CALM is safe to ignore" is false by design. The boundary is honest: player-originated harm (a cut, a vent, a hallucinating crewmate's tool) needs no tell, and Horror's own stalk is additive, so the check rules out the web, not your crew. Friendly harm stays Sanity's gate and the human channel stays the way to verify a crewmate. *Routed must-considers.* To **Sanity & Hallucination Rules:** phantom entities should be able to co-occur with real tells and breaches, where they are hardest to separate from the real thing. To **Horror Entities:** a non-lethal presence or stalk outside BREACH is required, so CALM is never known-safe (AT-15 gates lethal acts only; without this the pair would hollow hallucination class (a) and the horror).
2. *Reading the rim.* Attention's filaments resolve at the cone's edge in the web's iridescence (teal → green → bronze → gold, ART 4.4). Sanity stage 2 puts a violet fringe at the same rim. The rule players learn: *iridescence at the rim is the web, and it is true; violet at the rim is you, and it is a lie.* The two must differ by more than hue under colourblind modes and the luminance ladder (shape: branching strands against a continuous fringe; motion: resolving on the beat against a static tint). Routed to the art-director (CR-RIM).
3. *Hidden real entities still leave Attention's footprint.* "The hallucination hides the body, never the trail" (game concept, Pillar 4(a)): a low-sanity player who sees nothing in the dark still hears the bed.
4. *The beam is never touched.* Radius, temperature and shape do not change in any phase (ART 2.7), and a crewmate's light is still the crewmate.
5. *Closed by pillar, permanently.* False calm (Sanity suppressing a real tell) and phantom tells are rejected, not deferred (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN). A false calm hides a real tell, which produces exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids; a phantom tell spends the player's restraint on a lie and voids `TellAudit`. The tell is a standing truth channel in every tier. Sanity keeps its own disjoint sound family and its own rim fringe (item 2).

**Reference techniques (mechanic level; adopt or reject).**

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | Exactly what is taken or refused |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | noise attracts; consequence shown only in the world | **adopt** | noise as the main moment-to-moment stimulus with a long memory (`TAU_ATT_TICKS`); no HUD sound radius; loudness learned from your own footsteps; lethal, unforgiving consequence (user decision) |
| Alien: Isolation | audio-first approach (vents bang before it drops) | **adopt the ordering** | beat 0 is audio, published in the TELL-entry run |
| Alien: Isolation | motion tracker | **reject as a display** | no device or HUD shows Attention, including accessibility settings (ART 7.0, 7.6) |
| Thief | light gem | **reject** | a visibility readout; visibility is read from your own cone and the fixtures you powered |
| Amnesia: The Dark Descent | sanity readout plus darkness penalty | **reject both** | Attention is not sanity; darkness is not punished by Attention, it is priced by the lit-only verb rule (IA-6) |
| Darkest Dungeon | stress bar | **reject** | an on-screen meter |
| Subnautica | distant leviathan call; depth-gated danger | **adopt both** | the bed as "heard everywhere, located nowhere"; the depth-set baseline as the depth gradient |
| Stellaris | dread from systems, not cutscenes | **adopt** | no scripted breach; the onboarding arm obeys the tell floor; no authored exception |
| Barotrauma | hull failure you watch propagate | **adopt for BREACH** | STRESS makes endogenous failures propagate faster (LS-25); the breach is the ship failing, not a red screen |
| Pandorum / Beyond the Aquila Rift | wrongness arriving in a dead ship, leaking at the edge | **adopt the tone** | the tell is at the edge; the beam interior is identical, contrast rises because the rim gets busy; the chest-mounted hands-free lamp is untouched |
| Left 4 Dead "AI Director" | hidden pacing timer scheduling threats | **reject** | fails Pillar 3's design test; the refractory is a published, state-gated valley, not a scheduler |
| Resident Evil 7 / Outlast | heartbeat, breathing, screen shake as dread | **reject** | "the ship changes, you don't" (PCI:135, PIPE:151) |

**Anti-pillar and neighbour guard rails the fantasy must not break.** (1) *Not a combat game:* Attention spawns nothing and defines no creature; the suppressant is a contract only, worded "repel or distract" and never "fight" or "kill"; a breach is survived or escaped, never won. (2) *Not intentional PvP:* Attention never gates or enables friendly harm; noise is attributed to sections for the locus only, never shown as blame, and no "who made the noise" readout exists. (3) *Not a freeform builder:* no placement bonus or penalty beyond what placed modules emit. (4) *No authored story, no win state:* no scripted breach; decay returns the level to the baseline and never below, and the survivable-dark floor holds at every ring, so a dark ship can always end a tell. (5) *The ship changes, you don't:* no adrenaline, speed, noise or control change in any phase (PCI:135, PCI:1010) and no camera reaction, shake or zoom (PIPE:151); the prose never says "your heart races", "tunnel vision" or "time slows". (6) *Nothing shows the level:* the UI Requirements section reads "none, by decision". (7) *The end of the tell is unreadable.* (8) *The sane palette:* iridescence only, never flesh or violet. (9) *Audio is the guaranteed channel and the visual channel exists while an eligible emitter is lit* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; a deaf player with an eligible emitter lit and a low-vision player each have one, a deaf player in the dark has the opt-in haptic pulse on beats and nothing else (user ruling, OQ-AT-1 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (10) *Restraint must be real and must not be free:* going dark costs verbs (lit-only, IA-6), time (`TAU_ATT_TICKS` memory, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, the lost partial) and a breaker's inrush (LSG:809). It does **not** cost air or heat: the survival loops stay on through the dark (they are the unswitchable load of AT-6 and E-38), so the breaker is a small help and never a lever (about 2 seconds saved, given back by the inrush on re-closing, F-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier claim that the loops shut off in the dark contradicted E-38, AT-6 and F-16)*.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are named `AT-n`. Each states a **Rule**, a **Criterion** (acceptance-criterion ids `AC-AT-nn`, written in Acceptance Criteria; the tell-window ones are written first, as the index requires) and a **Pillar**. Citations name the counterpart document and section; line numbers (`LSG:144`, `EM:247`, `IA:276`, `SL:528`, `ART:741`) were verified against the files on disk on 2026-10-26 and are given for convenience. **Units.** A *run* is one execution of `attention.step` (6 ticks at `TICK_RATE`, the Graph's `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`); the level is stored in **mAU** (milli-attention-units, 64-bit integer); **1 AU is one walking tick of noise** (defined by the Controller-owned registry row `NOISE_PER_TICK`, cited, never restated with a number here). All durations are ticks. Every new number below is a `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/attention/`; the ruling that fixed its form is named in parentheses. **Disk state at authoring (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26, re-run at the part 2 pass: 17 files including this one):** designed and read: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Suit & Equipment, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Interaction System, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Item & Module Database, Failure & Trail, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera. Two neighbours are on disk with status `In Design` and are **siblings in flight**, so they are cited by section id first and their line numbers drift (every `BDY:` and `DA-` line below is as of the 21:21 write and must be re-grepped): **Ship Body & Interior Physics** (`ship-body-and-interior-physics.md`) and **Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)** (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`; DA-24: exactly one BEACON in the world, on `home_ship` in `spine_corridor`, `node_ref` = `bcn_main`, a dedicated LOAD node (`mod_lamp`, 20 W, DA-24; OQ-DA-9 closed 'no sharing'), lint `BEACON_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT), none on derelicts A or B; DA-23: `fx_cryo_lamp` is an EMERGENCY fixture, self-powered (`node_ref` 0) and never modulated by Attention's tell). *(Superseded 2026-10-27 — design review: every file named in this paragraph now exists with status Designed, including Ship Body and Derelict Authoring, which were In Design when this was written. Their rows are no longer provisional as to existence; each *must repeat* clause is a contract to diff, not a status to tick. Line numbers in this document are advisory and drift: cite by section or rule id.)* No file existed for Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Web Map & Transit, Modular Ship Assembly, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table or Debug Overlay at authoring: every contract with those was **provisional** and carries a *must repeat* clause.

#### Group A — Identity and scope

**AT-1. Block and hook identity.** *Rule.* One `SIM` block `attention.state` and one step hook `attention.step`. Both ids are new; the hook's `after` ids already exist, so an unknown id cannot fail boot.

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| hook | `(hook_id attention.step, owner attention, divisor 6, offset 0, after [graph.propagate.power, emitter.place])` |
| why offset 0 | LS-8: a reader shares its writer's offset (LSG:104). The kernel's stagger example "Graph 0, Attention 2, Suit 4" (KRN Formulas, D.6 Output Range) is a refresh owed (C3); it is not edited here |
| ordering | `emitter.place` is divisor 1, so a hook declaring `after: [emitter.place]` sees the current tick's emitters (EM-25); `graph.propagate.power` (root) is the Graph's power phase, so `power_use` is this step's. Suit's `suit.vitals` precedes the hook transitively (`suit.vitals → controller.step → body.integrate → emitter.place → attention.step`), so no `after: suit.vitals` is declared: Attention reads no Suit state at MVP |
| block | `class SIM`, `persist` true, `persist_scope WORLD`, `scope SHARED`, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions [1]`, `restore_after [graph.state, emitter.state]` (section ids resolve in `post_restore`) |
| restore | SL-15 in full: two-phase `restore`, with failure reported through `ctx.fault(ATTENTION_RESTORE_*)` carrying the owner's domain code and a named detail, never by return value; the kernel fires `load_failed` and Save/Load maps it to the `RESTORE_FAILED` class (ADR-0006 section 2.2; the former `(ok, reason, detail)` return form is retired) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)*; `restore_epoch` is **not** a `restore` argument: Save/Load owns it in the per-slot `slot_epoch_a/b.bin` pair *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14)* and the kernel exposes it read-only in `kernel.clock`, and only the kernel's `RngFacet.draw_epoch` reads it there to key tell draws made after a restore (AT-8); Attention never reads it and never stores it in the block *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)*; closed reason set `ATTENTION_RESTORE_{SCHEMA, RANGE, PHASE_INCOHERENT, SECTION_UNKNOWN, RNG_COUNTER}`; an out-of-range value is a hard failure and is **never clamped**; mutators track dirty keys explicitly (Godot 4.7: a packed-array element write does not call the property setter); a restore round trip is idempotent |
| persisted fields | `level_mAU`, `phase`, `tell_epoch`, `tell_start_T`, `tell_len`, `breach_due_T`, `tell_beat`, `esc_ticks` (the escalation clock, AT-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `beat_next_off`, `below_hold_runs`, `clear_runs`, `breach_enter_T`, `subside_end_T`, `refract_until_T`, `rearm_until_T`, `locus_section_id`, `locus_body` (the section set recorded at TELL entry), `locus_occupants_at_entry` (the living seats inside `locus_body` at BREACH entry), `tell_occupants_at_entry` (the living seats inside `locus_body` at TELL entry, AT-22), `occ_gone` (one bit per recorded seat, F-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `tell_outcome`, `rng_counter`, `pending_noise_mAU`, `pending_burden` (bool, F-1, F-5) and `tell_carry_items` (sorted item ids, at most one per seat, AT-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*, `pending_ledger_mAU[section]`, `ledger_mAU[section]`, `nl_hist_w[section]`, `held_ring`, `stress_next_burst_run`, `stress_pending_cursor`, `beacon_commanded`, `beacon_retry_left`. The pending noise scalar, the pending ledger entries, the ledger, the power history, the RNG counter and every timer persist (G4 save-equivalence). Section-keyed arrays are stored sorted by `section_id` and hold loaded sections only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `pending_ledger_mAU`, `ledger_mAU` and `nl_hist_w` (five ints per section) therefore have at most one entry per loaded section (the home body plus at most one unwelded derelict, WM-24), which bounds the block. The ranges SL-15 checks on restore are: `pending_noise_mAU` and every `pending_ledger_mAU[s]` in `[0, L_CEIL]`; every `nl_hist_w` value in `[0, 10,000]`; keys unique, sorted and known; the seat set sorted and made of bound seats (AC-AT-70); `esc_ticks` in `[0, TELL_MAX_TICKS]`, a multiple of 6, 0 outside TELL and at most `T − tell_start_T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `occ_gone` has exactly one bit per seat of the recorded set in force; `tell_carry_items` is sorted, unique, at most one per bound seat and empty when neither TELL nor the rearm window holds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `schema_version` stays 1 because nothing is implemented or saved yet: these are design-time field additions, not a migration. The `source` PlayerId tag is **transient**: it is consumed at push time (attribution, AT-32 diagnostics) and never stored, so Vertical Slice per-player attribution adds one persisted per-player array and a schema bump, a data change and not a rewrite |
| dirty keys | `ATT/L` (level) and `ATT/P` (phase block). The level changes every run, so presentation **polls** `attention.view`; the notification is not treated as an event |

*Criterion.* AC-AT-62 (hook and block registration; AC-AT-01 is the tell-window contract and is written first), AC-AT-22 (G4 mid-tell snapshot), AC-AT-23 (no reroll). *Pillar.* P2, P3 (serves P1 through truth). *Decided by:* R1; KRN CR-3, CR-8; LSG:104; EM-25.

**AT-2. One level, a ledger only to choose the locus.** *Rule.* There is **one** attention level `L` for the world: the web watches the ship, not a body. A per-section `ledger_mAU` exists only to choose the locus (AT-14); it decays with the same `TAU_ATT_TICKS` as `L`, so the locus reflects *recent* contribution and a section lit for an hour does not win forever. Noise carries a `source` PlayerId tag (transient, consumed at push time, AT-1) so Vertical Slice attribution is a data change, not a rewrite. Dormant (unloaded) sections contribute nothing and hold no ledger entry (LSG:174, LS-17). A per-section level, a per-player level and a second machine were weighed and rejected: they multiply Horror's work, break Save/Load's single `tell_remaining`, and exceed "MVP: one threshold".
*Criterion.* AC-AT-02. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R2; game concept MVP definition.

#### Group B — Intake: two paths, no others

**AT-3. Path 1, push: noise, one mutator, class-based.** *Rule.* `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)` is a published **Class B mutator** (clamp, never reject; the kernel's CR-9 path a). `kind` is `NOISE` at MVP; other values are reserved and rejected by lint. `class ∈ {QUIET, WALK, RUN, CARRY, TOOL, LOUD, BURDEN}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. An unknown class is a counted no-op. It is callable only inside a `WriteScope` by the declared callers, the Controller and Interaction (Horror Entities pushes no noise, HE-36, and is not a declared caller). A push adds `U[class] × 1000` mAU to the scalar `pending_noise_mAU` (clamped at `L_CEIL`) and, when its source resolves **at push time** (inside the caller's WriteScope, through Ship Body's `actor_section` for a PlayerId or `section_of` for an object) to a **loaded** section, the same amount to `pending_ledger_mAU[section]` (clamped at `L_CEIL`); a source in a dormant or unknown section adds to the scalar only (E-3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Attention owns the unit table `U[class]`; the Controller and Interaction own the *classes*.**

| Class | `U[class]` (AU per push) | Owner of the number | Pushed by | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `QUIET` | 0 | Attention | Interaction | never adds; counted |
| `WALK` | cited from registry `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick |
| `RUN` | cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (run) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick, ramp ticks included (PCI:560) |
| `CARRY` | cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (carry) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick |
| `TOOL` | `U_TOOL` placeholder 4 | Attention | Interaction | once per *accrued* channel tick; a tick that aborts before accrual pushes nothing (IA-33) |
| `LOUD` | `U_LOUD` placeholder 180 (= 30 × 6) | Attention | Interaction | one-shot at cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply, throw landing, and the `berth` apply (IA-40; default applied pending confirmation, OQ-AT-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `BURDEN` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | `U_BURDEN` placeholder 7 (7,000 mAU per tick, derived in F-26) | Attention | Controller | every tick a **salvage carry** is held, moving or standing, until the item is dropped, installed or thrown; a salvage carry is a carried instance (any class, any mass) that was picked up inside a non-home locus, that is, a section of a body other than the home ship (a derelict welded to the home ship is part of the home body under Layout, E-44, so an item taken there is not salvage); never for carrying at home or for the player's own kit; the push is also gated on place *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: it is pushed only on ticks when the holder's `actor_section` is in a non-home locus body and the holder's `loco_state` is not `IN_POD`. It stops when the seat enters the pod (in the fiction, the web hears the taking at the wreck, Boarding BS-15) and never runs aboard the home body, so the pod leg and the walk to the home hardpoint push nothing and the decay resumes there; the item stays a salvage carry (the flag and `carry_ref` persist) and the push resumes if the seat carries it back aboard a non-home body (answers Boarding ASK-BS-A2 (a), OQ-BS-30, delegated ruling R5); keyed on the carry state and never on `mass_g` (IDB-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* |

Three relations are handed to Attention by Interaction and **adopted** (IA-33, hand-over I-8, Interaction OQ 13; registry `noise_push`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`) and become registered formulas and lint criteria: **IR-1** `2 ≤ U_TOOL ≤ 6`; **IR-2** `U_LOUD ≥ 30 × U_TOOL` (180 holds across the *whole* legal `U_TOOL` range, not only at the default); **IR-3** `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS`. A fourth, **IR-4**, is Attention's own and binds `U_BURDEN` to the reference carry (`carry_decision`, F-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: it asserts the crossing and **reports** the live share; the live-choice definition and its pass line are Boarding & Salvage's (K3, AC-BS-123), stated with Attention's integrator *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. A breaker close pushes nothing, a door motor pushes nothing and an `unberth` pushes nothing (QUIET) (IA-33, IA-40); the Graph's inrush enters as a *power* stimulus through `power_use` (AT-4). A push that lands after `attention.step` in the same tick, by topological order, is integrated on the next run; this is deterministic, so no `after: interaction.*` is declared. **Noise timing (orchestrator ruling: the ruling-time wording "the Controller keeps pushing from the previous tick's `moved`" is superseded by Ship Body & Interior Physics 4.1 on disk).** Ship Body (its read table for `moved(actor_id)` and its Cross-References refresh row "Controller / Attention", cited by section id first, as BDY-19 and BDY 4.1 (that row's own rule column; 4.1 is open with the Controller as owner); sibling in flight, lines drift: at 23:14 the Dependents row is `BDY:344` and the refresh row is `BDY:988`, at the 21:21 write `BDY:341` and `BDY:985`, at the 21:10 checkpoint `:497` and `:936`) does *not* adopt a persisted `moved`; it offers `moved(actor_id)` readable only from a hook ordered `after: [body.integrate]`, and the Controller owner chooses between a second divisor-1 Controller hook and another reader. Attention is indifferent: a zero- or one-tick lag is below its six-tick resolution, and the sum Attention sees per run is the Controller's per-tick pushes of up to six ticks (0 to 24 AU per run for one runner, PCI:560). **Attention repeats Body's text and accepts either** (Ship Body CP-3, 8.3 row "Controller / Attention"): running noise uses `moved` only from a hook ordered after `body.integrate` (the Controller's chosen hook, `controller.noise`, PC-10, which also carries the `BURDEN` push *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*); Attention reads `actor_section` and `body_of_actor` only, from `attention.step`, which is ordered transitively after `body.integrate` (`suit.vitals → controller.step → body.integrate → emitter.place → attention.step`). The Controller's change from "pushes a number" to "pushes a gait class" (its registry row resolves the class through `NOISE_PER_TICK`; its D.13 maps `loco_state` to the class) is a small refresh owed to the Controller (C9).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-03, AC-AT-04, AC-AT-05 (lint IR-1 to IR-3), AC-AT-06a to AC-AT-06e (a 180-tick patch pushes exactly 720,000 mAU; the AC-IA-107 stub), AC-AT-06f (the `BURDEN` push), AC-AT-132 (IR-4). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R3; IA:645 ("class, never units"); registry `NOISE_PER_TICK`: "Attention's intake defines the units".

**AT-4. Path 2, ordered read: light and power.** *Rule.* At each run, after `emitter.place` and `graph.propagate.power`, Attention reads (a) `emitters()` for every loaded section (id, kind, `section_id`, `output_pm`, `reach_px`), and (b) `power_use(section_id)` per loaded section. It makes **no** `probe()` call, so `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK` is unused (the registry note already says Attention does not read it). Light is a weighted sum of each emitter's term, where the inner term is the Emitter State's own `section_emission` per-emitter term (EM D.12), **cited by name, never re-derived here**. Weights are per emitter kind, in one dimension (micro-AU per unit per tick), placeholders:

| Kind (registry `emitter_kind`, closed list) | `W_L` | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `BEAM` (the owner's chest beam) | 1000 | counted **once**, from the emitter list at apex value; Attention never reads `lux_pm` as a stimulus and never adds the beam a second time (EM-21, EM-27) |
| `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN` | 1000 | the cryo emergency fixture and the DOCK_STATUS pod glow (X2) count as light (real emitters) but are never tell carriers (LO-20; AT-20) |
| `DEPLOYABLE` (glowsticks at MVP) | 1000 | light is a spend, never a safe state (EM:722) |
| `FAULT_GLOW` | 1000 in CALM and TELL; **0** in BREACH and SUBSIDING | a breach's own fault glows never prolong it |
| `BEACON` | **0** (lint: always 0) | the alarm must not feed itself |

**Shape-blind by decision (default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): Emitter D25).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `W_L` is one weight per kind and ignores `light_shape`: a focused searchlight is louder (the narrow beam's EM D.12 term is about 2.3 times the fan's). That is deliberate, stated in the Fantasy and here, and an accident to fix only if a playtest shows players reading the lamp choice as a trap; the lever then is a per-shape `emission_weight_pm` column in the Emitter's table, not a second Attention weight. **Beam spill and switch spacing (Emitter D24 user ruling, D32 default).** The beam is still counted once, from its record: if the conditional `BEAM_SPILL` shape row ships (`BEAM_SPILL_PM` 600 × `BEAM_SPILL_RADIUS_PX` 24 ÷ 1000 = 14 mAU per tick under EM D.12, 4.9 percent of the wide beam's 288) it is the same record's term, cited and never re-derived, and the F-24 lint re-runs with it (it moves `walk_beam_pm` by about 4 permille, inside the 100-permille margin). A lamp switch-off refused `TOO_SOON` waits at most `SWITCH_MIN_SPACING_TICKS` (12 ticks, Emitter-owned) from the last toggle, which is inside `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` (180) and so changes no lint.

Power *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the one formula is **F-4**; this rule states it in words only (it no longer carries a second, unscaled copy). Attention reads the Graph's `power_use(section_id)` exactly as LS-19 now defines it: `draw_w` is the section's total served load (lamps and inrush included), `lamps_lit_w` is the served watts of LOAD subtype LAMP (it includes the beacon's own node `bcn_main`), `delta_w_last5` is a *signed* five-run delta that **includes lamp watts** and reads 0 after a load, and `noisy_w` is the served watts of SCRUBBER, PUMP, HEATER and DOOR_MOTOR nodes. Lamps are light and must not be counted twice (EM-27), so Attention works on the **non-lamp draw** `nl_w = max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w)`: a steady term `W_P × nl_w` and a transient term `W_S × max(0, nl_w now − nl_w five runs ago)`. Because the Graph publishes no lamp-excluded delta, Attention keeps its own five-deep history of `nl_w` per loaded section (`nl_hist_w[section]`, persisted, AT-1), seeded flat with the current value whenever a section loads or wakes, so a section that wakes already drawing 2,500 W adds **0** transient input on its first run. The Graph's `delta_w_last5` and `noisy_w` are **not** read (`noisy_w` stays reserved at weight `W_N` 0). The beacon needs no special case: `bcn_main` is a LAMP node, so its draw and its switch-on step are inside `lamps_lit_w` and never reach `nl_w` (the earlier `beacon_w` and `beacon_step_w` subtractions are deleted). Placeholders `W_P` 100, `W_S` 6000 micro-AU per W per tick. **`served_pm` is not a power stimulus:** it already reaches Attention as light through `output_pm` (registry `power_allocation`), and using it again would double count. **Graph ask (a simplification, not a dependency; C11 closed against LS-19, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*).** If the Graph owner publishes a lamp-excluded delta (a fifth `power_use` field, or a delta of `draw_w − lamps_lit_w`), Attention deletes `nl_hist_w` and reads it; the value of F-4 does not change. Carried in OQ-AT-6 and the cross-GDD notes. If `power_use` is absent, `I_power` is 0 for that section and one diagnostic is logged per process. The **Suit pushes nothing** (SE-31); **Assembly pushes nothing at MVP**, because Interaction already pushes LOUD at cut completion and a second push would double count; no `LOUD` is pushed at weld completion by anyone (Modular Ship Assembly T14 C5; IA-33). **Attention never reads Audio** (lint; the player's footsteps enter only as the Controller's pushed gait class).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-07, AC-AT-08, AC-AT-09, AC-AT-10, AC-AT-63, AC-AT-120. *Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by:* R5, R6, R25; EM:247 ("Attention owns units and weights"; "Off-screen and unoccupied emitters count"); LSG:794, LSG:809.

#### Group C — The level, the baseline, the threshold

**AT-5. The integrator: one level, one memory constant (a rate detector).** *Rule.* Each run: the three channel inputs are summed (`I_run = I_noise + I_light + I_power`), a **ceiling decay** toward the baseline `B` is subtracted, and the result is clamped to `[B, L_CEIL]`. There is one memory, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` (placeholder 1800: a job split across `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` then retains about 72 percent). There is **no separate spike term**: the power inrush enters as ordinary input over its at most five steps, because a second memory would contradict Interaction's single `tau_att`. The ceiling decay reaches `B` *exactly* (the integer truncation stall of LSG:464 cannot occur). **A held salvage carry suspends the decay** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: on a run that drained at least one `BURDEN` push (the persisted flag `pending_burden`, AT-1, F-1), `dec` is 0, so while any seat holds a salvage carry the level can only rise; the section ledger keeps its own decay (it only chooses the locus). Freezing with cargo is priced (user ruling X3), and the abortability lints are unchanged because every one of them assumes the cargo is set down (F-15b). Calibration placeholders: `ATT_THRESHOLD` (theta) 6000 AU, so continuous running with the beam on crosses theta in tens of seconds and continuous walking settles near 30 percent of theta; the derivation is in Formulas. A lint also bounds theta **from above** (F-5): a theta near the asymptote of the sustained verbs, about 8,000 AU, would make running or cutting unable to cause a tell *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**ADDITION X1 (adopted by checkpoint ruling CQ-2; APPROVED at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate): `L_CEIL`.** `L_CEIL = ATT_THRESHOLD × L_CEIL_PM / 1000`, placeholder `L_CEIL_PM` 1020. The ruled abortability invariant (AT-10 b) is stated for "the highest possible entry level, theta plus one run's input", but the entry level is not bounded by that: during the refractory and rearm windows the level keeps integrating and a runner can reach several times theta before the next legal tell, and one run's maximum input has no design bound (96 emitters alone are over 200 AU per run at weight 1000). Saturating the level at `L_CEIL` is the only way for the proof to exist, and it is what makes the abortability proof, and so P2's "window of agency", true at every entry level (the gate's reason for approving it). It changes nothing below theta.
**Stance (so no later pass "fixes" it): the level is a short-memory rate detector, not a session score.** With a memory of tens of seconds, a TELL means "you have been loud or lit at a high rate for roughly half a minute to a minute", never "you have done much in total". That makes causality local and legible (P3) and is why pacing comes from behaviour and not from drift. A longer `TAU_ATT_TICKS` is the only lever that makes a whole sortie matter, and it is capped from above by abortability (AT-10).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-11, AC-AT-12, AC-AT-13, AC-AT-14, AC-AT-57a to AC-AT-57g (calibration traces). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R4; IA:221 and :321 (one `tau_att`).

**AT-6. Baseline by depth.** *Rule.* `B = B_table[ring_B]`, where `ring_B` is the **maximum ring over sections that contain a living player** (a deterministic rule; "loudest player" is rejected as undefined). `ring_of(section_id)` is the registry formula `ring_of` (Web Map & Transit): the owning record's ring, a welded section resolving to the HOME ring, a parked pod to the ring of the record at `pod_at`, a pod mid-leg to the departure record's ring (equal to `held_ring`, AT-22); an absent or sub-1 ring resolves to 1, logged once per process. While a player is `IN_POD` the ring of the departure section is held (`held_ring`) until arrival. MVP ships ring 1 plus placeholder rows for rings 2 and 3 for tests; per-ring values belong to Depth Rings & Scarcity (Vertical Slice). A ring change that raises `B` above `L` lifts `L` to `B` at once; one that lowers `B` lets `L` decay toward the new floor. Two invariants are written as lint and automated criteria over the data: (i) `B_table` is non-decreasing with ring; (ii) the **survivable-dark floor**: at every ring, the dark steady level `x_dark` (the baseline plus everything the player cannot switch off, with the player still and the beam off) must satisfy **both** bounds. *The trivial steady bound* (R7 ii as ruled): `x_dark ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`. *The binding bound* (F-15, AT-10 b): the decay from `L_CEIL` at that floor must reach `theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN` before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse, which caps `x_dark` far below the trivial bound (about 1,253 AU at the placeholders, against 4,750 AU for the trivial one). **The unswitchable set** is named: (1) the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (`fx_cryo_lamp`, self-powered, DA-23), (2) `FAULT_GLOW` emitters (counted in CALM and TELL; a latent fault the player cannot extinguish), (3) `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W`, the home-ship's minimum survival load (O2, heat, reactor) with lamps off, through the power channel, and (4) the DOCK_STATUS glow of the berthed pod's dock room (X2: self-powered, `node_ref` 0 and switched by SYSTEM only, so the player cannot extinguish it; at most one is lit at a time because there is one pod; its output times reach is budgeted in F-16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The lint re-runs whenever Assembly, Authoring or the Graph changes that set. This is what guarantees that restraint can always end a tell and that no depth makes a breach unavoidable. **Which abortability guarantee holds** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: two statements had been blurred. **(A) Abortable at the switches:** a player who zeroes every input, sets any cargo down, kills the lamp and opens the breaker (home dark, survival load only) falls under the line inside the floor length at every ring row (F-15, F-16); this is the **guaranteed** one and the data lint enforces it at every row (the open breaker is the lint's best-case posture, not a lever the player needs: in play it saves about 2 seconds that its inrush gives back on re-closing, and the abort share by reaction latency of F-25 shows what each posture buys *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). **(B) Abortable anywhere:** the same player with the home ship left lit and not at the switches (the lit fixtures keep feeding the level) must still fall under the line within the **played** range (F-15b, row `x_dark_home_lit`); it holds at MVP ring 1 only, with thin slack (48 ticks on a clean ship, 24 with the two wake glows) and fails at the ring 2 and ring 3 test rows with the glows (-6 and -30 ticks), so those rows are flagged `SWITCHES`. Positive transients the player did not cause (an auto-reclose after a SCRAM, duty cycling, a STRESS-driven fault) are bounded by neither lint; the new row `autonomous_transient` adds the worst one (a 1,500 W cold start: 270 AU over five runs, landing at its worst moment) and shows that (B) does **not** survive it (-138 ticks at ring 1 with the glows) while (A) at the played range survives it at ring 1 (slack 30) and not at the ring-3 test row with the glows (-12). The creative-director's preference is (B) at ring 1 including the transient, met by retuning (the computed routes are `TELL_DRAW_LO` at least 1,038 or `HOLD_PM` about 850, each of which changes the felt tell or the cost of restraint and a registered value); that choice is OQ-AT-26, **default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D49): neither retune is applied**, (A) abortable at the switches stays the guarantee and the `autonomous_transient` row stays boot-advisory (WARN), to be revisited after the AC-AT-57 harness exists. The X3 recalibration (F-26) adds only a player-controlled input (the cargo, set down with `drop`) and changes no input of F-15, F-15b or F-16, so it does not move this default *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
**Consequence stated honestly (risk RK-2):** at the ruled memory and hold band the invariant caps the deep-ring baseline at a small fraction of theta, so on the Attention axis P3's "Depth sets the baseline" and P5's "Deeper Is Richer" are almost inert; that is acceptable for a ring-1-only MVP and not beyond it. **Pillar-flagged Vertical Slice REQUIREMENT (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN; no longer a proposal):** a per-ring stimulus gain on the light and power channels only, never applied to noise (footsteps are the player's only noise readout and its scale must not change with depth), which Depth Rings & Scarcity must satisfy or surface as a CONFLICT. Vertical Slice acceptance criterion: AC-AT-119.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-15, AC-AT-16, AC-AT-64 (`abortability_play`). *Pillar.* P5, P3. *Decided by:* R7; IDX bullet 17 ("baseline set by depth"), Systems Enumeration row for Depth Rings.

**AT-7. One threshold, one hold band.** *Rule.* One threshold `theta = ATT_THRESHOLD`, identical for every ring at MVP, plus a hold band `theta_HOLD = theta × HOLD_PM / 1000` (placeholder `HOLD_PM` 800). TELL entry requires **all** of: phase CALM, `L ≥ theta`, `T ≥ refract_until_T`, and `T ≥ rearm_until_T` (the guards are inclusive, so entry exactly at a timer boundary is legal). Per-ring thresholds are a Vertical Slice addendum. **(Orchestrator ruling: R8's absolute definition of theta_HOLD stands.)** The game-designer's proposal GD-3 defines the hold line relative to the baseline instead (`B + HOLD_PM × (theta − B) / 1000`) because the absolute line lengthens the descent when `B` is large; the two are close at ring 1. The question is carried to Open Questions; it is moot while the baseline stays under the cap that AT-6 (ii) and AT-10 (b) impose, and the lint over every ring row is the guard.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-17. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R8; game concept ("MVP: one threshold").

#### Group D — The tell

**AT-8. The tell window: length and the floor (contract part 1).** *Rule.* At TELL entry, `tell_len = rng_int(TELL_DRAW_LO, TELL_DRAW_HI)` (inclusive), drawn through the kernel's `RngFacet` as `draw_epoch(attention.tell, rng_counter)` (= `rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "attention.tell", rng_counter)`); Attention computes no `counter_eff`, never reads `restore_epoch` and registers the stream with `epoch_keyed = true` (the facet adds the epoch term inside `draw_epoch` and reads `world_seed` and `restore_epoch` from `kernel.clock`; the derivation and `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` are ADR-0005's, Horror's `horror.tell` uses the same path) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync: ADR-0005 section 2.4; closes OQ-6; supersedes delegated ruling R1's "Attention calls the kernel's `counter_eff` derivation")*, then `rng_counter` increments *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling D16: threat draws re-key per restore epoch)*. Two things stay exact: `rng_counter` is persisted and restored unchanged, and a `tell_len` already drawn is persisted with the block, so a restore taken during a TELL replays that tell's length and beats and cannot reroll it (no reroll oracle). What changes is the key of every draw made **after** a restore. `restore_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* is Save/Load's persisted u32, held in the per-slot `slot_epoch_a/b.bin` pair *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)* (each generation header keeps only a read-only copy, so generation bytes and every trailer SHA never change) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14)*: it starts at 0; a death-stub restore advances it by 1, and so does a Continue of any generation other than the newest non-demoted verified `QUIT` generation (after a kill, crash or fallback, in one process or after a fresh start); Save/Load makes each advance durable before it calls `request_restore`. A Continue of that newest `QUIT` generation carries it unchanged, so a Save & Quit then Continue replays the identical tell draws (quit buys no re-roll), while two death restores of one autosave give two new epochs. If the epoch write fails, the restore proceeds with the advanced epoch in memory and Save/Load logs `SL_EPOCH_NOT_DURABLE` with `save_status = FAILED`; a kill before any later successful write may replay that epoch once, an exposure Save/Load lists and owns (R14). The kernel exposes it read-only in `kernel.clock` as `(T, world_seed, restore_epoch)`. It is never stored in `attention.state`, so the block's round trip stays idempotent, and **no fallback is needed**: the clock always carries an epoch (0 before the first restore). Determinism is defined given (save bytes, `restore_epoch`): the same pair replays identically, and tests pin the epoch. `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` is the fixed 2^20 owned with the derivation by the kernel's `RngFacet` (ADR-0005 section 2.1; value unchanged; Attention keeps its own lint `rng_counter < RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE`, which the facet also counts as `RNG_COUNTER_RANGE_VIOLATION`) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*; the Graph's physical failures and every other persisted counter replay exactly as before, because Pillars 1 and 5 want the scrubber that fails at minute four to fail at minute four. Attention's requirements on the supplied value (Save/Load and the kernel own the mechanism): it is constant between restores, it differs after each advancing restore (every death-stub restore; every Continue except one of the newest `QUIT` generation) and is unchanged by a Continue of the newest `QUIT` generation, and it is a function of the restore history alone (never of player input, a wall clock or a random source), so the player cannot choose it and quit and death offer no way to read or pick a length beforehand (OQ-37). The beat jitter keeps a pure integer hash, now the shared `Ihash.h32(HASH_SEED, tell_epoch, k, 0, SALT_BEAT)` of ADR-0005 section 2.6 *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*: ±`BEAT_JITTER_TICKS` ticks cannot carry an ambush. `TELL_MIN_TICKS` is **derived, not a knob**: `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)`. `TELL_MAX_TICKS` is fixed by Pillar 2's text ("10–30 seconds") and by Save/Load's `tell_remaining` range. The tunable draw bounds satisfy `TELL_MIN_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO ≤ TELL_DRAW_HI ≤ TELL_MAX_TICKS` (lint). **Draw bounds (CQ-4, closed): `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 and `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 `[PLACEHOLDER]`**, so a tell a player meets in play lasts 15 to 30 seconds, inside Pillar 2's 10 to 30; the full 600-tick floor is reachable only through `attention_arm_tell` (AT-21) and fixtures. A second lint, `abortability_play`, binds the played range: `t_decay(ring) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO − 6` at every ring (`REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180 `[PLACEHOLDER]`; it also runs the `x_dark_home_lit` and `autonomous_transient` rows, F-15b, AT-6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the R10 (b) invariant still binds at `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (AT-10). **Hard rule:** no path reaches BREACH before `tell_start_T + tell_len`, and `tell_len ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS` on **every** path, including `attention_arm_tell` (AT-21). There is no "LOUD event → instant breach". Attention uses stream `"attention.tell"`; Horror keeps `"horror.tell"` for its own sub-tell (the kernel example is a refresh owed, C8). Rate-coupled length is a Vertical Slice playtest candidate.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-01a (written first), AC-AT-18, AC-AT-19, AC-AT-30, AC-AT-64, AC-AT-130 (the re-key). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R9; Pillar 2 design test; SL:528; user ruling D16 (OQ-AT-29).

**AT-9. Escalation: elapsed-keyed beats and tiers.** *Rule.* Escalation is keyed to the **escalation clock** `esc_ticks`, published as `tell_elapsed` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling OQ-AT-25, Option A)*: it is 0 on the TELL-entry run and then advances by 6 on every run whose integrated `L' ≥ theta_HOLD` and stays where it is on every run under the line (the same comparison that counts `below_hold_runs`, AT-10). It is **never** keyed to progress through `tell_len`: a progress-keyed cadence peaks exactly at the breach and leaks the end, whereas keyed to the player's own behaviour a 600-tick and an 1800-tick tell are identical for as long as the clock runs (the dread of not knowing), and nothing the clock carries depends on `tell_len` or `breach_due_T`. A player who stays loud sees the clock equal real elapsed time (`T − tell_start_T`) and the schedule below is the old elapsed-keyed one exactly; a player who goes under the hold line freezes the beat interval and the tier where they are, because the signal is earned: complying never makes the tell worse, and the escalation resumes only if the level climbs back to the line. The clock never runs backwards, so `tier` never falls inside an epoch. Beats keep publishing in real time at the frozen interval until the abort or the due run; only the interval and the tier freeze. Real elapsed time stays derivable as `T − phase_enter_T` and is not a hidden quantity. One SIM counter, `tell_beat`, drives **both** senses: each increment is one audio beat and one visual filament-resolve event in every lit region. Beat 0 is published in the TELL-entry run, so audio is first and the presenter may render no filament for an epoch before beat 0 exists (the art bible's rule that every tell has an audio component starting no later than its visual, HR-9). The beat interval shrinks from `BEAT_START_TICKS` (150) to `BEAT_END_TICKS` (45) over `BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` (1800) of the escalation clock, with jitter of ±`BEAT_JITTER_TICKS` (8) from a **pure integer hash**, `Ihash.h32(HASH_SEED, tell_epoch, k, 0, SALT_BEAT)`, never from `rng_derive` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*, and each gap at least `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (30).
