The schedule is built from nominal offsets, so run quantisation (a beat may publish up to five ticks late) never accumulates. `tier = 0` outside TELL and `1 + (e ≥ 600) + (e ≥ 1200)` inside, with `e = esc_ticks` (the whole floor window is tier 1 whatever the player does, because `esc_ticks` never exceeds real elapsed time), which is Lighting's tier-1 recipe (registry `gutter_recipe`: tier 1 480/960 frames, tier 2 from `TIER2_TICKS` 300/600, tier 3 from `TIER3_TICKS` 180/360, placeholders). Filament density in a lit region is a function of `tell_beat` (AV-7), which follows this schedule. **Two-channel criterion.** (auto, headless) every legal `(tell_len, seed)` and every legal level trajectory (including a clock frozen from the first run) publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` (4) beats in `[tell_start_T, tell_start_T + TELL_MIN_TICKS)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; (manual, screenshot plus audio capture) the presenter renders each beat in both senses; the audio channel is sufficient alone, and the visual channel is sufficient alone while an eligible emitter is lit (ART:954) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The lamp gutter is an optional **third** channel: Lighting's tier-1 spacing (480/960 frames) can give zero dips inside the 600-tick floor window (C2), so the contract does not lean on it; the shorter tier-2/3 spacing asked for in OQ-AT-7 now exists in the registry `gutter_recipe` as placeholder bounds. Attention publishes *facts*; Adaptive Audio & Tells owns every sound, mix and cue name (its requests are in Visual/Audio Requirements and Interactions as must-repeat). **Final-gap beat suppression is dropped (CQ-5, closed):** `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` constrains consecutive beats only, with no beat-to-breach gap, so the worst case for an organic tell in the floor window is five beats (offsets 0, 158, 307, 448, 580) against `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4, and the universal bound over every level trajectory (the clock frozen from the first run) is exactly four (0, 158, 316, 474; F-12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `BEAT_START_TICKS` 135 is recorded only as the fallback if the suppression ever returns. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), OQ-AT-25: escalation freezes while `L < theta_HOLD` (behaviour-keyed, Option A; the creative-director's call under coordination rule 3).** It replaces the elapsed-keyed text above, keeps the end unreadable (the clock keys on the player's own restraint, never on `tell_len`), changes AT-9, F-12, F-13, the persisted fields and the Audio tier layering (Adaptive Audio's contrary recommendation to reject freezing, OQ-AA-34, is superseded), and redoes the F-12 floor proof. The Feel AC AC-AT-125 still measures the share of first-time testers who stay still and reopens the ruling if it fails *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-20 to AC-AT-24, AC-AT-47, AC-AT-127 (the freeze), AC-AT-128 (the floor under any trajectory). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R11; ART:954, :980; user ruling OQ-AT-25.

**AT-10. Restraint aborts a tell.** *Rule.* During TELL, after each run's integration: if `L < theta_HOLD` then `below_hold_runs += 1`, else it resets to 0. The tell **aborts to CALM** when `below_hold_runs × 6 ≥ ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` (placeholder 120, run-quantised; the first run under the line counts as run 1), **or** at the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T` if `L < theta_HOLD` at that run, **or** at that same run if the departure void holds (`locus_vacated`, AT-22; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*). If both are true on the same run the abort wins (one transition). An abort deposits no STRESS, switches no beacon, sets `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, freezes `tell_beat`, leaves `L` unchanged (a knob `ABORT_RELIEF_PM` defaulting to 1000, which means "no relief" and means the abort is unchanged from the ruled behaviour, lets a tuner cap the level at an abort if playtests show chained-abort fatigue) and sets `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS` (placeholder 600). **The rearm keys on the item, not on the pickup** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: while a TELL runs, Attention records in `tell_carry_items` (persisted, AT-1) the `carry_ref` of every seat whose `BURDEN` push it drained (an ordered read of `controller.state`, the same read as `loco_state`, AT-31); after an abort the set is kept until `rearm_until_T`. On any CALM run before `rearm_until_T` that drains a `BURDEN` push from a seat whose `carry_ref` is in the set, `rearm_until_T` is set to that run's `T`, so dropping the cargo to abort and taking the same item up again grants no fresh `TELL_REARM_TICKS` immunity: the next crossing opens a tell at once. A different item leaves the rearm in place. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The test is the push, not the pickup: any CALM run before `rearm_until_T` that drains a `BURDEN` push from a seat whose `carry_ref` is in the set ends the rearm, whether the seat re-took the item or never put it down (a continuous hold). By the location gate of AT-3 the push is not drained `IN_POD` or aboard the home body, so a hauler who hops with the item is not charged here: the void sets `rearm_until_T = T_d + TELL_REARM_TICKS` (`T_d` the due run) and it holds through the pod leg and the home carry. The cancel can fire after a hop only if the seat is back aboard a non-home body still holding the item before `rearm_until_T`: that takes a round trip, 2 × (`HOP_LEG_TICKS` 900 + `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` 45) = 1,890 ticks from the hop, against a rearm that ends d + 600 ticks after the hop (d = ticks of the tell still to run at the hop), so it is reachable only when d > 1,890 − 600 = 1,290 (a hop in the first 510 ticks of an 1,800-tick tell); otherwise the rearm is whole. The narrower rule, that only a re-take cancels, is not adopted (creative-director lean). The set is cleared at TELL entry (and refilled during the tell), at BREACH entry (a breached tell sets no rearm) and when the rearm ends. A player who keeps being loud through the tell forfeits the abort; that is intended ("we chose to be loud"). Restraint always costs the partial (Formulas F-15b), and that price is deliberate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; it is balanced by a breach that has teeth (AT-11), so neither choice dominates. `tell_remaining` published to Save/Load is the time to `breach_due_T`: the conservative direction, because an abort only ends the block early. Two invariants, both lint: **(a) rearm against autosave:** `TELL_REARM_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` (cited), which guarantees an open soft-gate window between chained aborted tells even under STRICT mode. **(b) abortability at entry:** a player who enters TELL at the highest possible level (`L_CEIL`, AT-5) and then zeroes every player-controlled input (still, beam off, no verbs, any cargo set down) falls under `theta_HOLD` minus a margin `ATT_MARGIN` before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse, at the ring's survivable-dark floor. In words: the tell is a window of agency even at its shortest. The play-level variant, which adds a reaction allowance and evaluates against the shortest draw a player can actually meet, is the registered formula `abortability_play` (AT-8, CQ-4; Formulas F-15b).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-25, AC-AT-26, AC-AT-27, AC-AT-16, AC-AT-52. *Pillar.* P3 (restraint is real), P2 (a tell is a window of agency). *Decided by:* R10; Pillar 3 design test.

#### Group E — The breach and its locus

**AT-11. Breach entry actions (same run).** *Rule.* At the run that satisfies `T ≥ breach_due_T` with `L ≥ theta_HOLD`: `phase = BREACH`, `breach_enter_T = T`; record `locus_occupants_at_entry` = the set of living seats whose `actor_section` is in `locus_body` at this BREACH-entry run; M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, s, magnitude = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT)` for every section `s` of the locus body, in ascending `section_id`, at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` (placeholder 8) per run, continuing on following runs (`stress_pending_cursor`) until the body is covered; and `set_beacon(on)` submitted as a `SYSTEM` command at T+1 (CR-9 path b). The magnitude is cited by registry name and never as a number; passing that constant as `magnitude` also makes the Graph's M17 clamp reading (`stress_pm = clamp(magnitude, 1000, 3000)`) and its D.9 / LS-25 reading (`stress_pm = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`) agree (C15, a Graph wording refresh). **Footprint cap:** `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` bounds honoured footprints per Graph run; Attention takes at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` per run. **Attention has no first claim on the cap** (CP-C5): Horror's footprints arrive by **path (a)**: the published Class B mutators `deposit_trace` (M17) and `apply_suit_damage`, called from the `horror.step` hook, which is ordered `after` Attention (KRN Interactions row 9; Horror HE-28, C-HE-3), not as SYSTEM commands in P2; Horror submits no SYSTEM command at MVP. The Graph's "first `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` in arrival order within P2" (LSG:807) carries a P2 premise that path (a) does not satisfy, so Attention claims no first place in the cap. The guarantee is therefore a **Horror must-repeat**: its per-run footprint budget is at most `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`. The registry unit of `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` (revised 2026-10-27) says per run across all callers and subjects (global), the first 16 by arrival applied in ascending subject id and the rest dropped and counted, which matches LSG:727 (C22). A STRESS deposit the cap drops is not lost for good: the next burst run re-arms it (AT-12; an edge case below). **Attention's MVP breach is STRESS plus the beacon plus the published phase.** RUPTURE and GOUGE are Horror's; this keeps Attention fully testable without Horror. **Stance (the breach is a consequence amplifier, stated so it is a decision):** STRESS multiplies *endogenous* damage only ("the breach rhythm makes the ship *fail* faster, not *leak* faster", LSG D.9), so a breach costs the crew what they had deferred; on a clean ship it would be nearly a non-event. **Why this is not left as a stance (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN, applied):** each choice is defensible alone, but a toothless breach on a clean ship together with an abort that always forfeits the partial (AT-10, Moment A) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* makes *commit* the dominant choice. Restraint would always cost the partial, riding it out would cost almost nothing, P3's "restraint and stealth are real strategies" would be empty, the "chaos" half of P2 would have no chaos, and players would learn to ignore tells. Two requirements follow, and neither is a mitigation to be traded away. **(1) Content requirement (a Derelict Authoring must-repeat).** Every body that can be a locus on MVP derelicts A and B is authored with at least one active overload or fatigue fault (on derelict A, a fault that wakes when its bus is served: option (b), OQ-AT-24), so a derelict breach always has something to amplify (AC-AT-58 is the seeded-fault scenario, AC-AT-117 the authoring check). This fits P5: the outer ring is kept unsafe by wear. A body the player has repaired clean is the player's earned result and not a content failure, and a home-ship locus carries whatever the crew has left unrepaired. **(2) Horror Entities' single MVP behaviour wired to BREACH is a BLOCKING dependency** (OQ-AT-9, AC-AT-77), no longer an ask: a breach brings the thing the crew fears, not only a faster-failing hull. `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` is not raised to hide this. **Playtest tripwire:** the Moment-ordering guard (Player Fantasy, AC-AT-80) is named for exactly this failure: a 30-minute session with only breaches means commit has become dominant. **Re-evaluated against the designed Horror and Derelict Authoring** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the claim that STRESS alone leaves a derelict breach toothless was written before Horror existed. Horror now supplies crew-side teeth of its own: by HE-F16 a stationary seat in the locus body cannot be killed in a minimum-length breach (`BREACH_MIN_TICKS` 900 gives two REACH starts) but can be in any breach of at least 986 ticks and certainly in any of at least 1,156, and the REACH ladder (HE-F13, HE-F17) gives the breach a content beat. The breach therefore has two teeth: STRESS (ship-side, it amplifies deferred faults) and Horror's REACH (crew-side, it needs a seat that lingers); neither substitutes for the other, and a crew that leaves the body by pod is exposed to neither beyond the STRESS tail. Requirement (1) stays as the ship-side tooth. **Derelict A, option (b)** (creative-director recommendation; **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), OQ-AT-24: option (b)**, which preserves EM-24's count of 8 live faults; the dead-bus exemption (a) is not adopted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: its pass condition is a Graph subject authored with a fault that *wakes when its bus is served* (`j_a_main`, a dead SHORT that becomes live once fed, with `pump_a` on its branch), which keeps Emitter State's count of exactly 8 live faults (EM-24) and adds no ninth fault. **Story-readiness gate:** a story that builds AT-11, the breach ACs or Derelict Authoring's locus lint is not ready until AC-AT-58's tolerance is fixed with the Graph owner (OQ-AT-6); OQ-AT-24 is answered (user ruling, option (b)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-28, AC-AT-29, AC-AT-30, AC-AT-58 (seeded-fault scenario). *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by:* R12; LSG:196, :525, :906.

**AT-12. Breach duration and end.** *Rule.* During BREACH the level **keeps integrating** (the player's noise still matters), with `W_L[FAULT_GLOW] = W_L[BEACON] = 0` and the beacon's lamp watts never reaching the power terms (`bcn_main` is a LAMP node, so it sits inside `lamps_lit_w`, AT-4; the alarm cannot hold the level above `theta_HOLD` and delay the exit it is tested against) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. STRESS is re-armed in **bursts**: `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` (3) consecutive runs every `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS` (placeholder 300) runs, under the lint `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS + STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS < STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` (cited from LSG:907; registry `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`). A burst tolerates one dropped deposit because a deposit is idempotent. BREACH ends at the first of: (i) `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` (placeholder 3600, the death-spiral guard); (ii) after `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (placeholder 900), `L < theta_HOLD` for `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` (placeholder 300, run-quantised); (iii) pod-hop departure: the last living occupant recorded at BREACH entry (`locus_occupants_at_entry`) leaving a locus body that is not the home-ship (AT-22); (iv) **ADDITION X10 (APPROVED at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate: the combat anti-pillar explicitly permits decoys, and the lit derelict pays in STRESS on its own hardware; CR-LURE stays a playtest watch item with `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` as the lever):** if at BREACH entry the locus body is not the home-ship and `locus_occupants_at_entry` is empty (it holds no living player), at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (the retreat the departure clause models has already happened) (reachable at MVP only through a derelict that Web Map keeps loaded while berthed, WM-21 and WM-24, after the player has left it; a *departed* unberthed derelict goes dormant at once under LS-17's default, which Web Map adopts, so that form is fixture-only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*). **Breach length is Attention's, not `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`.** STRESS lapses on the Graph's own hold after the last deposit and may outlast SUBSIDING: "a long tell, a spreading wrongness, a short collapse, then silence" (LSG:986). No Attention surface shows that tail. **Must-repeat request to Adaptive Audio & Tells (R30):** `phase == CALM` is never mapped to an "all clear" cue, because the Graph's consequence sounds, not Attention, carry the ongoing wrongness; this is Audio's rule to repeat, not Attention's.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-31, AC-AT-32, AC-AT-33 (lint), AC-AT-34. *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by:* R12.

**AT-13. Subsiding and the refractory.** *Rule.* On SUBSIDING entry: `set_beacon(off)` is submitted; no further STRESS deposits are made; and the level is reset to a residual, not wiped: `L ← min(L, B + POST_PM × (theta − B) / 1000)` with `POST_PM` a placeholder of 400. SUBSIDING lasts `SUBSIDE_TICKS` (placeholder 1200) while integrating normally, then the machine returns to CALM with `refract_until_T = T + REFRACT_TICKS` (placeholder 1800) and `tell_outcome = BREACHED`. During the refractory no TELL may begin, but the level keeps integrating, so **the refractory is not free safe time**: a player still loud at its end tips straight into a new tell (bounded by `L_CEIL`). The refractory is a published, state-gated valley, not a hidden scheduler. `phase` changes only inside `attention.step`; `post_restore` changes no phase and submits nothing (AT-27), so no reader can observe a half state; CALM → BREACH, TELL → SUBSIDING and BREACH → TELL do not exist. The phase table, guards and who may cause each transition are in States and Transitions.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-35, AC-AT-36, AC-AT-60 (table-driven transition test). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R13; LSG:986.

**AT-14. Locus and locus body.** *Rule.* The **locus** is the section with the highest `ledger_mAU` at TELL entry; ties go to the lowest `section_id`. It is fixed for the epoch and falls back to the home-ship's lowest `section_id` only if the locus section is cut away or goes dormant *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and no departure void applies: the fallback never converts an escape into a breach on the ship the player escaped to (AT-22, E-23). **During a TELL the dormancy fallback is suppressed from the first run at which `locus_vacated` holds** (a non-home locus body, `tell_occupants_at_entry` non-empty, no living seat in `locus_body`): the departure apply makes the derelict dormant many runs before `breach_due_T`, and without this the fallback would re-point the locus at the home ship in between and the due-run void would then never see the recorded body. The locus stays pinned to the departed body for the epoch (`locus_section_id` and `locus_body` remain valid ids; its ledger entry is dropped per AT-2, which is harmless because the locus is fixed), and the void resolves at the due run. The fallback is taken during a TELL only for a cut, a berth release, or a dormancy with a living seat still in the body. Attribution: a PlayerId source maps to the section containing the actor's foot origin through Ship Body's `actor_section(actor_id)` (BDY-29, Section lookup for Suit; sibling in flight); an object or emitter source maps to `section_of` or the emitter's `section_id`; light and power map to their own section. The **locus body** is `sections_of(body_of(locus))` (Layout reads, LY state inventory and Body row), recorded at TELL entry as `locus_body`. STRESS covers the whole locus body: welded sections share the breach, so a welded derelict is not a safe harbour. An optional knob `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` (default 1000 = "leader only", conforming) lets a tuner prefer a section holding a living player if playtests show an unattended, powered derelict absorbing most breaches. Layout's pathing asks (`room_surface`, `floor_tile_at`, `room_adjacency`, LY Interactions row for Attention / Horror) are Horror's; Attention takes only the membership reads.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-37, AC-AT-59 (decoy knob default reproduces the rule). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R14.

#### Group F — The contract, the view, the truth channel

**AT-15. The tell-window contract: one predicate for all web-originated harm.** *Rule.* `exogenous_permitted(section) = phase ∈ {BREACH, SUBSIDING} AND section ∈ locus_body`. Every web-originated exogenous call (M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE and STRESS; environmental M18 `apply_damage(..., EXOGENOUS)`) must satisfy it. A `TellAudit` ledger (a DIAGNOSTIC subscriber and a **test fixture first**, written before Horror and Audio exist) records each such call as `(tick, caller, kind, section, tell_epoch, phase)`; it also records every AT-14 fallback as `LOCUS_FALLBACK(epoch, old_body, new_body, tick)`, and the replay evaluates `section_e` against the body in force at `t_e`; the replay asserts, for every harm event `e`, that some epoch `k` satisfies `tell_start_k + tell_len_k ≤ t_e < subside_end_k` with `tell_len_k ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS` and `section_e ∈ locus_body_k` (registry formula `tell_window_contract`). **Scope (R15, provisional):** TellAudit also records every lethal act by a horror on a crew body, because the Pillar 2 promise ("every breach is preceded by a 10–30 second tell") is a promise to the *player*, not to the hull; a lethal act outside a told breach is exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and Horror's stalk is additive, never a substitute. **Horror Entities must REPEAT this rule or surface a CONFLICT.** "Perceivable" is defined relative to **every living player**, not to the locus: the tell must be audible to all of them through the non-positional structure-borne bed, whose existence and listener scope are contractual (its dB margin is Audio's). The bed is what makes a tell on an unoccupied lit derelict fair. Player-originated exogenous acts (cut, vent) never need the tell (LSG:144: agency); the Graph's endogenous gate (`W_MIN_WARNING_S`) is not how this contract is enforced and neither replaces nor is replaced by it. **This rule discharges LSG:1063** ("every call site of M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE and STRESS and every environmental M18 `apply_damage(…, EXOGENOUS)` source is preceded by that system's own 10–30 s Pillar 2 tell, named in its GDD, and none relies on the Graph's gate for it").
*Criterion.* AC-AT-01 (first; discharges LSG:1063), AC-AT-01a, AC-AT-68 (the provisional crew-body scope). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R15; LSG:144, :1063.

**AT-16. The published read view.** *Rule.* `attention.view` is a SIM read view of integers, never `PERCEPTION_OUT` (AC-SL-95a, AC-SL-121). The field set is final (Save/Load and Audio restate these names):

| Field | Values | Reader and note |
|---|---|---|
| `phase` | CALM, TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING | Audio, Lighting, Presenter, Sanity, Horror. **Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase`** (lint) |
| `breach_phase` | CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING | **Save/Load only.** TELL is reported as CALM so Save/Load's FAIR mode stays live (C5); not an edit to Save/Load |
| `tell_active` | 0/1 | Save/Load |
| `tell_remaining` | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, 0 outside TELL | Save/Load, SIM readers (Horror) and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only; **never a PRESENTATION subscriber** |
| `tell_elapsed` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, 0 outside TELL; **the escalation clock** (`esc_ticks`, AT-9): equal to real elapsed time while the level stays at or above `theta_HOLD`, frozen while it is under; real elapsed time is `T − phase_enter_T` | Audio, Presenter, Lighting (readers that scale intensity or schedule tiers key on this or on `tier`, never on a clock of their own) |
| `tell_beat`, `tell_beat_T` | counter, tick | Audio, Presenter |
| `tier` | 0 to 3 | Lighting (`tier ≥ 1`), Audio, Sanity |
| `tell_outcome` | NONE, ABORTED, BREACHED | Audio, Sanity; NONE until the first tell ends, ABORTED from an abort until the next TELL entry, BREACHED from SUBSIDING end until the next TELL entry; survives load |
| `tell_epoch` | int, +1 at every TELL entry | all (replaces the brief's `breach_epoch`, because aborted tells have no breach) |
| `phase_enter_T`, `breach_enter_T` | ticks | Audio, Presenter |
| `level_pm` | 0 to 1000, baseline-relative: `min(1000, ⌊1000 × (L − B) / (theta − B)⌋)` | Presenter and Audio, **quantised only** (AT-26) |
| `locus_section_id` | id | Audio, Horror |
| `exogenous_permitted(section)` | predicate | Horror, Graph-side callers |

`tell_progress_pm` is **not published** (it would leak the end). Audio's dedupe key is the pair `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` compared lexicographically, because a new tell resets `tell_beat` to 0 while `tell_epoch` increases. Published-field table by phase is in States and Transitions. The Save/Load gate formula (`attention_tell_block`, `soft_block`) is owned by Save/Load; Attention owns only the three field *values* and their names. **The read view of Save/Load's SL-5 Soft (b) (answers Save/Load OQ-24):** the condition reads `attention.view` (SIM), and nothing else of Attention: the active tell is `tell_active` = 1, its remaining ticks are `tell_remaining` (compared by Save/Load against `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` under `gate_tell_mode` FAIR; STRICT defers for the whole tell), and the breach phase is `breach_phase` (CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING; TELL reports CALM, C5). **Pod-hop notification:** the name is Web Map & Transit's, not Attention's; Web Map WM-31 names `POD_DEPARTED` and `POD_ARRIVED`, which are presentation (Audio) events only and not a sim input; Attention reads departure and arrival from `web.state`'s read view (`pod_at`, `transit_state`, `leg_epoch`) in its hook (a hook owner never subscribes, CR-10), and Save/Load detects arrival by polling the monotonic `pod_arrived_seq` counter in the `web.state` read view (ADR-0006 1.12); it does not consume the notification *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)*. Attention needs neither event for correctness (AT-22 derives departure from Ship Body).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-38, AC-AT-39, AC-AT-40, AC-AT-66, AC-AT-67, AC-SL-121 (discharged). *Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by:* R16; SL:517, :528, :531.

**AT-17. The tell is a truth channel.** *Rule.* Nothing Attention publishes or presents is removed, added, delayed or altered by the Perception Filter or by Sanity. There is **no phantom tell and no false calm, in any tier and at any stage** (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN: rejected by pillar, permanent, not deferred): a false calm hides a real tell and produces exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and a phantom tell spends the player's restraint on a lie and voids `TellAudit`. The tell is a standing truth channel in every tier; Sanity keeps its own disjoint sound family and its own rim fringe (AV-17). The art bible's tell element "a needle that drifts on a *real* gauge" (ART 2.7) is **dropped from the tell (C1)**: no Graph or instrument value may take Attention as input (LSG:281), and a gauge moving with no physical cause would be a lie that Failure & Trail refuses to draw (FT-4); a real needle change caused by breach STRESS damage is a breach *consequence*, not a tell, and the wording amendment is routed to the art-director and In-World Instruments. Attention reads no sanity and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` value (CR-12).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-41, AC-AT-40. *Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by:* R17.

#### Group G — The world writes and the presenter

**AT-18. The beacon.** *Rule.* BEACON `gates_actions` is **false**: Attention concurred (the beacon sweeps the cone over a point for roughly a tenth of each sweep) and the user has ruled (Emitter D20, confirmed 2026-10-27, OQ-AT-2 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the registry's "provisional, user decision pending" mark on `emitter_kind` is cleared by the registry request, not here. The Emitter State's `set_beacon` `max_age_ticks` of **120 is confirmed by Attention** (EM asks for exactly that): a T+1 SYSTEM command with 20 runs of slack. **Which beacon:** per Derelict Authoring DA-24 *(`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`, status Designed since 2026-10-27; was In Design at authoring)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, exactly one BEACON exists in the world, on `home_ship` in `spine_corridor`, authored with `node_ref` = `bcn_main`, a dedicated LOAD node (`mod_lamp`, 20 W, DA-24; OQ-DA-9 closed 'no sharing') under the lint `BEACON_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT); derelicts A and B have none. DA-24 adds "Attention … may relocate it": **placement is Authoring's data row and is not Attention's** (refresh owed to DA, C20); Attention only submits `set_beacon` to the one beacon the data holds. Attention switches that beacon on at **every** BREACH entry regardless of locus, because it asserts a true global fact ("a breach is on"); the positional creak layer (AUD-4 a, in TELL) and STRESS carry *where*; *that* is carried by the beacon where it is in view and by Audio's alarm pulse on its aligned or fallback clock (the non-positional tell bed ended at BREACH entry, Audio AA-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. It switches off at SUBSIDING entry. `W_L[BEACON] = 0`. **The beacon is also a Graph LOAD** (its own LOAD node `bcn_main`, 20 W, DA-24), so switching it on adds draw and a `delta_w_last5` step on the home-ship. Attention needs no beacon-specific term *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: LS-19 defines `lamps_lit_w` as including any LAMP node such as `bcn_main`, so the beacon's 20 W draw and its switch-on step are inside the lamp subset that F-4 already removes, and the alarm cannot feed the level that the BREACH exit tests (C11 closed). `fx_cryo_lamp` (DA-23) is the one `node_ref` 0 fixture and is never touched by the tell. Under reduced flashing the beacon becomes a static mark (LO-26). LO-26 also names a HUD glyph, which contradicts ART:741 and is routed as C27, so the static mark and the audio carry the breach. A rejected `set_beacon` (`NOT_LIVE` when home-ship power is dead, `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_BEACON`) is logged once and retried once per run for `BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` (placeholder 20, equal to the command's max age); it never blocks the machine. Alarm cadence (one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`) is a must-repeat request to Adaptive Audio, not an Attention rule.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-42, AC-AT-29, AC-AT-63 (the beacon never reaches the power terms). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R18; EM:165, :200.

**AT-19. The suppressant: contract only.** *Rule.* The contract is stated and **no effect verb ships at MVP**: Attention reads `tool_charge(player_id, def_id)` (0..`charges_max`; `def_id` = `tool_suppressant`) through an ordered read and is never pushed to by Suit; the verb's registrant calls `consume_charge(player_id, def_id) -> ok`; and `OUT_OF_CHARGES` is the repeated read name (SE-14 and its open question 7, ruled CK-5). The effect is a Vertical Slice addendum. It is worded "repel or distract", never "fight" or "kill", and it may not bypass restraint (a suppressant that aborts a tell outright would make restraint an item). The index edge Attention → Suit is downgraded to "soft, none at MVP" (refresh owed, C14). Answer to IDB OQ-16: at MVP the suppressant has no Attention effect, full or empty; any MVP effect is Horror Entities' (repel or distract, never touching the level or a tell). Horror ships none (HE-39). **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), X6 Option A (Attention OQ-AT-5, Horror OQ 3, Suit OQ-34, Item DB OQ 29):** `tool_suppressant` is **removed from the MVP start kit** (Suit SE-11 slot 4, SE-14, SE-15, the Item DB MVP list and the HUD), so no inert tool reaches the player; HE-39 repel stays a dormant `[CUT-FIRST]` interface, AC-HE-124 is BLOCKING and IDB AC-109 passes. Attention agreed all along, because an inert tool is a Pillar 1 lie, and the contract names above are kept only for the Vertical Slice addendum. This document cites no `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` value (the registry retirement belongs to Suit and the Item DB) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The contract names above stand for the Vertical Slice addendum, and Attention declares `after: suit.vitals` only if a future revision reads Suit state (none at MVP).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-43. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R19; IA:133, SE:201, IDB:249.

**AT-20. The Attention Presenter (the world tells).** *Rule.* A PRESENTATION subscriber owned by this GDD draws, at MVP: (a) rim **filaments only inside a lit region of a player-controllable real emitter** (the gate widening, below; unlit space draws none: Principle 1, ART:302), and (b) a **dust-direction change in the beam**. **Hull-stress hairlines are cut from MVP** and routed to the art-director: they read too easily as the Integrity trail (ART:312, :565) and risk Pillar 1. Filaments are web pieces in the Y-sort layer (rotation permitted for web and horror pieces, PIPE row 16); colour is the web's iridescence only (teal → green → bronze → gold, ART:1371), never flesh or violet. All presentation randomness is a pure integer hash, never `rng_derive` (the Lighting precedent, LO-19). The camera does nothing (PIPE:151); the beam, ambient light, camera and controls do not change in any phase (ART 2.7, PCI:135). **"Lit region" (gate ruling CD-GDD-ALIGN, OQ-AT-3 closed).** A strand resolves at the edge of the lit region of a **player-controllable real emitter**: the eligible kinds are exactly `BEAM` (the cone), `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN` and `DEPLOYABLE` (a pool, with a rim band of `RIM_BAND_PM` of the emitter's reach), the kinds of the registry's closed `emitter_kind` list that the player can switch, power, carry or place. **Excluded:** `BEACON` (the alarm lens is not a tell carrier), the cryo EMERGENCY lamp `fx_cryo_lamp` (FIXTURE subkind EMERGENCY, self-powered; keeps LO-20, "never a tell carrier"), `FAULT_GLOW` (a latent fault's glow is a consequence, not a tell) and the **DOCK_STATUS** glow of the berthed pod's dock room (FIXTURE subkind DOCK_STATUS, X2: self-powered and switched by SYSTEM only, so not a player-controllable emitter; it is light and an unswitchable one, AT-6, but a site lit only by it is not eligible) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Why: with cone-only, a player whose chest lamp is off while their own fixtures burn would get no visual channel for a tell their own fixtures caused, which contradicts P3's lesson that the web sees the ship, not the body (Moment C), and P2's "a careful player could have perceived" for a deaf player. **Principle 1 is unchanged: a strand is never drawn in unlit space**, a site lit only by an excluded emitter is not eligible, and strands appear only in the **true** lit region, never in a falsely extended reach. **Producer scope fallback:** cone-only (BEAM regions alone) remains allowed if the producer must cut scope for MVP; it is recorded as scope and not as creative direction, and the deaf-player channel then reduces to the beam. In co-op, filaments resolve in every lit region, each beat shared (SHARED_SUMMARY).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-44, AC-AT-45, AC-AT-53. *Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by:* R20; PIPE:305.

#### Group H — Seams, determinism, scope

**AT-21. The arming seam (test and onboarding).** *Rule.* `attention_arm_tell {tell_len}` has `allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`, no player binding and no PLAYER origin. It is valid only in CALM (otherwise rejected `NOT_CALM`); `tell_len` is clamped to `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]`; the tell then follows every rule of AT-8 to AT-13. It serves test fixtures and the Vertical Slice's hand-led onboarding script and is listed in the control manifest as non-gameplay. It is not a cheat: the floor still binds.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-19. *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R21.

**AT-22. Dormancy and pod hop *(Web Map & Transit conformed 2026-10-28: WM-26, WM-26b (1), AC-WM-105 and AC-WM-122 now state the void; C28 and OQ-AT-35 closed)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Rule.* Dormant sections give no stimuli and hold no ledger. `IN_POD` holds the departure ring (AT-6); a hop between two ring-1 loci (the MVP's only kind, HOME and a ring-1 derelict) holds `held_ring` 1 throughout, so `B` does not change (Web Map ASK-A1). The pod's running light is cosmetic presentation, not authored as an emitter (WM-26): it is not an eligible tell region (AT-20) and not a light stimulus (AT-4). The DOCK_STATUS glow on the docked pod's dock-room prop is a different object: it is a real emitter (Emitter EM-3), so it is a light stimulus (F-3) and an unswitchable one (AT-6, F-16), but never a tell carrier (AT-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. A pod hop does **not** interrupt a TELL (the clock, the bed and the beats run through the hop), but it can **void the due-run breach** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(creative-director ruling on OQ-AT-12; **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): the void is confirmed**)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: at TELL entry the living seats inside `locus_body` are recorded as `tell_occupants_at_entry`. At the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T`, if the locus body is **not** the home-ship body, `tell_occupants_at_entry` is non-empty and **no living seat's `actor_section` is in the recorded `locus_body`** (everyone who was in it has left, by pod or otherwise; a recorded seat that died inside the body or whose position cannot be resolved still counts as present, and a seat in the pod counts as gone, the `occ_gone` rule of F-14 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), the tell ends as an abort: TELL → CALM, `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, no STRESS, no beacon, `rearm_until_T` set, the release cue plays (`locus_vacated`, F-14 third clause). The test runs against the recorded `locus_body` **before** any dormancy fallback, and during the TELL the fallback is suppressed from the first run at which `locus_vacated` holds (AT-14), so a departed, dormant derelict is never turned into a breach on the home ship the player just escaped to, neither at the due run nor on any run between the hop and it. Reason: a fallback that punishes the hop contradicts the Player Fantasy ('leave, and the web lets go'), punishes the game's natural escape verb in a lethal-breach game (Horror HE-F16) and teaches players not to flee during a tell, against Pillar 3's 'a price chosen with full warning'. If a living seat stays in the locus body the breach proceeds on that body unchanged (co-op: the staying seat faces it; the departing seat is safe unless the body is welded to theirs). A home-ship locus is not voidable: there is no escape by pod from a breach centred on the home ship (Moment B variant). **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), OQ-AT-12 (2):** a home-ship-locus tell is NOT escapable by hopping to a derelict; 'the lights you left on' is the price *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Reply to Web Map ASK-A4 (WM-26b, OQ-WM-42): declined** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: Attention does not hold the TELL-to-STRESS transfer while no seat is aboard the home body; a home-ship-locus breach proceeds as ruled, because holding it until someone returns would turn the hop into a way to defer a home breach, which the ruling above forbids. Web Map closed ASK-A4 as DECLINED and withdrew ASK-G5 on 2026-10-28 (WM-26b, delegated ruling R21) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. With the void, a hop away from a non-home locus never moves STRESS onto the home ship, so WM-26's former sentence 'the due-run breach STRESSes the home ship' described behaviour this document no longer has, and Web Map removed it on 2026-10-28; the home-ship locus with nobody aboard is E-73. A locus body that held no living seat at TELL entry (a lit, unoccupied, berthed derelict, WM-21) has nothing to void and breaches as before, ending under X10. BREACH ends when the last living occupant recorded at BREACH entry (`locus_occupants_at_entry`) leaves a locus body that is not the home-ship; a player who was never inside does not trigger it, and while any recorded occupant is still inside the clause is false. **Departure is derivable now** from Ship Body's `actor_section(actor_id)`: `locus_occupants_at_entry` is non-empty and no living seat's `actor_section` is in the recorded `locus_body` (a set of section ids), equivalently `body_of_actor(actor_id) ≠ body_of(locus_section_id)` for every living seat, plus the persisted `occ_gone` bits for recorded seats that are dead or cannot be resolved (F-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, which makes the rule testable before Web Map exists; Web Map's pod-hop departure is read from `web.state`'s view (`pod_at` / `transit_state` / `leg_epoch`) in the Attention hook; `POD_DEPARTED` (WM-31) is a presentation (Audio) event only, and Save/Load polls `pod_arrived_seq` instead of subscribing *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)*. If the locus section is cut away or goes dormant mid-event, the locus falls back per AT-14 and the locus body is recomputed (never as the consequence of a departure, which the void above resolves first); STRESS already armed on a cut-away section lapses on its own hold. **A death in the pod** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling D60, 2026-10-27)*: statuses run in the pod (Suit's air, cell, thermal and counters, and the Sanity and Attention clocks, all keep running) and a seat that dies there is the Controller's `DEAD`; Attention reads that through `loco_state` (AT-31), so a seat in the pod is living until then, a dead one stops counting for `B` and for the pod hold, and if it was the last recorded occupant of a non-home locus its section had already left `locus_body`, so the departure clauses already hold. **Ordering in BREACH:** the departure test above is evaluated against the recorded `locus_body` before any dormancy fallback in the same run, so a pod departure that empties a non-home locus ends the breach and no fallback occurs (WM:87, Moment 2; E-27).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-46, AC-AT-133 (death in the pod). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R22; creative-director ruling on OQ-AT-12 (2026-10-27 design review), confirmed as a user ruling; D60.

**AT-23. Co-op seams.** *Rule.* The `source` PlayerId tag is kept; one machine on one host; `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`. Whether a downed crewmate's beam or a corpse is a lantern is noted only (EM open question). There is no per-player balance at MVP. *Living* means `loco_state ≠ DEAD`; a DOWNED seat counts as living (AT-31).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-55. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R23.

**AT-24. Accessibility seams.** *Rule.* Reduce-motion: filament motion becomes a density change. Reduce-flashing: the gutter keeps its spacing (it passes by construction) and the beacon becomes a static mark (LO-26); reduce flashing defaults ON at first launch (user ruling D79), so the beacon is the static mark until the player turns it off *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. LO-26 also names a HUD glyph, which contradicts ART:741 and is routed as C27, so the static mark and the audio carry the breach; no glyph is drawn. **No captions are published for tell beats and no caption field exists**: a caption is an Attention indicator in effect (ART:741, :954); the generic "[creaking]" caption was weighed and not chosen, and no caption data is designed. **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), OQ-AT-1:** the deaf or muted player in the dark gets an **opt-in haptic pulse on beats**, off by default, because it carries the same information as the audio beat and so is not an indicator *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Contract: a PRESENTATION subscriber fires exactly one pulse per `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` change, on the same notification as the audio request (so audio-first holds, AUD-2), at one fixed amplitude and duration; the pulse is never keyed to `level_pm`, `tier`, the cue step, the phase or anything but the beat; there is no pulse in CALM, none for pre-tell creaks, none for the alarm and none for the abort release. It is a setting under Display & Comfort (default off) whose wording says it repeats the beat and is not a meter, and it needs ART:741 and Adaptive Audio's AA-42 to admit it (a request to both; haptics driven only by the player's own true body cues, the Suit's, are not Attention indicators under the standing user decision, and Audio records that). With nothing lit there is still no visual channel (ART:954 accepts this); the audio channel is sufficient alone, for a deaf player with any eligible emitter lit (the beam, a fixture, a lantern or a glowstick) the visual channel is sufficient alone, and for a deaf or muted player in the dark the opt-in pulse is the channel. `design/accessibility-requirements.md` does not exist yet and is to be authored later with `/ux-design` accessibility; this paragraph is the Attention input to it.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-47, AC-AT-39, AC-AT-103, AC-AT-129 (the haptic pulse). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R24; user ruling OQ-AT-1.

**AT-25. One-way edges and the only writes.** *Rule.* Attention **reads only**: the Emitter State (`emitters()`), the Graph (`power_use`), Ship Body (`actor_section`, `body_of_actor`) and Layout (`body_of`, `sections_of`) lookups, the Controller's `loco_state` and `carry_ref` (AT-31; `carry_ref` for the item-keyed rearm only, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*) and Web Map's ring (provisional). It reads nothing from Lighting, Audio, Sanity, Horror, the Perception Filter, HUD, Instruments, Failure & Trail, Camera or Save/Load. It **writes the world only through two doors**: M17 STRESS and `set_beacon`. Lighting reads only the tier and timing (LO-20 supersedes the index's "Lighting never reads Attention", C12). There are lints in both directions: no Graph, Controller, Suit, Interaction-verb data, camera, Failure & Trail or Item DB code reads an Attention symbol (LSG:281, PCI:1010, SE-25, IA-4, PIPE:151, FT-4, IDB:473). The two soft cycles (Attention ↔ Lighting, Attention ↔ Horror) are therefore resolved one-way.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-48, AC-AT-09. *Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided by:* R26.

**AT-26. Pre-tell presentation in CALM is quantised and floor-gated.** *Rule.* `level_pm` may drive only **quantised, floor-gated, diegetic density**: ordinary hull-settling creaks and the count of cone-edge filaments, in `CUE_STEPS` (4) steps above `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` (800, **derived** by the calibration lint below and recorded in Tuning Knobs; the seed value 350 failed that lint, and the 2026-10-26 value 550 failed the recalibrated lint: it left step 1 on permanently during ordinary exploration with the home ship lit, C23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, with a hysteresis `CUE_HYST_PM` (25) on the way down. **No continuous mapping of `level_pm` to any visible or audible parameter may exist**, because it would be an Attention meter by eye or by ear (ART:741, :64). **The ladder runs in CALM only** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: while `phase ≠ CALM` the step is forced to 0 and no pre-tell cue is requested, so a falling ladder can never become a live readout of abort progress during a TELL (guard rail 7; the creative-director accepted the semi-indicator for CALM only); after an abort or a SUBSIDING end the step re-arms from 0 and climbs again only across a rise edge, so pre-tell creaks never replay straight after the release cue (F-24). Pre-tell cues use event ids distinct from tell beats and are excluded from `TellAudit` and from the floor-window count. **Calibration lint (adopted from the audio-director and game-designer, values in Tuning Knobs):** the floor must exceed the steady `level_pm` of a player walking with the beam on **while the home ship is lit and the two wake glows burn** (the ordinary exploration state) by a margin `CUE_MARGIN_PM`, checked per ring *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; otherwise hull creaks would play whenever the player walks and the tell's onset would blur into pre-tell noise. The computation is in Formulas (F-24): that state settles at `B + (1000 + 288 + 766 + 72) × TAU_ATT_TICKS` mAU, which is 4,127 AU at ring 1 (671 pm), 689 pm at ring 2 and 702 pm at ring 3, so the floor is the MVP-active ring's value plus `CUE_MARGIN_PM` (100), rounded up to the next 50: 800; the ring-3 test row would need 850 and is flagged `SWITCHES`. **Must-repeat request to Adaptive Audio & Tells (R30):** no music change precedes beat 0; adaptive music must not telegraph the tell earlier than the contract's start tick. That is Audio's rule to repeat, not Attention's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The creative-director accepts that quantised pre-tell creaks are a **semi-indicator for hearing players** (a coarse, floor-gated, four-step reading of the level); recorded here, in OQ-AT-1, and tested by the creak-versus-beat listening AC-AT-121.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-49a to AC-AT-49c. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R27; Audio AA-22 (the pre-tell cue rule).

**AT-27. Persistence and restore.** *Rule.* `post_restore` rebuilds caches (section-id resolution, the sorted section arrays) and **nothing else**: it changes no `phase` and submits no command, because hooks receive `submit_system()` (KRN:593, :609) and a SYSTEM command pending at snapshot time is dropped on reload. The **beacon is reconciled in the first `attention.step` run after a restore**: the run compares `beacon_commanded` (persisted) with the emitter's switch state and submits `set_beacon` **only on a mismatch** (`phase == BREACH` and the beacon off: submit `on`; `phase != BREACH` and it on: submit `off`); no duplicate is submitted when the two already match, and a restore-time mismatch obeys the same `BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` retry as any rejection (AT-18). Cues fire on **transitions only**: loading a save made in any phase triggers no onset, no breach-enter and no release cue. The death stub restores from the newest verified autosave (SL-7) and Attention rolls back with it (WORLD scope); "Attention restores exactly; no meter is stored for display". A restore mid-TELL restores `rng_counter`, `tell_len`, `breach_due_T`, `tell_beat`, `esc_ticks`, `beat_next_off` and `below_hold_runs`, so no length is redrawn and the sequence is hash-identical; a draw made after a restore takes the new `restore_epoch` key (AT-8), so a death restore does not replay the next tell's length *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Restore coherence: `rng_counter ≤ tell_epoch` (an armed tell increments the epoch but not the counter); a block violating it fails with `ATTENTION_RESTORE_RNG_COUNTER` (AC-AT-70).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-22, AC-AT-23, AC-AT-50, AC-AT-65 (beacon reconciled by the first post-restore run; `post_restore` submits nothing). *Pillar.* P5. *Decided by:* SL-7, SL-8, SL-15; R1.

**AT-28. Determinism and RNG discipline.** *Rule.* Integer-only sim. The only RNG draw is the tell draw, `draw_epoch(attention.tell, rng_counter)` through the kernel's `RngFacet` (keyed per restore epoch inside the facet, AT-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Beat jitter and every presentation draw are pure integer hashes, `Ihash.h32(HASH_SEED, tell_epoch, k, 0, SALT_BEAT)` for the jitter, with `HASH_SEED` a fixed non-zero constant and `Ihash` the shared pure-integer hash of ADR-0005 section 2.6 (the former 'hash is an ADR candidate shared with Lighting (LO-19)' note is closed) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*. All sums are 64-bit and bounded; Attention uses sim `T` only, never wall time. A paused game stops `T` and so stops Attention.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-22, AC-AT-12. *Pillar.* kernel G3, G4.

**AT-29. Event-defer coupling to Save/Load.** *Rule.* One event never forces a RISKY autosave: `TELL_MAX_TICKS + BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS < AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (all cited or from knobs; registry formula `event_defer_invariant`), and the CALM gap between chained events is at least one retry (`REFRACT_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`, `TELL_REARM_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`). Re-check across every `gate_tell_mode` (FAIR and STRICT) whenever any term changes. Placeholders: 1800 + 3600 + 1200 + 300 = 6900, below the cited cap; the lint therefore binds `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` from above. Save/Load's ruled third soft-gate input (a bound seat at `vitals_state ≥ CRITICAL`, capped and then RISKY, user ruling D14) is a deferral cause that shares the same cap, so it adds no term and the inequality is unchanged (reading its cap as `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` is Save/Load's to confirm); the withheld Assembly-cut event autosave (D54) touches none of Attention's phases *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-51, AC-AT-52. *Pillar.* P5. *Decided by:* R12; SL:190-193.

**AT-30. MVP scope.** *Rule.* MVP = one threshold, one tier of lamp tell, one locus rule, one breach content (STRESS plus beacon), one noise-only push API. **Vertical Slice addenda, not absorbed:** multiple tiers, per-ring thresholds, ring baselines beyond ring 1 and the per-ring stimulus gain (a pillar-flagged requirement, AT-6), the suppressant verb, per-player attribution, rate-coupled tell length, co-op scaling, Knowledge Codex recognition of a decoded tell, and a scripted onboarding tell. Phantom tells and false calm are not on this list: they are rejected by pillar (AT-17), permanently.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-61 (review). *Pillar.* all.

**AT-31. Seat state read (added by the author stage; flagged).** *Rule.* "Living player" (AT-6, AT-23) and the pod hold (AT-6, AT-22) need the Controller's `loco_state` per bound seat: a seat is *living* if `loco_state ≠ DEAD` (DOWNED counts as living) and *in pod* if `loco_state == IN_POD`; a seat in the pod can die there (user ruling D60, AT-22) and is living until `loco_state` reads DEAD *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. This is an ordered read of the Controller's `controller.state` (no `after` needed: `controller.step` precedes the hook transitively); the same read also takes each seat's `carry_ref` for the item-keyed rearm of AT-10 (it is never a stimulus: the burden still arrives only as the pushed class) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*; it is **added to the read list of AT-25**, which the rulings' one-way list omitted. It creates no cycle (nothing in the Controller reads Attention, PCI:1010). The Controller owner is asked to repeat that `loco_state` is a published SIM read.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-55. *Pillar.* P3.

**AT-32. Diagnostics are inert.** *Rule.* A DIAGNOSTIC subscriber records, per run and per session, the counts of TELL entries, aborts and breaches, with every abort classified by its cause, **restraint** (the hold clause or the at-due level clause) or **void** (the departure void, AT-22), so a void never reads as restraint *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; the published `tell_outcome` stays ABORTED for both, AT-16 is unchanged)*, ticks in each phase, the per-channel contribution (noise, light, power) of `I_run` over the ticks preceding each TELL entry, abort latency, and locus occupancy at breach. These values are never read by sim code, never published in `attention.view`, never shown to the player and are excluded from `state_hash`. They exist so a designer can answer "what crossed the line" without a meter in the game. (ADR candidate: diagnostics pipeline and export format.)
*Criterion.* AC-AT-56 (hash identical with diagnostics on and off). *Pillar.* P3.

### States and Transitions

*The machine has four states. Every transition is evaluated inside `attention.step`, after the run's integration (AT-5) and in the order below; `post_restore` changes no `phase` and submits no command (the beacon is reconciled in the first `attention.step` run after a restore, AT-27). "Who may cause it" lists the only causes: no PLAYER-origin command, no other system's mutator and no timer outside the block can move the machine, apart from the SYSTEM-origin `attention_arm_tell` seam.*

#### State table

| State | Entry condition (checked in the run, after integrating) | Entry actions | While in state | Exit condition and next | Who may cause it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CALM** (initial; `default_state`: `phase` CALM, `L = B`, all timers 0, `tell_outcome` NONE, counters 0) | boot; TELL abort; SUBSIDING end | after an abort: `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS`. After SUBSIDING: `tell_outcome = BREACHED`, `refract_until_T = T + REFRACT_TICKS` | integrate (AT-5); publish `level_pm`; pre-tell cues quantised (AT-26); `tell_active` 0 | F-9 true (AT-7: `L ≥ theta`, past both timers) → TELL | the level (stimuli) crossing theta; or `attention_arm_tell` (SYSTEM) |
| **TELL** | entry guard of AT-7, or `attention_arm_tell` | `tell_epoch += 1`; `tell_start_T = T`; `tell_len` drawn (AT-8); `breach_due_T = T + tell_len`; locus and `locus_body` fixed (AT-14); `tell_occupants_at_entry` recorded (AT-22); `tell_beat = 0` **published in this run**; `tier = 1`; `below_hold_runs = 0`; `esc_ticks = 0` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `tell_outcome = NONE` (the AT-16 row: ABORTED or BREACHED last only until the next TELL entry); `occ_gone` all false; `tell_carry_items` cleared *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | integrate; beats on the behaviour-keyed schedule (AT-9); `esc_ticks += 6` on each run with `L' ≥ theta_HOLD`, frozen otherwise; tier by `esc_ticks` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `tell_remaining = max(0, breach_due_T − T)`; abort counter (AT-10) | abort rule (AT-10) → CALM; at the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T`: the departure void (`locus_vacated`, AT-22) or `L < theta_HOLD` → CALM (abort), else → BREACH | the level and the clock; for the void, the departure of every recorded occupant of a non-home locus body. A pod hop does not interrupt the tell itself *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **BREACH** | the TELL due run with `L ≥ theta_HOLD` | AT-11: STRESS deposits for the locus body, `set_beacon(on)` at T+1; `breach_enter_T = T`; `clear_runs = 0`; record `locus_occupants_at_entry` = the set of living seats whose `actor_section` is in `locus_body` at the BREACH-entry run; `occ_gone` reset to all false over that set; `tell_carry_items` cleared *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | integrate with `W_L[FAULT_GLOW] = W_L[BEACON] = 0`; STRESS bursts (AT-12); `exogenous_permitted` true for the locus body | the first of `BREACH_MAX_TICKS`; `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` then quiet for `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS`; pod-hop departure (the last living occupant recorded at BREACH entry leaving a non-home locus body); empty non-home locus body at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` → SUBSIDING | the level, the clock, and (for departure) the last living occupant recorded at BREACH entry leaving the recorded `locus_body` |
| **SUBSIDING** | BREACH end | `set_beacon(off)`; stop deposits; `L ← min(L, B + POST_PM × (theta − B) / 1000)`; `subside_end_T = T + SUBSIDE_TICKS` | integrate normally; `exogenous_permitted` stays true for the locus body | `T ≥ subside_end_T` → CALM | the clock only |

**Illegal or ignored transitions.** CALM → BREACH (none); TELL → SUBSIDING (none); BREACH → TELL (none); `attention_arm_tell` outside CALM is rejected `NOT_CALM`. A second crossing of theta in the same run (two LOUD pushes in co-op) produces one TELL, one `tell_epoch` increment and one RNG draw.

#### Transition guards

| Transition | Guard | Actions |
|---|---|---|
| CALM → TELL | `phase == CALM AND L ≥ theta AND T ≥ refract_until_T AND T ≥ rearm_until_T` (inclusive) | AT-7, AT-8, AT-9 entry; no catch-up elapsed time is credited if the level sat above theta during a timer |
| TELL → CALM (abort, early) | `below_hold_runs × 6 ≥ ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | `tell_outcome = ABORTED`; no STRESS; no beacon; `L` unchanged (or `ABORT_RELIEF_PM`, default no relief); `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS` |
| TELL → CALM (abort, at due) | `T ≥ breach_due_T AND (L < theta_HOLD OR locus_vacated)`; `locus_vacated` = the locus body is not the home-ship body, `tell_occupants_at_entry` is non-empty, no living seat's `actor_section` is in `locus_body` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and every recorded seat has `occ_gone` set (F-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | same as above; wins over a simultaneous BREACH decision |
| TELL → BREACH | `T ≥ breach_due_T AND L ≥ theta_HOLD AND NOT locus_vacated` | AT-11 |
| BREACH → SUBSIDING | AT-12 (i) to (iv) | AT-13 entry |
| SUBSIDING → CALM | `T ≥ subside_end_T` | `tell_outcome = BREACHED`; `refract_until_T = T + REFRACT_TICKS` |

#### Published fields by state

| Field | CALM | TELL | BREACH | SUBSIDING |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_active` | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| `breach_phase` (Save/Load only) | CALM | **CALM** | BREACH | SUBSIDING |
| `tell_remaining` | 0 | `tell_len` down to 0 (non-increasing within an epoch; the field's range is 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) | 0 | 0 |
| `tell_elapsed` (the escalation clock, `esc_ticks`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 0 | 0 up, frozen while `L < theta_HOLD` | 0 | 0 |
| `tier` | 0 | 1 to 3 | 0 | 0 |
| `tell_beat` | frozen | counts up from 0 | frozen | frozen |
| `exogenous_permitted(locus body)` | false | false | **true** | **true** |
| beacon (commanded) | off | off | **on** (home-ship's single beacon) | off |
| STRESS deposits | none | none | at entry and in bursts | none (existing arming lapses on the Graph's hold) |
| `W_L[FAULT_GLOW]` | 1000 | 1000 | **0** | **0** |

#### Persisted timers and counters (all in `attention.state`; see AT-1)

| Item | Set at | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `tell_start_T`, `tell_len`, `breach_due_T` | TELL entry | the seeded, fixed window; `tell_len ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS` on every path |
| `rng_counter` | each TELL draw | the only RNG draw (`draw_epoch(attention.tell, rng_counter)`, ADR-0005) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*; restores with the block (no reroll) |
| `below_hold_runs` | each TELL run | consecutive runs under theta_HOLD |
| `esc_ticks` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | each TELL run | the escalation clock: 0 at entry, +6 per run at or above the hold line, frozen under it; published as `tell_elapsed`; keys the beat interval and the tier (AT-9, F-12, F-13) |
| `pending_noise_mAU`, `pending_ledger_mAU[s]` | each accepted push; zeroed at step (1) of the next run | noise since the last run: the scalar feeds `I_noise` whether or not the source could be placed, the per-section entries (loaded sections only) feed the ledger; both clamped at `L_CEIL` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `nl_hist_w[s]` | each run; seeded flat when a section loads, wakes or is restored without an entry | the last five runs' non-lamp draw per loaded section, for the lamp-excluded transient of F-4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `tell_occupants_at_entry` | TELL entry | the living seats inside `locus_body` at TELL entry; the reference set for the departure void (AT-22, F-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `occ_gone` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | TELL entry and BREACH entry (all false), then each TELL and BREACH run | one bit per recorded seat: whether it has left `locus_body`; a dead or unresolved seat keeps its last bit, a seat in the pod is gone (F-14) |
| `pending_burden` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | each accepted `BURDEN` push; cleared at step (1) of the next run | a salvage carry was held since the last run, so this run's decay is 0 (F-5) |
| `tell_carry_items` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | each TELL run that drains a `BURDEN` push; cleared at TELL entry, at BREACH entry and when the rearm ends | the salvage items carried in the tell; any CALM run before `rearm_until_T` that drains a `BURDEN` push from a seat whose `carry_ref` is in the set ends the rearm: a re-take, or a continued hold carried back aboard a non-home body (AT-10; none in the pod or at home, AT-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `rearm_until_T` | abort | no new TELL before this tick (ended early by a re-taken salvage item, AT-10) |
| `breach_enter_T`, `clear_runs`, `stress_next_burst_run`, `stress_pending_cursor` | BREACH | duration, quiet counter, STRESS schedule |
| `locus_occupants_at_entry` | BREACH entry | the set of living seats whose `actor_section` was in `locus_body` at the BREACH-entry run; the reference set for the pod-hop clause (AT-12 iii, F-17) and the X10 clause (AT-12 iv); persisted (AT-1) so a restore mid-BREACH keeps both clauses |
| `subside_end_T`, `refract_until_T` | SUBSIDING, SUBSIDING end | valley timers |
| `tell_beat`, `beat_next_off` | each beat | beat counter and the nominal offset of the next beat |
| `locus_section_id`, `locus_body`, `held_ring`, `beacon_commanded`, `beacon_retry_left` | TELL entry, pod hop, BREACH entry/exit, a rejected `set_beacon` | scoping and the beacon request state; `beacon_retry_left` counts down from `BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` and is what the first post-restore run reconciles against (AT-27) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Data flow is from Attention's perspective ("in" = to Attention, "out" = from Attention). Where a counterpart GDD exists its own Interactions or Dependencies row for Attention is **quoted** (section id, then the line on disk on 2026-10-26) so the two documents can be checked against each other (bidirectional consistency). Rows about a system whose GDD is absent are **provisional** and end with what that GDD **must repeat**. This GDD never edits another document: every wording drift is routed as a "refresh owed" in Cross-References (C1 to C40 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*).*

| System | Data in | Data out | Interface owner and exact names | Counterpart's text (quoted) | Must repeat / refresh owed |

**What each dependent must repeat in its own GDD** (so the dependency is bidirectional, per the design-docs rule): the **Controller** pushes a class and repeats Body's timing text; **Interaction** repeats IR-1 to IR-3 as accepted; the **Graph** repeats that Attention is the caller of M17 STRESS at the breach-rhythm transition (any Horror STRESS lands inside the same `exogenous_permitted` predicate) and defines `power_use`'s fields; the **Emitter State** repeats the confirmed `max_age_ticks` and records the user's confirmation that `gates_actions` is false (Emitter D20, 2026-10-27); **Lighting** repeats the four must-repeats above; **Save/Load** repeats the exact view names; **Horror**, **Audio**, **Sanity**, **Web Map** and **Assembly** repeat the rows above.

## Formulas

*Conventions (read first; every formula below uses them).* **Units.** The level is stored in **mAU** (64-bit integer); 1 AU = 1000 mAU = the noise of one walking tick (the Controller-owned registry row `NOISE_PER_TICK`, cited; its walk value defines the unit). `theta = ATT_THRESHOLD × 1000` mAU; `theta_HOLD = theta × HOLD_PM / 1000`; `L_CEIL = theta × L_CEIL_PM / 1000`; `TAU_ATT_TICKS` is written `tau` where space is short. All durations are ticks at `TICK_RATE`; a *run* is one `attention.step` execution at `T` a multiple of 6. **Integer rule (kernel determinism).** Everything that is hashed, persisted or replicated is integer: `floor()` is applied to non-negative operands only, `ceil(a / b)` for `a ≥ 0` is written `(a + b − 1) div b`, no float is used in the sim, and a float appears only in the closed-form *oracles* of the tests. **Weights** `W_L`, `W_P`, `W_S` are in micro-AU per unit per tick (1000 = 1 mAU per unit per tick), so `× 6 / 1000` turns a per-tick weight into mAU per run in one dimension (CQ-7: the systems-designer's single dimension; the game-designer's felt-target table is rationale and the playtest alternative, F-25). **Placeholders used in the worked examples** (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/attention/`): `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000 AU (`theta` 6,000,000), `HOLD_PM` 800 (`theta_HOLD` 4,800,000), `L_CEIL_PM` 1020 (`L_CEIL` 6,120,000), `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1800, `ATT_MARGIN` 50 AU, `B_table` {ring 1: 300, ring 2: 450, ring 3: 550} AU, `U_TOOL` 4, `U_LOUD` 180, `W_L` 1000 for every lit kind, `W_P` 100, `W_S` 6000, `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W` 1500 W. **Cited, never re-derived:** the per-emitter term of EM D.12 (`section_emission`), KRN D.10 (`rng_derive`, `rng_int`), `ticks_for_seconds`, Graph D.11 (`fairness_gate`, `W_MIN_STEPS`), Save/Load's `autosave_gate`. **Evaluation order inside one run (the formulas depend on it):** (1) drain `pending_noise_mAU` into `I_noise` and `pending_ledger_mAU[s]` into the ledger inputs (F-1); (2) read `emitters()` and `power_use`, compute `nl_w` and `d_nl` and shift `nl_hist_w` (F-3, F-4); (3) integrate `L` (F-5); (4) update the ledger (F-6); (5) evaluate the phase logic on the integrated `L` (F-9, F-14, F-17) and advance `esc_ticks` while in TELL (F-12); (6) perform entry and exit actions (M17 deposits, `set_beacon`, F-18, F-19); (7) compute the beat schedule, tier and the published view (F-7, F-12, F-13). A TELL-entry run therefore publishes beat 0 (step 7) and runs no abort check on that same run.

#### F-1. Noise intake (pending accumulator)

The noise_intake formula is defined as:

`pending_noise_mAU = min(L_CEIL, pending_noise_mAU + U[class] × 1000)` per accepted push; `pending_ledger_mAU[sec] = min(L_CEIL, pending_ledger_mAU[sec] + U[class] × 1000)` per accepted push whose source resolves, at push time, to a loaded section `sec`; at step (1): `I_noise = pending_noise_mAU` and `I_s(noise) = pending_ledger_mAU[s]`, then both are zeroed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; an accepted `BURDEN` push also sets the persisted flag `pending_burden`, which step (1) reads (it suspends this run's decay, F-5) and then clears *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U[class]` | int AU | 0 to `U_LOUD` (knob range up to `ATT_THRESHOLD` ÷ 10) | the intake table (AT-3): `QUIET` 0; `WALK`, `RUN`, `CARRY` cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1); `TOOL` = `U_TOOL`; `LOUD` = `U_LOUD`; `BURDEN` = `U_BURDEN` (F-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | attention units added by one push |
| `src` | PlayerId or object ref | transient | `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)` | the attribution key, resolved to a section **at push time** (F-2) and then discarded; never persisted |
| `pending_noise_mAU` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` | block `attention.state` (persisted) | noise accumulated since the last run, for `I_noise`, whether or not its source could be placed; saturates at `L_CEIL` |
| `pending_ledger_mAU[sec]` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` per loaded section | block (persisted, sorted by `section_id`, loaded sections only) | the part of that noise attributed to a loaded section, for the ledger only (F-6) |
| `I_noise` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` | derived | the noise input of this run |
| `pending_burden` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | bool | 0/1 | block (persisted) | set by any accepted `BURDEN` push since the last run; read and cleared at step (1); when set, this run's decay is 0 (F-5) |

**Output Range:** one source, one run: walking 6,000 mAU; running or carrying-while-running 24,000 mAU (PCI:560: 0 to 24 per run); a `TOOL` channel run 24,000 mAU; one `LOUD` push 180,000 mAU; holding a salvage carry 48,000 mAU moving (the CARRY walk push 6,000 plus `BURDEN` 42,000; any class, R5) and 42,000 mAU standing still *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The saturation at `L_CEIL` changes nothing below the ceiling (the level is clamped there anyway, F-5) and gives the overflow edge case a defined, bounded result. It binds only for a storm: one `LOUD` push is about 3 percent of `L_CEIL` at the placeholders, so it takes 34 of them in a single run to reach it, and no real source does; the earlier per-source clamp could never bind and is dropped (the review found it unreachable), which is why AC-AT-12 uses a synthetic storm. A `pending_ledger_mAU[s]` whose section became unloaded between the push and the step (a same-tick departure) is dropped and counted in `dropped_unattributed` at step (1), so AT-2's "no ledger entry for a dormant section" holds on that run too. An unknown class adds 0 and increments a counter.
**Example:** the player runs for 6 ticks and a cut completes in the same run: `24,000 + 180,000 = 204,000` mAU enter `pending_noise_mAU` (and the same into `pending_ledger_mAU` of the player's section). A 180-tick patch (`TOOL` pushed once per accrued tick) pushes `180 × 4 × 1000 = 720,000` mAU = 720 AU (IA D.9: equal to running for the same 180 ticks, four times walking). A `RUN` push of 24,000 mAU from a foot origin in a dormant section adds 24,000 to `pending_noise_mAU` and nothing to `pending_ledger_mAU`.

#### F-2. Source-to-section attribution (feeds the ledger, not the level)

The source_attribution formula is defined as:

`section(src) = actor_section(src)` for a PlayerId source (foot-origin lookup); `section_of(src)` for an object source, both evaluated **at push time** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `emitter.section_id` for light; the `power_use` section for power; a push whose section is dormant or unknown is dropped from the ledger (it stays in `pending_noise_mAU`) and counted

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `actor_section(actor_id)` | section id | loaded sections | Ship Body read *(Body is Designed, 2026-10-27)* | the section holding the actor's foot origin |
| `section_of(ref)` | section id | loaded sections | Layout / owning system | the section of an object source |
| `dropped_unattributed` | int counter | 0 to unbounded | DIAGNOSTIC | pushes dropped because the section was dormant or unknown |

**Output Range:** a loaded section id or a counted drop. A drop removes the push from the **ledger only**; the push still counts toward `I_noise` and `L` (the web hears a sound whose source it cannot place; the level is the world's, the locus is a choice). **Example:** the player in derelict A (`sec_a_2`) pushes `RUN` for 6 ticks: 24,000 mAU enter `pending_noise_mAU` and 24,000 mAU enter `pending_ledger_mAU[sec_a_2]`, which feeds `ledger[sec_a_2]` at step (4) (F-6).

#### F-3. Light stimulus

The light_stimulus formula is defined as:

`I_light[s] = ( Σ_{e in emitters(s)} W_L[kind_e] × floor(output_pm_e × reach_px_e / 1000) × 6 ) div 1000`, and `I_light = Σ_s I_light[s]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_L[kind]` | int (micro-AU per unit per tick) | 0 to 4000; 1000 for `BEAM`, `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN`, `DEPLOYABLE`; `FAULT_GLOW` 1000 in CALM and TELL and **0** in BREACH and SUBSIDING; `BEACON` **0** always (lint) | `assets/data/attention/weights` | the per-kind weight |
| `output_pm_e` | int | 0 to 1000 | emitter record (`emitters()`) | the emitter's steady output |
| `reach_px_e` | int px | 8 to 384 (EM shape table) | emitter record | the emitter's current reach |
| `floor(output_pm_e × reach_px_e / 1000)` | int | 0 to 384 | **cited: EM D.12 per-emitter term** | the Emitter State's own term, never re-derived |
| `kind_e` | enum | the closed `emitter_kind` list | emitter record | selects the weight |
| `emitters(s)` | list | at most `MAX_EMITTERS` in the world | Emitter State | every emitter of loaded section `s`, lit or not as the record reports `output_pm` |
| `I_light` | int64 mAU | 0 to 884,736 per run at the weight cap | derived | light input of this run |

**Output Range:** 0 with no lit emitter. At the placeholder weights the maximum is `96 × 384 × 1000 × 6 div 1000 = 221,184` mAU per run (221 AU, `MAX_EMITTERS` emitters all at output 1000 and reach 384); 884,736 at the weight cap 4000. The owner's beam is counted **once**, from the emitter list at apex value; `lux_pm` is never read as a stimulus and `probe()` is never called (EM-21, EM-27). `served_pm` is not a stimulus: it reaches Attention as light through `output_pm`.
**Example:** the owner's wide beam (output 1000, reach 288, term 288), three home fixtures (1000, 144) and the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (1000, 144) all in one section: `(1000 × (288 + 4 × 144) × 6) div 1000 = (1000 × 864 × 6) div 1000 = 5,184` mAU per run. A live glowstick (1000, 96) adds `576` mAU per run; the stack cap `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` (four) adds 2,304: light is a spend, never a safe state.
*Derivation of `W_L[BEAM]` = 1000:* the wide beam's term 288 at weight 1000 is 288 mAU per tick, about 0.29 of a walking tick; running plus the beam from `L = B` settles toward `B + 4,288 × 1800 mAU` and crosses `theta` in 2,412 ticks (40 s) in the integer model (R4's calibration "about 45 s" is the same order); walking alone settles `1 AU × tau` = 1,800 AU above `B`, 30 percent of `theta`.

#### F-4. Power stimulus

The power_stimulus formula is defined as:

`nl_w[s] = max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w)`; `d_nl[s] = nl_w[s] − nl_hist_w[s][4]` (the value five runs ago); `I_power[s] = ( W_P × nl_w[s] × 6 ) div 1000 + ( W_S × max(0, d_nl[s]) × 6 ) div 1000`; then `nl_hist_w[s]` shifts by one and `nl_w[s]` is stored at index 0; a loaded section with no entry (boot, wake, a restore that lacks it) is seeded `nl_hist_w[s][0..4] = nl_w[s]` before the first read; `W_N × noisy_w` is reserved at `W_N` = 0. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (C11 closed against LS-19: no `beacon_w`, no `beacon_step_w`, no read of `delta_w_last5`)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_P` | int (micro-AU per W per tick) | 0 to 300 (schema bound, `CONTENT_FIELD_RANGE`); placeholder 100 | data | steady non-lamp draw weight ("surplus draw is never free", LSG:794) |
| `W_S` | int (micro-AU per W per tick) | 0 to 20000; placeholder 6000 | data | transient (inrush) weight (the hiding verb's cost, LSG:809) |
| `draw_w` | int W | 0 to 10,000 per section | `power_use(section_id)` (LS-19: total served W, lamps and inrush included, battery charge excluded) | the section's total served load this step |
| `lamps_lit_w` | int W | 0 to `draw_w` | `power_use` (LS-19: served W of LOAD subtype LAMP, including any LAMP node such as `bcn_main`) | subtracted so a lamp is counted once, as light (EM-27) |
| `nl_w` | int W | 0 to 10,000 | derived: `max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w)` | the non-lamp draw |
| `nl_hist_w[s][0..4]` | 5 × int W | 0 to 10,000 each | block `attention.state` (persisted per loaded section, sorted by `section_id`; AT-1) | the last five runs' `nl_w`, oldest at index 4; seeded flat when a section loads, wakes or is restored without an entry |
| `d_nl` | int W (signed) | −10,000 to 10,000 | derived | the lamp-excluded transient (the inrush enters here) |
| `delta_w_last5` | — | **not read** | `power_use` (LS-19: signed, lamps included, reads 0 after a load) | cannot be used as the transient: it contains lamp watts; reserved in case the Graph adds a lamp-excluded field |
| `noisy_w` | int W | reserved | `power_use` (LS-19: served W of SCRUBBER, PUMP, HEATER, DOOR_MOTOR) | now defined; weight `W_N` 0 until a tuning pass wants it |

**Output Range:** 0 for an idle or absent section; a dead (SCRAM) section reports no draw and gives 0. The cold start of the whole tier 0 and 1 branch **with its lamps off** at `INRUSH_PM` 1500 (the Graph's data, default 1500 permille over `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 steps) takes `nl_w` from 0 to 1,500 W and gives `(6000 × 1500 × 6) div 1000 = 54,000` mAU through `W_S` for each of five runs = 270 AU in total (4.5 percent of `theta`), plus the steady `W_P` share. A cold start that also lights lamps enters the lamps as light (F-3) and adds **nothing** to `W_S` for them, because `nl_w` excludes lamp watts. If `power_use` is absent for a section, `I_power[s]` is 0, its history is left untouched and one diagnostic is logged per process. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
