**Example (steady):** home-ship section, `draw_w` 2,500, `lamps_lit_w` 600: `nl_w` 1,900 and `(100 × 1900 × 6) div 1000 = 1,140` mAU per run. **Example (a section wakes already drawing 2,500 W):** the history is seeded flat at 1,900, so `d_nl` is 0 and the first run adds `1,140 + 0 = 1,140` mAU; with an unseeded (zeroed) history `d_nl` would read 1,900 and add `(6000 × 1900 × 6) div 1000 = 68,400` mAU of phantom transient, which AC-AT-120 forbids. **Example (a lamp switch-on):** a 300 W lamp bank is closed: `draw_w` and `lamps_lit_w` both rise by 300, `nl_w` does not change, `d_nl` is 0 and `W_S` adds nothing; the lamps enter through F-3 only (reading the Graph's `delta_w_last5`, which includes lamp watts, would have added `5 × 10,800` = 54,000 mAU, 54 AU, over five runs, the double count the review found). **Example (the beacon):** BREACH, `draw_w` 2,520 (including the 20 W beacon `bcn_main`), `lamps_lit_w` 620 (LS-19 puts `bcn_main` in the lamp subset), `nl_w` 1,900 before and after the switch-on: `I_power = 1,140` mAU, no transient and no special term. *C11 closed:* LS-19 defines every `power_use` field; the lamp-excluded history is the fallback the review named, and the Graph ask in AT-4 is a simplification only. **Re-run of the dependent lints:** `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W` is a lamps-off load, `r_dark` (F-16) never contained a lamp term in `W_P` and the transient term is 0 at steady state, so the F-15, F-15b, F-16 and F-25 figures are **unchanged** (the integer model reproduces 90 / 93 / 95 runs on a clean ship and 93 / 96 / 98 with the two wake glows).
*Choice of `W_P` 100:* the MVP home-ship tier 0 and 1 load is 1,000 W (LSG:433), giving 100 mAU per tick = 180 AU at steady state, small enough to leave the survivable-dark floor (F-16) room. The ceiling is `W_P` 116 at ring 3 with the two wake glows (each +1 adds 2.7 AU to `x_dark` at 1,500 W and the ring-3 slack is 12 AU in level terms).

#### F-5. The integrator (registry: `attention_integrator`)

The attention_integrator formula is defined as:

`dec = 0` if `pending_burden` was set when this run drained its pending input (a salvage carry is held aboard a non-home locus body and not `IN_POD`, F-26, AT-3 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*), else `dec = max(0, (6 × (L − B) + tau − 1) div tau)`; `L' = min(L_CEIL, max(B, L + I_run − dec))`; `I_run = I_noise + I_light + I_power` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5: the decay suspension)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L` | int64 mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | block (persisted) | the level before this run |
| `B` | int64 mAU | 300,000 to about 724,000 at the placeholder rows (about 594,000 with the two wake glows) | F-8 | the baseline for the current ring |
| `tau` | int ticks | 300 to 3600 (at least 300 by IR-3), placeholder 1800 | `TAU_ATT_TICKS` | the single memory constant |
| `I_run` | int64 mAU | at least 0 | F-1, F-3, F-4 | this run's input |
| `L_CEIL` | int64 mAU | `theta` to 1.1 `theta` | data (`L_CEIL_PM`, ADDITION X1 adopted by checkpoint ruling CQ-2 and approved at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate) | the saturation level |
| `dec` | int64 mAU | 0 to `(L_CEIL − B) × 6 / 1800 + 1` | derived | the ceiling decay of this run, computed on the **pre-input** `L` |

**Output Range:** `[B, L_CEIL]` = [300,000, 6,120,000] mAU at ring 1. `dec` is at least 1 whenever `L > B`, so with zero input the level reaches `B` **exactly** (the integer truncation stall of LSG:464 cannot occur); with large input it saturates at `L_CEIL`. Under a constant input `r` (mAU per tick) the steady level is `L_ss = B + r × tau` (the per-run fixed point has `dec = 6r`); while a salvage carry is held aboard a non-home locus body (not `IN_POD`, AT-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* there is no steady state, and the level rises by `I_run` per run until `L_CEIL` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. If a ring change raises `B` above `L`, then `L − B` is negative, `dec` is 0 and `L` is lifted to `B` in the same run; if `B` falls, `L` decays toward the new floor.
**Upper-bound lint on theta** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (`reckless_crossing`): the sustained verbs (run, or the tool channel, with the beam) inflow `r_sustain = 4,000 + 288 = 4,288` mAU per tick, so the level can never exceed `B + r_sustain × tau` = 8,018 AU at ring 1 and a theta near that is unreachable. The lint is `t_cross(theta, r_sustain, from B) ≤ CROSS_MAX_TICKS` (placeholder 4,320, 72 s), run through the integer F-5: theta 6,000 crosses in 2,412 ticks, 7,000 in 3,642, 7,200 (the game-designer alternative) in 4,038, 7,300 in 4,272 (the edge), 7,400 in 4,542 (fails), 8,000 in 10,872 (fails) and 8,100 never. So the safe range of `ATT_THRESHOLD` is 5,816 to 7,300 AU (AC-AT-126).
**Example:** `B` 300,000; `L` 2,000,000; this run: running 24,000, beam 1,728, three home fixtures 2,592, home power 1,140, EMERGENCY lamp 864, so `I_run` = 30,324; `dec = (6 × 1,700,000 + 1799) div 1800 = 5,667`; `L' = 2,000,000 + 30,324 − 5,667 = 2,024,657`. *Stance:* the level is a **rate detector with a 30-second memory**, not a session score (AT-5); a TELL means "loud or lit at a high rate for roughly half a minute to a minute", and `tau` is capped from above by abortability (F-15).

#### F-6. Section ledger and the locus (locus choice only)

The section_ledger formula is defined as:

`ledger'[s] = min(L_CEIL, max(0, ledger[s] + I_s − (6 × ledger[s] + tau − 1) div tau))`; `score[s] = ledger[s] × (1000 if occupied(s) else LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM) div 1000`; `locus = argmax_s score[s]`, ties to the lowest `section_id`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ledger[s]` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` | block (persisted, sorted by `section_id`) | the per-section memory, same `tau` as `L` |
| `I_s` | int64 mAU | at least 0 | F-1 to F-4 attributed by F-2 | the part of `I_run` attributed to section `s` |
| `occupied(s)` | bool | | the Controller's `loco_state` and `actor_section` *(provisional on Body)* | whether a living player is in `s` |
| `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` | int permille | 600 to 1000; default 1000 | data | 1000 means "leader only" (conforming to R14); lower values discount unoccupied sections |

**Output Range:** `ledger[s]` in `[0, L_CEIL]`; a dormant section holds no entry; the locus is evaluated once, at TELL entry, and fixed for the epoch (AT-14). **Example:** derelict A's ledger 3,000,000 with `I_s` 30,324: decay `(6 × 3,000,000 + 1799) div 1800 = 10,000`, so `ledger' = 3,020,324`; the lit, idle home ship has a steady ledger of about 1,379,000 (`766 × 1800`); locus = derelict A. If the player left A's fixtures lit and now welds loudly at home, the home ledger can win: the intended P3 consequence. A tie at 2,000,000 each goes to the lower `section_id`. **Steady-state comparison** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (each steady ledger is `r_s × tau` at `tau` 1,800, all in mAU, so like is compared with like): the lit idle home ship 1,379,000 (766 per tick); walking with the beam in a derelict section 2,318,400 (1,288 per tick); standing still there with the beam 518,400 (288 per tick); welding at home with the home ship lit `(4,000 + 766) × 1,800`, which clamps at `L_CEIL` 6,120,000; a berthed derelict's two lit fixtures 518,400 (2 × 144 per tick). Noise wins the locus wherever someone is moving or working; light and power decide it only between quiet sections, which is the one meaning of 'the lights you left on' (Moment C; AC-AT-59 fixtures).

#### F-7. Published level (`level_pm`)

The level_publication formula is defined as:

`level_pm = min(1000, (1000 × (L − B)) div (theta − B))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L`, `B`, `theta` | int64 mAU | as F-5 | block, F-8, data | level, baseline and threshold |
| `level_pm` | int | 0 to 1000 | derived | baseline-relative, **never drawn directly** |

**Output Range:** 0 at `L = B`; 1000 at `L ≥ theta`. Lint: `theta > B` at every ring (a division by zero otherwise). Presentation may use `level_pm` only after the quantisation of F-24; no continuous mapping exists (AT-26). **Example:** `L` 3,000,000, `B` 300,000: `(1000 × 2,700,000) div 5,700,000 = 473`.

#### F-8. Baseline by depth

The baseline_by_depth formula is defined as:

`B = B_table[ring_B] × 1000`, with `ring_B = max over sections s containing a living player of ring_of(s)`, `held_ring` while a seat is IN_POD

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `B_table[ring]` | int AU | non-decreasing in `ring`; placeholder 300 / 450 / 550 | `assets/data/attention/baseline` (rows beyond ring 1 are test rows; Depth Rings owns them in the Vertical Slice) | the baseline per ring |
| `ring_of(s)` | int | 1 and up | the registry formula `ring_of` (Web Map & Transit): the owning record's ring (welded to HOME, parked pod to `pod_at`'s record, mid-leg pod to the departure record); absent or sub-1 resolves to 1, logged once per process | the section's ring |
| `held_ring` | int | 1 and up | block (persisted) | the departure ring held while a seat is `IN_POD` |

**Output Range:** 300,000 to 550,000 mAU at the placeholder rows; the hard ceiling is the F-15 cap (about 724 AU at the clean-ship dark load and about 594 AU with the two wake glows, F-15, F-16). Invariants (lint): `B_table` non-decreasing; F-16 holds at every row with `N_GLOW_DARK` = the glows authored active at wake. **Example:** a player in derelict A (ring 1) and a crewmate at home (ring 1): `B = 300,000`; the player pods to a ring 2 section: the ring of the departure section is held until arrival, then `B = 450,000` and `L` is lifted to `B` if lower.

#### F-9. TELL entry guard

The tell_entry formula is defined as:

`enter_TELL = (phase == CALM) AND (L' ≥ theta) AND (T ≥ refract_until_T) AND (T ≥ rearm_until_T)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L'` | int64 mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | F-5 | the level after this run's integration |
| `refract_until_T` | int ticks | 0 and up | block | set at SUBSIDING end |
| `rearm_until_T` | int ticks | 0 and up | block | set at an abort |

**Output Range:** boolean; the guards are inclusive, so entry exactly at a timer boundary is legal. No catch-up elapsed time is credited when the level sat above `theta` during a timer. `attention_arm_tell` replaces the whole guard except `phase == CALM` (F-10): it is a SYSTEM-origin test and onboarding seam that does not honour the two timers, the floor still binds, and a fixture or script arms only from a clean state (*orchestrator ruling*). **Example:** `L'` 6,000,000 at `T` 7,200 with `refract_until_T` 5,400 and `rearm_until_T` 6,000: true.

#### F-10. Tell draw

The tell_draw formula is defined as:

`tell_len = rng_int(TELL_DRAW_LO, TELL_DRAW_HI)` with the raw value drawn through the kernel's `RngFacet` as `draw_epoch(attention.tell, rng_counter)` (= `rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "attention.tell", rng_counter)`; the facet owns the epoch term, Attention computes no `counter_eff` and never reads `restore_epoch`; ADR-0005 section 2.4), then `rng_counter += 1` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*; armed: `tell_len = clamp(arg, TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS)` with no draw

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_DRAW_LO`, `TELL_DRAW_HI` | int ticks | `TELL_MIN_TICKS` to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`; placeholders **900** and 1800 (CQ-4) | data | the played range, 15 to 30 s |
| `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 | **derived**: `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` | the floor; never a knob |
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | fixed by P2's "10 to 30 s" and SL:528 | the cap |
| `rng_counter` | int | 0 and up, below `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` (lint) | block (persisted) | the stream position; restore cannot reroll a tell already drawn |
| `restore_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | u32 | 0 and up | owned by Save/Load (`slot_epoch_a/b.bin` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)*; advanced, durably before `request_restore`, on every restore except a Continue of the newest `QUIT` generation, which carries it; R1 as amended by R13/R14); read-only in `kernel.clock`; not stored in the block *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | constant between restores, differs after each advancing restore and is unchanged by a carried Continue, a function of the restore history alone (AT-8) |
| `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int | fixed 2^20 = 1,048,576 | Command & Simulation Layer (the kernel's `RngFacet` owns it with the derivation; ADR-0005 section 2.1; cited here) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* | keeps the epoch term from colliding with any reachable `rng_counter` |
| `arg` | int ticks | any | `attention_arm_tell {tell_len}` (SYSTEM origin) | the armed length, clamped |

**Output Range:** 900 to 1800 ticks for an organic tell (mean 1350); 600 to 1800 only through `attention_arm_tell`. Lint: `TELL_MIN_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO ≤ TELL_DRAW_HI ≤ TELL_MAX_TICKS`. *(Orchestrator ruling: an armed tell does not consume `rng_counter`, so arming never shifts the stream of later organic tells.)* **Example:** counter 3 at `restore_epoch` 0 with the stub stream yielding 1,044 gives `tell_len` 1,044 (inside 900 to 1800); after two advancing restores (for example two death restores; a Continue of the newest `QUIT` generation would not advance it, R13) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* the same counter reads the stream at 3 + 2 × 1,048,576, a different draw (AC-AT-130); `attention_arm_tell {tell_len: 100}` is clamped to 600 and leaves `rng_counter` unchanged.

#### F-11. Remaining and due

The tell_remaining formula is defined as:

`breach_due_T = tell_start_T + tell_len`; `tell_remaining(T) = max(0, breach_due_T − T)` in TELL, else 0; the BREACH-or-abort decision happens at the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_start_T`, `tell_len` | int ticks | as F-10 | block | the seeded, fixed window |
| `breach_due_T` | int ticks | | block | the earliest breach tick |
| `tell_remaining` | int ticks | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | published to Save/Load, SIM readers and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only | the conservative time to `breach_due_T` |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1800, strictly non-increasing within an epoch. The BREACH decision falls in `[breach_due_T, breach_due_T + 5]` and **never earlier** (AC-AT-30). **Example:** `tell_start_T` 7,200 and `tell_len` 1,044: at `T` 7,800 `tell_remaining` = 444; the due run is `T` 8,244 (a multiple of 6, so no lateness). With `tell_len` 1,047 the due run is 8,250 (3 ticks late).

#### F-12. Beat schedule (elapsed-keyed)

The beat_schedule formula is defined as:

`off[0] = 0`; `off[k+1] = off[k] + max(BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS, interval(e_k) + jit(k))` with `e_k = min(esc_ticks at the run that publishes beat k, off[k])` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `interval(e) = BEAT_START_TICKS − ((BEAT_START_TICKS − BEAT_END_TICKS) × min(e, BEAT_RAMP_TICKS)) div BEAT_RAMP_TICKS`; `jit(k) = (Ihash.h32(HASH_SEED, tell_epoch, k, 0, SALT_BEAT) mod (2 × BEAT_JITTER_TICKS + 1)) − BEAT_JITTER_TICKS`, with `HASH_SEED` a fixed non-zero constant and `Ihash` the shared pure-integer hash of ADR-0005 section 2.6 (AT-28 and the 'hash is an ADR candidate' tuning-knob note are closed) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)*; beat `k` is published on the first run with `T ≥ tell_start_T + off[k]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BEAT_START_TICKS`, `BEAT_END_TICKS`, `BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` | int ticks | 150, 45, 1800 (`BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` = `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) | data | the opening interval, the closing interval and the ramp length |
| `BEAT_JITTER_TICKS`, `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 8, 30 | data | the jitter amplitude and the gap floor between consecutive beats |
| `Ihash.h32` (formerly `hash32`) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* | pure integer function | 0 to 2^32 − 1 | the shared `Ihash` of ADR-0005 section 2.6 (Attention supplies `HASH_SEED` and `SALT_BEAT`; closes the former ADR candidate shared with Lighting, LO:255) | **never `rng_derive`**; reconstructable from the persisted epoch |
| `k` | int | 0 and up | `tell_beat` | the beat index |
| `off[k]` | int ticks | 0 and up | block (`beat_next_off`) | the nominal offset from `tell_start_T` (real time) |
| `e_k` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int ticks | 0 to `off[k]` | derived from the persisted `esc_ticks` | the escalation clock as the beat interval sees it; equal to `off[k]` while the player stays at or above the hold line (so the schedule is then exactly the old elapsed-keyed one), smaller after a freeze |

**Output Range:** intervals 37 to 158 ticks; about 20 beats in an 1,800-tick tell (the integral of `1 / interval` over 0 to 1800 is `(1800 / 105) × ln(150 / 45)` = 20.6). The schedule runs on **nominal** offsets, so run quantisation (a beat may publish up to five ticks late) never accumulates. **No beat is suppressed in the final gap before `breach_due_T`** (CQ-5).
**Floor proof (`MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4 in `[tell_start_T, tell_start_T + TELL_MIN_TICKS)`).** `off[k] + interval(off[k])` is strictly increasing in `off[k]` (slope about 0.94), so the latest `off[4]` comes from the largest jitter on every gap. Jitter 0: 0, 150, 292, 425, 551 (five beats; next 669; this sweep is the loud case, the frozen-clock cases follow). Every jitter +8: 0, 158, 307, 448, 580 (published on the run at +582, inside the window; next 705). Every jitter −8: 0, 142, 276, 402, 521 (five beats; next 633). So every legal `(tell_len ≥ 600, seed)` publishes at least **5** beats in the floor window against `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4: one beat of margin. An aborted tell is excluded from the sweep (it may end the block early). The sweep above is the case where the clock equals elapsed time (the player stays at or above the hold line); the frozen cases follow.

**Floor proof under the frozen clock** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(redone for the OQ-AT-25 ruling; integer model; AC-AT-128 executes it)*. Two statements. **(1) Universal bound, any level trajectory.** `interval` is non-increasing in `e`, so `interval(e_k) ≤ interval(0) = BEAT_START_TICKS` and every gap is at most `BEAT_START_TICKS + BEAT_JITTER_TICKS` = 158: the first four beats sit at offsets at most 0, 158, 316 and 474 (published at most five ticks late, so by 479), all inside the 600-tick window. The clock frozen from the first run with every jitter +8 gives exactly 0, 158, 316, 474 and the fifth beat at 632: **four beats, margin 0**; with jitter 0 the fifth falls on 600, outside the half-open window, again four; with every jitter −8 it is 0, 142, 284, 426, 568, five. So `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4 holds for every `(tell_len, seed)` and every `L` trajectory, with no slack in the extreme, which is why the knob may not be raised to 5 without lengthening the floor window. **Lint (frozen-clock floor)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `3 × (BEAT_START_TICKS + BEAT_JITTER_TICKS) + 5 < TELL_MIN_TICKS` (the fourth beat, published at most five ticks late, inside the window): 3 × 158 + 5 = 479 at the placeholders; it caps `BEAT_START_TICKS` at 190 at jitter 8 and 186 at jitter 12. **(2) Organic tells (entered at `L' ≥ theta`, no armed seam).** A freeze needs `L < theta_HOLD`; from `theta` the fastest possible fall under the line (zero input beyond the unswitchable dark load, ring 1, clean ship) is 80 runs, and 82 to 87 runs at the other rows (F-15b), so the clock has run at least 474 ticks (79 runs after the entry run) before any freeze can begin, and the first four beats are the loud schedule's: all +8 offsets 0, 158, 307, 448; beat 4 takes `interval(min(esc, 448)) = interval(448) = 124` plus 8, so it is due at 580 and publishes on the run at +582, inside the window. **Five beats, margin 1**, as in the elapsed-keyed proof (jitter 0: 0, 150, 292, 425, 551; jitter −8: 0, 142, 276, 402, 521; the clock frozen at 474 changes none of them). An armed tell entered under the hold line is case (1) and ends by the abort after `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` unless the fixture holds the level up (E-24). **Example:** epoch 12, `tell_start_T` 7,200: beat 0 publishes in the entry run at 7,200; beat 1 is due at offset 150 and publishes on the run at 7,350 (exact, since 150 is a multiple of 6); beat 4 with every jitter +8 is due at offset 580 and publishes at 7,782.

#### F-13. Tier

The tell_tier formula is defined as:

`tier = 0` outside TELL; inside, `tier = 1 + (e ≥ TIER2_TICKS) + (e ≥ TIER3_TICKS)` with `e = esc_ticks` (the escalation clock, AT-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 and 1200 (safe 600 to 900 / 900 to 1500; `TIER2_TICKS` stays at least 600 while Lighting reads `tier ≥ 1`) | data | the elapsed thresholds |
| `e` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int ticks | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | block (`esc_ticks`, published as `tell_elapsed`) | the escalation clock: elapsed time while `L' ≥ theta_HOLD`, frozen under it |

**Output Range:** 0 to 3. The whole floor window is tier 1, Lighting's tier-1 gutter recipe (registry `gutter_recipe`, LO:257); a tell reaches tier 2 whenever `L` stays at or above `theta_HOLD` for 600 ticks of the escalation clock, which the registry `gutter_recipe` spaces at 300/600 frames (placeholder) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "every organic tell reaches tier 2" ignored the freeze)*. **Ticks to freeze and tier at freeze** (ring 1, entry at `theta`, the tool and beam running until the stop, home ship lit unless the breaker is open; clean ship / two wake glows): a stop with the beam on freezes after 684 / 714 ticks at `e` 678 / 708, tier 2; with the lamp killed 588 / 612 at `e` 582 / 606, tier 1 / 2; with the lamp killed and the breaker open 480 / 492 at `e` 474 / 486, tier 1. With a 180-tick reaction every posture reaches tier 2 first (696 to 954 ticks). So only the all-dark stop, made at once, holds the tell at tier 1; this is the lag the Player Fantasy now states, and AC-AT-135 reports it. **Example:** the run at `e` = 594 shows tier 1; the run at `e` = 600 shows tier 2. A player who falls under the hold line at `e` = 594 stays at tier 1 for the rest of the epoch, and one who climbs back above it resumes from 594 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

#### F-14. Abort

The tell_abort formula is defined as:

`below_hold_runs' = (L' < theta_HOLD) ? below_hold_runs + 1 : 0`; `abort = (below_hold_runs' × 6 ≥ ABORT_HOLD_TICKS) OR (T ≥ breach_due_T AND L' < theta_HOLD) OR (T ≥ breach_due_T AND locus_vacated)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `locus_vacated = (locus_body is not the home-ship body) AND (tell_occupants_at_entry ≠ ∅) AND (no living seat has actor_section in locus_body) AND (every seat of tell_occupants_at_entry has occ_gone set)`; per recorded seat, every TELL and BREACH run: `occ_gone[seat] = true` if `loco_state == IN_POD`; else, if the seat is living and `actor_section` resolves, `occ_gone[seat] = (actor_section ∉ locus_body)`; else (the seat is DEAD, or its `actor_section` is unavailable) `occ_gone[seat]` keeps its last value, and one diagnostic is logged per epoch *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `theta_HOLD` | int64 mAU | `theta × HOLD_PM / 1000` | F-5 conventions | the hold line (R8's absolute definition stands; the baseline-relative form is an Open Question) |
| `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 60 to 300, a multiple of 6; placeholder 120 | data | consecutive time under the hold line (20 runs; the first run under counts as run 1) |
| `below_hold_runs` | int | 0 to 50 | block (persisted) | the consecutive-run counter |
| `locus_vacated` | bool | | derived at the due run from the persisted `locus_body`, `tell_occupants_at_entry` and the seats' `actor_section` (Ship Body); evaluated before any dormancy fallback | every seat recorded in the non-home locus body at TELL entry has left it, so the due-run breach is voided (AT-22, E-23); evaluated on every TELL run (it suppresses the dormancy fallback from the first run it holds, AT-14) and acts on the decision only at the due run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `occ_gone[seat]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | bool per recorded seat | false at TELL entry (and again at BREACH entry, over `locus_occupants_at_entry`) | block (persisted with the recorded seat sets, AT-1) | whether a recorded occupant has left the locus body. **A recorded seat that dies inside the body, or whose position cannot be resolved, keeps the status it last had (present, if it never left), and a seat in the pod is gone**: so a membership test can no longer be passed vacuously by an unavailable `actor_section` (E-62), a death inside the locus never reads as a departure (no relief rewarded by a death in co-op), and a death in the pod still reads as gone (E-69, user ruling D60) |

**Output Range:** boolean. If more than one clause is true on one run, the abort wins (one transition). An abort deposits no STRESS, switches no beacon, sets `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, freezes `tell_beat`, leaves `L` unchanged when `ABORT_RELIEF_PM` is 1000 (else `L' = min(L', B + ABORT_RELIEF_PM × (theta − B) div 1000)`), and sets `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS`. **Example:** `L` falls under 4,800,000 at the run `T` = `tell_start_T` + 468; the 20th consecutive run under the line is at +582 and the tell aborts there (114 ticks of elapsed time, 20 runs counted).

#### F-15. Abortability invariant (registry: `abortability_invariant`)

The abortability_invariant formula is defined as:

`x_dark + (L_CEIL − x_dark) × (1 − 6 / tau)^n ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`, with `n = (TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6) div 6`; the lint executes F-5 for `n` runs from `L_CEIL` with `I_run = 6 × r_dark` and asserts `L ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x_dark` | int mAU | `B` to `theta_HOLD` | F-16 | the ring's survivable-dark steady level |
| `ATT_MARGIN` | int AU | 0 to 200; placeholder 50 | data | the proof's safety margin |
| `n` | int runs | 99 | derived from `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | runs available before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse |
| `(1 − 6/tau)^n` | decay factor | 0.7185 at the placeholders | derived (continuous form 0.7189; the ceiling decay only falls faster, so this is the safe side) | what survives of the excess over `x_dark` |

**Output Range:** boolean per ring, and a cap on the dark level: `x_dark ≤ (theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN − 0.7185 × L_CEIL) / (1 − 0.7185)` = `(4,750 − 4,397) / 0.2815` = **1,253 AU**. Entry is assumed at `L_CEIL`, the highest the level can be (X1: during the refractory and the rearm window the level keeps integrating, and one run's maximum input has no design bound, so only a saturation makes the proof exist).
**Example (integer lint, no glows, F-16):** ring 1, `x_dark` 829 AU: the level falls below 4,750,000 mAU after **90** runs (540 ticks); ring 2 (979 AU): **93** runs (558 ticks); ring 3 (1,079 AU): **95** runs (570 ticks). All are at most 99 runs (594 ticks): holds, with 54, 36 and 24 ticks of slack. **With the two wake glows** (`N_GLOW_DARK` 2, `r_dark` 366, F-16): **93**, **96** and **98** runs (558, 576 and 588 ticks), slack 36, 18 and 6 ticks. *Sensitivity (the cost of depth, RK-2):*

| `tau` | decay over 594 ticks | `x_dark` cap (AU) | baseline cap after the 529 AU of dark load (AU) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 900 | 0.5169 | 3,284 | 2,755 |
| 1200 | 0.6096 | 2,611 | 2,082 |
| 1800 (ruled) | 0.7185 | 1,253 | 724 |

#### F-15b. Abortability, played range (registry: `abortability_play`)

The abortability_play formula is defined as:

`t_decay(ring) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO − 6`, with `t_decay(ring) = 6 × n_ring`, `n_ring` = the least `n` for which F-5 from `L_CEIL` at `I_run = 6 × r_dark` gives `L < theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_decay(ring)` | int ticks | 540 / 558 / 570 at rings 1 / 2 / 3 (558 / 576 / 588 with the two wake glows) | derived (closed form `tau × ln((L_CEIL − x_dark) / (theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN − x_dark))` = 540, 558, 570 to the run) | the dark decay time from the highest entry level |
| `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 to 204 (the ring-1 `x_dark_home_lit` row with the glows binds: 690 + 204 = 894) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; placeholder 180 | data | the allowance between beat 0 and the player zeroing every input |
| `TELL_DRAW_LO` | int ticks | 900 | F-10 | the shortest tell a player meets in play |

**Output Range:** boolean per ring; slack = `TELL_DRAW_LO − 6 − (t_decay + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS)`. **Example:** ring 1: `540 + 180 = 720 ≤ 894`; ring 2: `558 + 180 = 738 ≤ 894`; ring 3: `570 + 180 = 750 ≤ 894`. All hold, with slack 174, 156 and 144 ticks (with the two wake glows: 738, 756 and 768, slack 156, 138 and 126). The early-abort path adds `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` (120) to the reaction allowance and the decay and still holds: 840, 858 and 870 (858, 876 and 888 with the two wake glows), all within 894 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier totals 660, 678 and 690 omitted `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS`)*. **Moment A timeline check** (a player enters at `theta`, stops every input at once, ring 1, no glows): the level leaves `theta_HOLD` after **80** runs (480 ticks) and the 20th consecutive run under it is run 99, so the abort completes **594 ticks** after the player stops, against `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; the abort completes 6 ticks (100 ms) inside the TTL, which no human can use; with the two wake glows that ship today (`r_dark` 366) the level leaves the line after 82 runs and the abort completes at **606** ticks, 6 past the TTL *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 594 is the clean-ship case)*, so **an abort always forfeits the partial** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (with the home ship lit and only the lamp killed it completes at 702 ticks, past the TTL for certain). **Stated stance (moved here from Player Fantasy; answers the creative-director's CR-TIMELINE check):** the 594 ticks are 480 ticks to cross `theta_HOLD` from `theta` at ring 1 plus 114 ticks of `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` hold (the first run under the line counts as run 1, so the hold adds 19 runs), six inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; an abort therefore always forfeits the partial. That is a legitimate price of restraint, stated so it is a decision and not an accident, and it is balanced by a breach that has teeth (AT-11). The levers if a playtest wants a real resume window are `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` down, `HOLD_PM` up, or a wider `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` (Interaction-owned; IR-3 allows up to twice `TAU_ATT_TICKS`) (Tuning Knobs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**Added lint rows (the home ship lit; an unplanned transient)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The two rows above assume the home fixtures are dark. `r_home_lit` = `r_dark` + the lit home ship's three fixtures (432 mAU per tick) + 40 for the extra 400 W of non-lamp draw: 766 clean, 838 with the two wake glows. `autonomous_transient` re-runs F-5 with an extra `INRUSH` bump of 54,000 mAU for five consecutive runs (270 AU, the Graph's cold start) injected at the worst run. Integer model, entry at `L_CEIL`, `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180, limit `TELL_DRAW_LO − 6` = 894:

| Row (two wake glows) | Ring 1 | Ring 2 test | Ring 3 test |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x_dark` (home dark): `t_decay` + 180, slack | 738, +156 | 756, +138 | 768, +126 |
| `x_dark_home_lit`: `t_decay` (+ 180), slack | 690 (870), **+24** | 720 (900), **-6** | 744 (924), **-30** |
| `x_dark_home_lit`, clean ship (no glows) | 666 (846), +48 | 696 (876), +18 | 714 (894), 0 |
| `autonomous_transient`, home dark: total + 180, slack | 684 (864), +30 | 708 (888), +6 | 726 (906), -12 |
| `autonomous_transient`, home lit: total + 180, slack | 852 (1,032), **-138** | 888 (1,068), -174 | 918 (1,098), -204 |

Reading: at ring 1 the guarantee "abortable anywhere" holds, barely, until an unplanned transient arrives; at rings 2 and 3 it is a switches-only guarantee. Retune routes (single change, ring 1, glows, home lit, with the transient): `TELL_DRAW_LO` 1,038 or more, or `HOLD_PM` 850 (total 840), each recorded in OQ-AT-26 (**default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D49): neither is applied**, because each moves a registered value and the felt cost of restraint; revisit after the AC-AT-57 harness *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*).

**The carry and the abort** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(user ruling X3)*. The burden of F-26 is a player-controlled input, zeroed by `drop` like the tool channel, so the scripted player double of AC-AT-16 and AC-AT-64 now also sets any cargo down, and no row above changes: the rows already assume the highest entry level (`L_CEIL`) and a reaction allowance before the inputs are zero. What the carry adds is a posture the table did not have: **holding the cargo keeps the level pinned**. With the cargo in hand and everything else still, the inflow is `U_BURDEN × 1000` plus the lit terms (8,054 mAU per tick with the beam and the lit home ship) and the decay is suspended while it is held (F-5), so the level only rises, to `L_CEIL`, and the tell can only end in a breach; the abort starts the moment the cargo is down *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5; the reference numbers below were re-run with the suspension and are unchanged, because the level reaches `L_CEIL` within a few runs either way)*. Reference numbers (integer model, ring 1, home ship lit, the F-26 reference carry, a tell that opens at `theta`): drop at once and stand still with the beam on, and the abort completes 810 ticks later; the shortest played tell (900 ticks) still aborts at its due run if the drop comes within 162 ticks of the onset and breaches if it comes at 168 or later; kill the lamp as well and a drop at the full `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` (180) still aborts at the due run (the level is 4.65M against the 4.8M line). That is the stated price of attention, and it is why the carry decision is hard (F-26).

#### F-16. Survivable-dark floor (registry: `survivable_dark_floor`)

The survivable_dark_floor formula is defined as:

`x_dark = B + r_dark × tau`, with `r_dark = (W_P × DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W) div 1000 + (W_L[FIXTURE] × floor(output_pm_E × reach_px_E / 1000)) div 1000 + Σ_{g in N_GLOW_DARK} (W_L[FAULT_GLOW] × floor(output_pm_g × reach_px_g / 1000)) div 1000 + (W_L[FIXTURE] × g_dock) div 1000` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; conditions (ii-a) `x_dark ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN` and (ii-b) F-15

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r_dark` | int mAU per tick | 294 on a clean ship (150 + 144 + 0); 366 with the two wake glows | derived | everything the player cannot switch off, with the player still and the beam off |
| `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W` | int W | placeholder 1500 (of which 1000 W is the tier 0 and 1 branch, LSG:433) | the lint fixture: the home-ship's minimum survival load (O2, heat, reactor) with lamps off | the unswitchable power load; re-run when Assembly or the Graph changes the survival set |
| `E` | the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (1000, 144) | | DA-23 | the one `node_ref` 0 fixture; it counts as light (R5) but never carries a tell |
| `N_GLOW_DARK` | set of FAULT_GLOW emitters | **1 under the closed-valve staging, 2 under the OQ-DA-18 valve-OPEN fallback that ships until ASK-G1 is accepted** (DA-16: FUEL-DRIP's glow, plus O2-SHIP's 1/GAS glow only when `v_o2_cryo` is OPEN at wake; Derelict Authoring C-12, OQ-DA-35). The shipped rows therefore use **2**, the shipping value today, and are the conservative bound for the closed-valve staging; **0 for the clean-ship comparison**; the lint evaluates the count at every ring row | Derelict Authoring's fault list and Failure & Trail's glow table *(provisional)* | the unswitchable glows; a nominal glow (output 500, reach 72) adds 36 mAU per tick = 65 AU |

**Output Range:** clean ship (`N_GLOW_DARK` 0): ring 1: 829 AU; ring 2: 979 AU; ring 3: 1,079 AU (each extra unswitchable fixture adds 259 AU; each 100 W of survival load adds 18 AU; each nominal glow adds 65 AU). **The lint evaluates `N_GLOW_DARK` = the count of FAULT_GLOW emitters authored active at wake (two under the valve-OPEN fallback that ships until ASK-G1 is accepted, one under the closed-valve staging, DA-16) at every ring row**; at the placeholder `B_table` 300 / 450 / 550 it holds at 93 / 96 / 98 runs (at most 99). **Both bounds are checked** (CP-C8): (ii-a) is trivial (4,750 AU), and (ii-b) is the binding one (1,253 AU). **Example:** `x_dark(ring 1) = 300 + 270 + 259 = 829` AU, where 270 AU is `150 mAU/tick × 1800` and 259 AU is `144 × 1800`. **The glow case (RK-6, resolved by the placeholder rows):** two nominal glows (the fuel drip, plus the O2-SHIP gas glow that DA-16 makes active at wake only under the valve-OPEN fallback) add 72 mAU per tick, so `r_dark` = 366: ring 1 (959 AU) holds at 93 runs, ring 2 (1,109 AU) at 96 runs and ring 3 (1,209 AU) at 98 runs = 588 ticks, 6 ticks inside F-15 (it also passes F-15b: 588 + 180 = 768 ≤ 894). A ring-3 row of 600 AU needs 100 runs = 600 ticks with the two glows and fails F-15 by 6 ticks, which is why the placeholder row is 550; ring 3 can rise to about 594 AU with the two glows (595 fails) and to 724 AU on a clean ship, and a third nominal glow fails the 550 row (100 runs).

**The DOCK_STATUS glow (X2, user ruling)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The docked pod's dock-room status glow is self-powered and switched by SYSTEM only (Emitter EM-3), so it belongs to everything the player cannot switch off whenever the pod is berthed (always, except mid-leg). `g_dock = floor(output_pm_D × reach_px_D / 1000)` is its Emitter term; the Emitter and Derelict Authoring own the knobs and have published no value yet, so `g_dock` is `[PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]` and the shipped rows above are evaluated at `g_dock` 0 until it is published, when the lint adds it. The constraint it must satisfy is that F-15 still holds at every shipped row with the two wake glows: at most 99 runs. Integer model (entry at `L_CEIL`, `r_dark` = 366 + `g_dock`): `g_dock` 0 gives 93 / 96 / 98 runs at rings 1 / 2 / 3; 6 to 24 gives 93 to 94 / 96 to 97 / 99; 30 gives 94 / 97 / 100, so the ring-3 test row fails at 30 (each +1 mAU per tick adds 1.8 AU to `x_dark`, and the ring-3 slack was 6 ticks). The budget handed to Emitter and Authoring is therefore `g_dock ≤ DOCK_GLOW_TERM_MAX` = 24 mAU per tick (for example output 300 × reach 80); at 24 the rows are 94 / 97 / 99 runs (slack 30 / 12 / 0 ticks), the `x_dark_home_lit` ring-1 row is 116 runs against a limit of 119 (slack 18 ticks), and `walk_beam_pm` rises by about 8 permille (F-24, inside its margin). The glow also lifts the lit-idle steady state by `g_dock × 1800` mAU (43 AU at 24), which is why the home-idle figures of F-25 are quoted without it. It is never a tell carrier (AT-20).

#### F-17. Breach end

The breach_end formula is defined as:

`end_BREACH = (D ≥ BREACH_MAX_TICKS) OR (D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS AND clear_runs × 6 ≥ CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS) OR pod_departure_nonhome_locus OR (empty_nonhome_locus AND D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS)`, with `D = T − breach_enter_T` and `clear_runs' = (D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS AND L' < theta_HOLD) ? clear_runs + 1 : 0`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 900 (300 to 1800) | data | the shortest breach |
| `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 3600 (1800 to 3899, bound by F-20) | data | the death-spiral guard |
| `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 300 (60 to 900, a multiple of 6) | data | consecutive time under the hold line after the minimum |
| `pod_departure_nonhome_locus` | bool | | true when the locus body is not the home-ship, `locus_occupants_at_entry` is non-empty, no living seat's `actor_section` is in `locus_body`, and every seat of `locus_occupants_at_entry` has `occ_gone` set (the F-14 seat rule: a recorded seat that dies inside or cannot be resolved stays present, a seat in the pod is gone) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the last living occupant has left; a player who was never inside does not trigger it) *(provisional on Web Map's notification; derivable now from Body)* | the pod-hop clause (AT-22) |
| `empty_nonhome_locus` | bool | evaluated **at BREACH entry only** | `locus_occupants_at_entry` is empty AND the locus body is not the home-ship | the X10 clause (CQ-9); at MVP reachable only for a berthed derelict that stays loaded after the crew left it (WM-21, WM-24); a departure during the TELL never reaches this formula, it voids the breach earlier (F-14, AT-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** a breach lasts at most 3,600 ticks; a pod departure from a non-home locus body can end it at any run, and the empty non-home body clause ends it at 900; the earliest end by quiet is `BREACH_MIN_TICKS + CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS − 6` = **1,194** ticks (the first counted run is the one at `D` = 900). **Example:** entry at `T` 20,004; the player flees to the pod and the level drops under `theta_HOLD` at `T` 20,400; counting starts at the run `T` 20,904 (`D` = 900) and the 50th consecutive run is `T` 21,198, which ends the breach (`D` = 1,194).

#### F-18. STRESS schedule

The stress_schedule formula is defined as:

`deposit at run q (q = runs since BREACH entry) iff phase == BREACH AND ( q < (S + STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN − 1) div STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN OR ( q ≥ STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS AND (q mod STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS) < STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS ) )`; each deposit run is M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, s, magnitude = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT)` for the next at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` sections of `locus_body` in ascending `section_id`, from `stress_pending_cursor`, wrapping

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S` | int | the number of sections of `locus_body` | recorded at TELL entry | the sections to cover |
| `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` | int | 1 to 16; placeholder 8 | data | per-run deposit count (Horror's budget is at most `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`, C22) |
| `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS` | int runs | 100 to (`STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` − burst − margin); placeholder 300 | data | the re-arm period |
| `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` | int runs | 1 to 6; placeholder 3 | data | consecutive runs of one burst (tolerates a dropped deposit; M17 is idempotent) |
| `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` | int steps | cited from the registry (`STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, 600 steps; Graph LSG:526, :907) | the Graph | how long one deposit keeps STRESS armed |
| `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` | permille | cited from the registry (2000) | the Graph | the magnitude, passed by name |

**Output Range:** the coverage margin `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS − STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS − STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` (297 steps = 29.7 s at the cited value) is positive by lint, and a second lint requires `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS × STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN ≥ S_max` so one burst re-arms the whole body (24 ≥ 14, the MVP's largest body: home ship 5, derelict A 4 and derelict B 5 compartments). STRESS lapses `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS × 6` ticks after the last honoured deposit, so the **tail** past SUBSIDING's end is `2,400 − (BREACH end − last deposit)` ticks, 606 to 2,400. No deposit is made on or after the run that ends BREACH. **Example:** `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 3,600 (600 runs), a locus body of 5 sections: all five are covered at `q` = 0; a burst at `q` = 300, 301 and 302; the breach ends at `q` = 600; the last deposit (`q` 302) is 1,788 ticks before the end, so STRESS lapses 3,600 ticks after it, which is 612 ticks after SUBSIDING ends. For a breach that ends early at 1,194 ticks the tail is 1,206 ticks.

#### F-19. Subside reset

The subside_reset formula is defined as:

`L' = min(L, B + (POST_PM × (theta − B)) div 1000)` at SUBSIDING entry

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `POST_PM` | int permille | 200 to 600; placeholder 400 | data | the residual fraction of the span kept after a breach |
| `SUBSIDE_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 to 2400; placeholder 1200 | data | the valley length |

**Output Range:** `B` to `theta`; the level is cut to a residual, never wiped. **Example:** `B` 300,000: `300,000 + (400 × 5,700,000) div 1000 = 2,580,000` (2,580 AU). A player who keeps running with the beam on from that residual re-crosses `theta` 1,782 ticks after the reset, which is 582 ticks into the 1,800-tick refractory (the refractory starts when SUBSIDING's 1,200 ticks end), so it is the refractory, not the level, that holds the next tell off.

#### F-20. Event-defer invariant (registry: `event_defer_invariant`)

The event_defer_invariant formula is defined as:

`TELL_MAX_TICKS + BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS < AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, `SUBSIDE_TICKS`, `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800, 1200, 3600 | this GDD | the three phase caps |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 300) | Save/Load | one retry of the soft gate |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 7200) | Save/Load | the deferral cap past which a save is RISKY |

**Output Range:** boolean. **Example:** `1800 + 3600 + 1200 + 300 = 6900 < 7200` (margin 300 ticks, one retry). The inequality binds `BREACH_MAX_TICKS < 3,900` at the other placeholders; at 3,900, one above the range top, it fails (AC-AT-51's negative fixture). Re-check across every `gate_tell_mode` (FAIR and STRICT) whenever any term changes.

#### F-21. Rearm and refractory against the retry

The rearm_refract formula is defined as:

`TELL_REARM_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` and `REFRACT_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_REARM_TICKS` | int ticks | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` to 1800; placeholder 600 | data | the gap after an abort |
| `REFRACT_TICKS` | int ticks | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` to 3600; placeholder 1800 | data | the gap after a breach |

**Output Range:** boolean. An open soft-gate window exists between chained aborted tells and between chained events, even in STRICT mode. **Example:** `600 ≥ 300` and `1800 ≥ 300`: hold.

#### F-22. Noise unit relations (registry: `noise_unit_relations`)

The noise_unit_relations formula is defined as:

IR-1 `2 ≤ U_TOOL ≤ 6`; IR-2 `U_LOUD ≥ 30 × U_TOOL`; IR-3 `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U_TOOL` | int AU | 2 to 6; placeholder 4 | this GDD | one accrued tool-channel tick |
| `U_LOUD` | int AU | `30 × U_TOOL` to `ATT_THRESHOLD` ÷ 10 (a range lint, not a fourth relation) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; placeholder 180 (= 30 × 6) | this GDD | one-shot at cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply, throw landing |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 600) | Interaction | how long a partial job is kept |

**Output Range:** boolean ×3, handed over by Interaction (IA-33). 180 holds IR-2 across the **whole** legal `U_TOOL` range (at `U_TOOL` 6, `30 × 6 = 180`), not only at the default. **Example:** `2 ≤ 4 ≤ 6`; `180 ≥ 120`; `600 ≤ 3600` (a job split across the TTL retains `e^(−600/1800)` = 72 percent).

#### F-23. The tell-window contract (registry: `tell_window_contract`)

The tell_window_contract formula is defined as:

for every recorded harm event `e` at tick `t_e` in section `s_e`: `EXISTS epoch k: tell_start_k + tell_len_k ≤ t_e < subside_end_k AND tell_len_k ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS AND s_e in locus_body_k`; companion floor: for every epoch, at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats are published in `[tell_start_k, tell_start_k + TELL_MIN_TICKS)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e` | harm event | web-originated M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE, STRESS; environmental M18 `apply_damage(…, EXOGENOUS)`; **provisionally** a lethal act by a horror on a crew body | the `TellAudit` ledger (DIAGNOSTIC, a test fixture first) | what is audited |
| `t_e` | tick | | `T` of the call | when it happened |
| `tell_start_k`, `tell_len_k`, `subside_end_k` | ticks | | the epoch record | the epoch's window |
| `locus_body_k` | set of section ids | | the epoch's locus body in force at `t_e`: recorded at TELL entry and replaced only by an AT-14 fallback, which TellAudit records as `LOCUS_FALLBACK(epoch, old_body, new_body, tick)` | the sections the breach may harm |
| `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` | int | 3 to 4; placeholder 4 (5 fails the frozen-clock bound) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* | data | the companion floor |

**Output Range:** boolean over a replay; **0 violations** required. Player-originated acts (cut, vent) are not events. The Graph's endogenous gate is a different rule. **Example:** a STRESS deposit at `T` 8,244 against epoch 12 (`tell_start` 7,200, `tell_len` 1,044, BREACH from 8,244 for 3,600 ticks, so `subside_end` = 8,244 + 3,600 + 1,200 = 13,044): `7,200 + 1,044 = 8,244 ≤ 8,244 < 13,044`, `1,044 ≥ 600`, section in the body: passes. A stub that deposits at `T` 8,238 fails (one run early); a burst deposit at 13,044 fails (not `< subside_end`).

#### F-24. Pre-tell cue step and the cue-floor calibration lint

The ambient_cue_step formula is defined as:

**Phase guard** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `cue_step = 0` and no pre-tell cue event is requested while `phase ≠ CALM`; on the first CALM run after a TELL, BREACH or SUBSIDING the step re-arms from 0 (the hysteresis memory `rise_edge` is cleared, so it climbs again only by crossing a rise edge from below, never by replaying the pre-tell step). In CALM: `cue_step = 0` if `level_pm < AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`, else `min(CUE_STEPS, 1 + ((level_pm − AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM) × CUE_STEPS) div (1000 − AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM))`; a step falls only when `level_pm < rise_edge − CUE_HYST_PM`; lint: `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM ≥ max over the MVP-active rings of walk_beam_pm(ring) + CUE_MARGIN_PM`, with `walk_beam_pm(ring) = (1000 × r_wb × tau) div (theta − B_ring)` and `r_wb = U[WALK] × 1000 + W_L[BEAM] × beam_term div 1000 + r_home_lit_idle + r_glow` (the home ship lit and idle, F-25, and the two wake glows) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; second lint: `(1000 − AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM) div CUE_STEPS ≥ CUE_RUNG_MIN_PM` (50 permille per rung)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` | int permille | 750 to 800 (the calibration lint sets the lower edge, 671 + `CUE_MARGIN_PM` rounded up to 50; the rung lint caps it at `1000 − CUE_STEPS × CUE_RUNG_MIN_PM`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; **derived default 800** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | data; Tuning Knobs | below it no cue plays |
| `CUE_STEPS`, `CUE_HYST_PM`, `CUE_MARGIN_PM` | int | 4, 25, 100 (safe 3 to 4, 10 to 50, 50 to 129: the rung lint binds the first and third at the floor 800) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | data | the quantisation, the hysteresis and the calibration margin |
| `r_wb` | int mAU per tick | 2126 | derived: walking 1 AU (cited `NOISE_PER_TICK` walk) = 1000 mAU plus the wide beam's EM term 288 (EM D.12 example, wide beam (1000, 288)) at weight 1000 plus the lit idle home ship 766 (F-25) plus the two wake glows 72 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the walk-with-beam inflow in the ordinary exploration state |
| `walk_beam_pm(ring)` | int permille | 671 / 689 / 702 at rings 1 / 2 / 3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | derived | the steady `level_pm` of a player walking with the beam on, home lit |
| `CUE_RUNG_MIN_PM` | int permille | 50 (safe 30 to 100) | data | the narrowest allowed rung, so the ladder keeps four distinguishable steps |

**Output Range:** `cue_step` 0 to 4, no value between steps; at floor 800 the rise edges are 800, 850, 900 and 950 (and the falls 775, 825, 875 and 925) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **CP-C2 arithmetic (integers):** the walk-with-beam steady level with the home ship lit and the two wake glows is `B + 2,126 × 1,800` = `300,000 + 3,826,800` = **4,126,800 mAU (4,127 AU)** at ring 1, so `walk_beam_pm` = `(1000 × 3,826,800) div 5,700,000` = **671**; ring 2: `div 5,550,000` = **689**; ring 3 (`B` 550,000): `div 5,450,000` = **702**. The floor is the MVP-active ring's `671 + CUE_MARGIN_PM 100 = 771`, rounded up to the next multiple of 50 = **800**; the ring-2 row gives 789 (800 passes) and the ring-3 test row gives 802 (850), which leaves rungs of 37 permille and fails the rung lint, so ring 3 is flagged `SWITCHES` and its cue floor is a Depth Rings decision. Superseded values: the seed 350 (failed at 406 permille) and 550 (it passed the walk-and-beam trace of the old scope, 406 to 425, but sat below 648 to 671, the level of a player who merely walks with the beam while the home ship burns its lights, so the lowest rung carried no information). **Example:** `level_pm` 473 gives step 0; 810 gives step 1 (810 − 800 = 10; 10 × 4 div 200 = 0; 1 + 0 = 1); 900 gives step 3 (100 × 4 div 200 = 2; 1 + 2 = 3); 1000 clamps to step 4. *Lint scope, stated so it is a decision:* the trace is the ordinary exploration state, the player's own spend (walk plus beam) on top of the lit, idle home ship and the unswitchable wake glows. Pre-tell cues therefore start only when `L` has climbed to `B` plus 80 percent of the span (4,860 AU at ring 1, above `theta_HOLD`): a smaller early-warning window than 550 gave, and the price of making the lowest rung carry information. Audio's creak densities per step (AA-22) are unchanged; only the edges move (cross-GDD note). The DOCK_STATUS glow (at most 24 mAU per tick, F-16) and the conditional `BEAM_SPILL` row (14, AT-4) add under 8 and about 4 permille to `walk_beam_pm`; together they move 671 to about 683, so the floor 800 stands (783 rounds up to 800) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

#### F-25. Design aids (not rules): steady states, crossing times, the felt-target table

The steady_state_aids formula is defined as:

`L_ss = B + r × tau`; crossing time `t = tau × ln((L_ss − L0) / (L_ss − theta))`; decay-to-line time `t = tau × ln((L0 − x_dark) / (theta_HOLD − x_dark))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r` | mAU per tick | 0 to about 6,000 | the sum of the stimulus rates | a constant inflow |
| `L0` | mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | the start level | where the trace begins |
| `x_dark` | mAU | F-16 | F-16 | the decay target when every input stops |

**Output Range:** design aids for the calibration harness, never read by the sim (closed-form oracles; the integer model agrees within 3 percent, the game-designer's GD-F1 tolerance). **Example (scenario table at the placeholders, `B` 300 AU, integer-model checked):**

| Situation | `r` (mAU/tick) | `L_ss` (AU) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Home ship lit and idle (three fixtures, the EMERGENCY lamp, 1,900 W) | 576 + 190 = 766 | 1,679 | `level_pm` 241 |
| Dark (survival load and the EMERGENCY lamp) | 150 + 144 = 294 | 829 | the F-16 floor |
| Walking with the beam in a derelict, home ship lit | 766 + 1000 + 288 = 2,054 | 3,997 | `level_pm` 648; under `theta_HOLD` |
| Running with the beam, home ship lit | 766 + 4000 + 288 = 5,054 | 9,397 | crosses `theta` from idle in 1,476 ticks (24.6 s) |
| Tool channel with the beam (a cut) | 5,054 | 9,397 | same as running; the lit-only rule (IA-6) means a tool always carries light |
| Breaker cold start (`INRUSH_PM` 1500) | 54,000 mAU per run for five runs | +270 AU | the hiding verb has a price; this is a **lamps-off** cold start of the survival branch, and a cold start that also lights lamps adds the lamps through F-3 only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Moment A (enter at `theta`, stop everything) | 294 | | leaves `theta_HOLD` in 480 ticks; abort at 594 (F-15b) |
| Holding a salvage carry, walking with the beam, home lit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | 1,000 + 7,000 + 288 + 766 = 9,054 | none: the decay is suspended while held, so the level rises to `L_CEIL` | with the cut done first the reference tell opens 318 ticks into the carry (F-26) |
| Holding a salvage carry, standing still, beam on, home lit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | 7,000 + 1,054 = 8,054 | none (decay suspended; rises to `L_CEIL`) | the freeze is priced: crosses in 360 ticks after the cut and in 540 ticks from the lit-idle level (F-26) |
| Bare salvage haul, no cut, from the lit-idle level *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | 9,054 | none (decay suspended) | crosses in 480 ticks: any haul longer than 10 tiles tells (a 13-tile carry, 624 ticks, crosses 144 ticks before it ends) |

**Per-verb "seconds saved" (the restraint verbs do not weigh the same).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Ring 1, home ship lit, no wake glows, integer model: the time for the level to fall to the lint's line `theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN` = 4,750 AU, entered at `theta` (6,000 AU) / at `L_CEIL` (6,120 AU).

| Posture after the stop | `r` (mAU/tick) | Ticks to 4,750 AU from `theta` / `L_CEIL` | Saved against the row above |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keep working (tool channel, beam, home lit) | 5,054 | never (settles at 9,397 AU) | n/a |
| **Put the tool down**, keep walking with the beam | 2,054 | 1,764 / 1,866 | the difference between ever aborting and never |
| Hold the cargo, stand still, beam on *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 8,054 | never (the decay is suspended while salvage is held, so the level only rises, to `L_CEIL`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)* | the cargo is the lever |
| **Stand still** (cargo set down), beam on | 1,054 | 720 / 774 | **1,044 / 1,092 ticks (17.4 / 18.2 s)** |
| ...and **kill the chest lamp** | 766 | 618 / 666 | 102 / 108 ticks (1.7 / 1.8 s) |
| ...and **open the breaker** so the home fixtures go dark (survival load and the EMERGENCY lamp remain) | 294 | 498 / 540 | 120 / 126 ticks (2.0 / 2.1 s); the later re-close costs a 270 AU inrush (F-4), about 125 ticks of decay near the line, which gives back what it saved |

The two wake glows add 18 to 36 ticks to the lower three rows. Reading: **stopping the tool, setting any cargo down and standing still are the lever (about 17 seconds and the difference between aborting and not); darkness is worth about 2 seconds and power-down about 2 seconds, and the breaker's inrush gives that back.** The felt-target weights are deliberately not retuned to restore literal parity between the verbs before the AC-AT-57 harness exists.

**Abort share by reaction latency** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (design aid; ring 1, clean ship, home ship lit unless the breaker is open, entry at `theta`, the tool and beam running at 5,054 mAU per tick until the stop, integer model; the share is permille of the 901 draws `TELL_DRAW_LO` to `TELL_DRAW_HI` that abort):

| Posture after the stop | stop at 180 ticks | 300 | 600 | 900 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stand still, beam on | 986 | 852 | 519 | 186 |
| ...and kill the lamp | 1000 | 966 | 633 | 300 |
| ...and open the breaker (all dark) | 1000 | 1000 | 766 | 433 |

Reading: the abortability proof (F-15b) holds only for a prompt reaction; at 300 ticks a lamp-on stop already loses 15 percent of tells, and by 600 ticks the posture is worth as much as the reaction. This is why `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` is capped at 204 (Tuning Knobs) and why OQ-AT-26 goes back to the user together with the live-choice line (OQ-AT-36).

*Felt-target rationale (CQ-6, CQ-7).* The game-designer derived the weights from felt AU-per-tick targets: walk 1.0 and run 4.0 (cited), tool 4.0, chest beam about 0.58, a live glowstick about 0.29 (four live: 1.15, more than a walk), a powered fixture about 0.10 (five at rest add 0.5), surplus survival draw about 0.15 per kilowatt, one breaker close 150 to 300 AU, the dark-still floor about 0.30; this document keeps the single dimension above (beam 0.29, glowstick 0.10, fixture 0.14, surplus 0.15 per 1,500 W) because it reproduces R4's calibration and has no per-kind fudge, and the felt-target column is the **playtest alternative** (weights about 2000 for `BEAM` and 3000 for `DEPLOYABLE`, 700 for fixtures). *Alternative placeholder set (CQ-6):* the game-designer's K1 (`ATT_THRESHOLD` 7200 AU, `U_LOUD` 600) lengthens the reckless crossing from 24.6 s to 37.7 s (integer F-5 run from the home-idle level, 2,262 ticks) and leaves the AC-AT-57f chain crossing at about 50 s (the same fifth job end), at the cost of a larger `theta − B` span; both sets satisfy IR-2. *Occupancy:* for a player who never goes below the hold line, `calm_fraction = REFRACT_TICKS / (tell_mean + BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS + REFRACT_TICKS)` = 1,800 / (1,350 + 3,600 + 1,200 + 1,800) = 22.6 percent, inside the 15 to 35 percent calm-time target (and above AC-AT-80's 20 percent floor) for riding every tell; the levers are `REFRACT_TICKS` 3600 (36.9 percent) and `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 2700 (25.5 percent), a playtest trigger rather than a rule.

#### F-26. Carry burden intake and the carry-decision lints (registry: `carry_burden_intake`, `carry_decision`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*(User ruling X3, 2026-10-27: Boarding & Salvage OQ-BS-27 option B, Attention OQ-AT-27 lever (a), the calibration route; also D49 and D50.)*

The carry_burden_intake formula is defined as:

`I_burden(tick) = U_BURDEN × 1000 if a salvage carry is held AND the holder's body is not the home body AND loco_state ≠ IN_POD, else 0` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the location gate of AT-3)*, pushed by the Controller once per tick as class `BURDEN`; a run's burden is the sum over its six ticks (42,000 mAU for six ticks of holding at `U_BURDEN` 7), entering `pending_noise_mAU` and the holder's `pending_ledger_mAU` through F-1; the only new state is the flag `pending_burden`, which suspends that run's decay (F-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5: salvage scope and the decay suspension)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U_BURDEN` | int AU per tick | 7 to 10; placeholder 7 `[PLACEHOLDER — re-derive at the AC-AT-57h trace and Boarding's REF_GOLDEN]` | this GDD (derived below) | the intake of one tick of holding cargo; 7 AU is 7,000 mAU, 1.75 running ticks |
| `carry_ref` | item ref or none | | Controller (`controller.state`; PC-1 b, IDB-2) | the single carried item; the push is keyed on the carry state and never on `mass_g` (IDB-21), standing or moving, until `drop`, an install or a throw clears it; it is pushed only while the carry is a **salvage carry** (the instance was picked up inside a non-home locus; never at home, never the player's own kit) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*, and only while the holder is aboard a non-home body and not `IN_POD` (AT-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `Lc` | int ticks | 1,008 to 1,344 (21 to 28 tiles) | Derelict Authoring data (DA-28 allows 28 tiles); `V_CARRY` 1 px per tick, 48 px per tile (Boarding D.3: 13 tiles = 624 ticks) | the decision-bearing carry of the reference derelict |
| `L0` | int mAU | 1,678,501 | F-25 lit-idle steady state (Boarding D.2) | the start level of the lit golden schedule |
| `t_open`, `rem` | int ticks | | derived | the carry tick at which F-5 first reaches `theta`, and `rem = Lc − t_open`, the carry still ahead when the tell opens |

**Output Range:** 42,000 mAU per run while holding (7 AU per tick), on top of the CARRY walk push of 6,000 per run when moving; the decay is suspended while the carry is held, so the level only rises and saturates at `L_CEIL` within the reference carry *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. A run with no drained push has `pending_burden` clear, so the decay is not suspended in the pod or aboard the home body *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

**Why a new term and not new weights (the D50 recalibration, result).** The ruling asked for the weights and theta to be recalibrated so that an ordinary sortie reaches theta; that was run first, with the existing levers, and it does not close. Boarding's own finding (D.2) is that the lit golden sortie peaks at 57 percent of theta and that no retune of `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD` or `TAU_ATT_TICKS` reaches it; getting there with `W_L`, `W_P` or `B_table` needs about 3.4 times today's intake, and the dark-floor lint forbids it: 3.4 times the unswitchable draw and EMERGENCY lamp term moves `r_dark` from 294 to about 1,000 mAU per tick, so `x_dark` = 300 + 1,000 × 1.8 = 2,100 AU against the F-15 cap of 1,253 AU; lowering `ATT_THRESHOLD` is bounded below at 5,816 AU (Tuning Knobs). A crossing that belongs to the take's own act therefore needs an intake that exists only while hauling. **Result of the recalibration: `W_L`, `W_P`, `W_S`, `B_table`, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` and `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6,000 are retained unchanged** (the lit sortie crosses at the current theta once the burden term exists), and the F-15, F-15b, F-16 and F-24 lints keep their inputs.

**Derivation of `U_BURDEN`.** Integer F-5 over Boarding's lit golden schedule (walk-in 312 ticks at 1 AU, door attempts 60 idle, the cut 240 ticks at `U_TOOL` with `U_LOUD` on its last tick, the pickup 12 idle, then the carry at the CARRY push plus the burden, then 30 idle ticks holding at the console), beam 288 and lit home 766 mAU per tick, from `L0`. The same model with no burden reproduces Boarding's golden peak (3,394,795 mAU, 3,374,965 after the console) exactly, which is the check that it is the same recursion. **Attention's walk-in convention (312 ticks, fixed) and its run accounting are canonical; Boarding quotes them** (R5). The decay is suspended on every carry run (F-5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5: one definition of each constraint, owned by Boarding & Salvage, stated with Attention's integrator; Attention's earlier K list and its private live-choice test `rem > TELL_DRAW_LO` are withdrawn.)* The constraints are **Boarding's K1 to K5** (Boarding D.2, AC-BS-123); Attention asserts only the crossing and reports the rest:

- **K1 crossing (asserted by IR-4).** The tell opens after the pickup and before the console.
- **K2 causality (reported).** The cut and the carry supply at least 50 percent of the headroom `theta − L0` at the tick the tell opens.
- **K3 live choice (Boarding's definition and pass line; reported here in permille).** At the opening the carry still ahead, `rem = Lc − t_open`, must be at least `TELL_DRAW_LO + F_LIVE × (TELL_DRAW_HI − TELL_DRAW_LO)`, which is 1,125 ticks at Boarding's provisional `F_LIVE` 0.25 (250 permille). Attention quotes the **live share** `live_pm = max(0, min(1000, (rem − TELL_DRAW_LO) × 1000 div (TELL_DRAW_HI − TELL_DRAW_LO)))`, the share of the tell draw for which walking on loses to the breach.
- **K4 restraint without the carry (reported).** The variants in which no salvage carry runs (cut and leave; listen and leave) stay at or below 0.95 `theta_HOLD`; the suspension never applies to them.
- **K5 priced freeze (reported).** With the cargo in hand the level never falls, so freezing after the cut is never free.
- **Abortable (Attention's).** The burden is zeroed by `drop`, so F-15 and F-15b keep their inputs (the carry paragraph of F-15b).

Results for the 28-tile carry (`Lc` 1,344, carry starts at schedule tick 624; mAU per tick of burden; decay suspended while held) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*:

| Burden | Tell opens (carry tick) | `rem` | live share (permille) | Boarding K3 (`rem` ≥ 1,125) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3,000 | 570 | 774 | 0 | no |
| 5,000 | 408 | 936 | 40 | no |
| 6,000 | 360 | 984 | 93 | no |
| **7,000 (`U_BURDEN` 7)** | **318** | **1,026** | **140** | **no** |
| 8,000 | 288 | 1,056 | 173 | no |
| 10,000 (top of the boot range) | 240 | 1,104 | 226 | no |
| 11,500 (outside the range) | 216 | 1,128 | 253 | yes |

With the decay suspended any burden of about 250 mAU per tick or more crosses inside the 28-tile carry (250 opens at carry tick 1,242; 0 never does), so K1 is easy. **No burden inside the boot range 7 to 10 meets Boarding's K3 at the 28-tile carry**; it needs about 11,500. `U_BURDEN` therefore stays **7** `[PLACEHOLDER]` (the ruling's 'about 7,000'), and Attention does not raise it, or any weight, to force K3 green (R5): the honest live share at 7 is 140 permille against Boarding's 250, and the pass line moves to the boarding spike (AC-BS-123(d) re-written as a spike gate, the outcome R5 names as expected). K5: with the cut done and the cargo held, standing still crosses 360 ticks after the pickup, and from the lit-idle level with no cut at all 540 ticks. K2: the tell opens at schedule tick 942; with the cut and carry pushes replaced by idle the level there would be 2,092,108, so the cut and the carry supply (6,000,000 − 2,092,108) / (6,000,000 − 1,678,501) = 90 percent of the headroom. The peak is `L_CEIL` (6,120,000, 1.02 `theta`), which is as high as any run can go.

**Worked example (`U_BURDEN` 7, `Lc` 1,344).** At the start of the carry `L` = 3,144,759 mAU (after the walk-in, the cut with its `LOUD` push and the pickup). One carry run adds `6 × (1,000 + 7,000 + 288 + 766)` = 54,324 mAU and, with the decay suspended, loses nothing, so `L'` = 3,199,083; the level reaches `theta` on the 53rd run (6,023,931 mAU), 318 ticks in, with `rem` 1,026 ticks of carry still ahead *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*. A tell opened there lasts 900 to 1,800 ticks: for the 126 draws of 900 to 1,025 walking on arrives after the breach (140 permille of the draw); for every longer draw it arrives first, so the choice is live but thin, below Boarding's 250-permille line, which is why the unit carries a `[PLACEHOLDER]` tag and why the length of the carry matters more than the unit.

**What the burden does to the restraint variants.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5: decay suspended while held.)* With the beam off the tell opens 348 ticks into the carry, with the home lamps dark (start level 829,000) 432, with both 468; `rem` is then 996, 912 and 876 (live shares 106, 13 and 0 permille), so none meets Boarding's K3 and none avoids the tell. Light restraint does not save a hauler; setting the cargo down, or not cutting and hauling in the first place, does (a bare haul from the lit-idle level crosses in 480 ticks, so any salvage haul longer than 10 tiles from that level tells). Boarding's acceptance shape must be re-derived accordingly (notes for the Boarding owner). **A berth that costs `U_LOUD`** (OQ-AT-23, default applied) moves the opening to 306 ticks into the carry (`rem` 1,038, live share 153 permille).

**The reference carry is the lever Attention does not own.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5.)* At `U_BURDEN` 7 the tell opens 318 ticks into any carry of the ruled band, so Boarding's K3 needs `Lc` ≥ 1,125 + 318 = 1,443 ticks (30.1 tiles), beyond the ruled 21-to-28 tiles (Derelict Authoring's route, X3), and any live share at all needs `Lc` ≥ `TELL_DRAW_LO` + 318 + 1 = 1,219 ticks (25.4 tiles). A 21-tile carry (1,008 ticks) leaves `rem` 690, fits every tell and offers no live choice; making it live at all would need the tell to open within 107 ticks of the pickup, a burden above 20,000 mAU per tick, which nobody should want. Whether the choice is live is therefore Derelict Authoring's pick of the reference route, not Attention's unit.

**Lints.** `carry_decision` (IR-4, a CI data-lint over the reference derelict's `Lc` and `assets/data/attention/intake.json`): *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5.)* K1 holds on the lit golden schedule (the tell opens inside the carry), else `ATT_CARRY_NO_CROSSING` (ERROR); the live share is computed and reported against Boarding's K3 line as `ATT_CARRY_NOT_LIVE` (WARN, boot-advisory: the pass line belongs to the boarding spike, AC-BS-123(d), so IR-4, AC-AT-132 and Boarding's `REF_GOLDEN` assertion (d) are **not story-ready** as pass/fail gates until that spike sets it); the burden range 7 to 10 is guidance and an advisory CI diff (`U_BURDEN` 6 and 11 are reported, and boot does not refuse: no schema bound or named invariant binds the edge, ADR-0007 s2.5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix, round 4)*. At 10 a bare salvage haul from the lit-idle level tells within 360 ticks (7.5 tiles), which is the 'too high' edge. Registry: `U_BURDEN`, the class list of `noise_push` and the two formulas are requested (Cross-References).

## Edge Cases

*Each entry reads "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]" and names the diagnostic or reason code where one exists. Entries are grouped by theme; `E-n` ids are stable references for the Acceptance Criteria. Rule ids are `AT-n`, formulas `F-n`. Provisional entries say which sibling or undesigned system they depend on.*

#### Intake, level and bounds

- **E-1. If `push_stimulus` is called with an unknown `class` or a `kind` other than `NOISE`**: it adds nothing, increments the unknown-class counter and returns; the call never rejects or asserts (Class B: clamp, never reject; AT-3). Reserved kinds are rejected by lint at authoring, not at run time.
- **E-2. If a `QUIET` push arrives**: nothing is added to `pending_noise_mAU` or any `pending_ledger_mAU` entry *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the field is `pending_noise_mAU`, F-1)* and the `QUIET` counter increments. The class exists so Interaction can declare a silent verb explicitly (IA-33).
- **E-3. If a push's source resolves to a dormant or unknown section** (an object in a cut-away section, a foot origin in no section): the push still counts toward `I_noise` and so toward `L` (it is added to `pending_noise_mAU` only), nothing is written to `pending_ledger_mAU` (so no ledger key ever exists for an unloaded section), and `dropped_unattributed` increments (F-2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The web hears a sound it cannot place; the level is the world's and the locus is only a choice.
- **E-4. If many pushes stack in one run** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (six seats each pushing `LOUD` reach 1,080,000 mAU, 18 percent of `L_CEIL`, and saturate nothing; only a synthetic storm of 35 or more `LOUD` pushes at the placeholder `U_LOUD` does): `pending_noise_mAU` and each `pending_ledger_mAU[s]` saturate at `L_CEIL`, `I_run` is bounded by `L_CEIL` plus the F-3 and F-4 maxima, and `L'` clamps at `L_CEIL`; all arithmetic stays far below 2^63 (F-1, F-5). The saturation changes nothing below the ceiling.
- **E-5. If `power_use` is absent for a loaded section**: `I_power[s]` is 0, the diagnostic `ATT_POWER_ABSENT` is logged once per process, and `served_pm` is **not** substituted (it already enters as light). Nothing else changes (F-4).
- **E-6. If `emitters()` is empty, every section is dormant or no section is loaded**: `I_light` and `I_power` are 0, no ledger exists, `L` decays to `B` exactly (F-5), and a TELL cannot start (lint: `B < theta` at every ring).
- **E-7. If 96 emitters (`MAX_EMITTERS`) are live**: `I_light` is at most 221,184 mAU per run at weight 1000 and 884,736 at the weight cap (F-3), and `L` pins at `L_CEIL`; a TELL then starts at the first legal run. The MVP slice (five home compartments) cannot reach this; a Vertical Slice growth that does (about 29 powered fixtures reach the hold line at rest at the game-designer's K1 values) is a tripwire for the lint `survivable_dark_floor`, which fails at authoring, and for the open question on per-section lamp switching (GD-15). "Lights you left on" must stay a decision and not become an inevitability.
- **E-8. If a ring change raises `B` above `L`** (descent): `L` is lifted to `B` in the same run (`dec` is 0 because `L − B` is negative), `level_pm` reads 0, and no TELL starts unless `L ≥ theta`. The held-ring rule (AT-6) applies while a seat is `IN_POD`.
- **E-9. If `B` falls** (a return to the home ring): `L` decays toward the new `B` at a rate proportional to the excess; there is no free reset, because the level is absolute and the baseline only a floor.
- **E-10. If `theta ≤ B` for any ring, or a `B_table` row breaks F-15 or F-16**: boot refuses (the data lint of AT-6, evaluated with `N_GLOW_DARK` = the glows authored active at wake, F-16). A division by zero in `level_pm` and a ring on which restraint cannot end a tell are both data errors caught before play.
- **E-11. If `L` equals `theta_HOLD` exactly**: it is not below the line (the comparison is strict `<`), so no abort counting happens and, on the due run, the breach commits. The boundary is deterministic and chosen to favour consequence over relief.

#### Simultaneous rules and timing boundaries

- **E-12. If a `LOUD` push lands in the run that crosses `theta`**: it is part of that run's `I_run`; the run enters TELL, draws `tell_len ≥ TELL_DRAW_LO`, and publishes beat 0 in the same run. The breach cannot occur before `tell_start_T + tell_len`; there is no instant-breach path (AT-8).
- **E-13. If two crossings occur in one run** (two `LOUD` pushes in co-op): one TELL, one `tell_epoch` increment and one RNG draw (the counter advances by exactly 1).
- **E-14. If `L ≥ theta` while `T < refract_until_T` or `T < rearm_until_T`**: the phase stays CALM, `L` keeps integrating up to `L_CEIL`, `level_pm` reads 1000, and the first run at or after the later timer enters TELL with beat 0 and `tell_elapsed` 0. No catch-up elapsed time is credited, so a long wait never shortens the tell.
- **E-15. If TELL entry falls exactly on `T == refract_until_T`** (or `rearm_until_T`): it is legal (the guard is `≥`).
- **E-16. If the abort counter reaches `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` on the same run as `T ≥ breach_due_T`**: the abort wins and is one transition (`tell_outcome = ABORTED`, no STRESS, no beacon). A run that is both "due" and "under the line" is an abort, never a breach.
- **E-17. If `L` drops below `theta_HOLD` and rises back before `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` elapses**: `below_hold_runs` resets to 0; only the at-due path remains. One dip does not abort.
- **E-18. If the clear-hold clause and `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` are both satisfied on one run**: one transition, BREACH to SUBSIDING; deposits stop on that run (F-18).
- **E-19. If `attention_arm_tell` is called outside CALM**: it is rejected with reason `NOT_CALM` and changes nothing. Inside CALM it enters TELL at the next run, clamps `tell_len` to `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]` (100 becomes 600), does not honour the two timers and does not consume `rng_counter` (F-9, F-10).
- **E-20. If the beam is strobed in step with the six-tick cadence**: light is sampled once per run, so the sampled state can be systematically off. The Controller adapter's toggle debounce (EM:720) owns the defence; Attention adds no cooldown and no sim-side rule (the EM text says the sim adds none). A person cannot phase-lock to a sample tick they cannot see; only a macro could, and a macro strobing the beam off at every sample is paying the lit-only verb price (IA-6) for nothing.

#### Tell, abort and the pod
